\int_{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}}_{t},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\geq\varrho_{t}}\|\widetilde{s}({\bm{x}}_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t})\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{x}}_{t}\lesssim\bigl(D+D^{2}\ell_{\rm off}\bigr)\sigma_{t}^{-2}n^{-A}.

Proof.

Let
\ell_{\rm off}\geq 1
be the logarithmic factor in the coordinatewise bound of the lemma.
Fix
\varrho_{t}=C_{\varrho}(A)\sigma_{t}\sqrt{D\vee\log n}
with
C_{\varrho}(A)
chosen large enough for the polynomial tail exponent required below.
By
Lemma

C.2
it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}}_{t},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\geq\varrho_{t}}\Big\|\frac{\nabla p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t})}{p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t})}\Big\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{x}}_{t}\lesssim\frac{D}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}n^{-A}.

By
Lemma

C.1
and the coordinatewise uniform assumption
\sup_{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\|\widetilde{s}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{D\ell_{\rm off}}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}}_{t},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\geq\varrho_{t}}\|\widetilde{s}({\bm{x}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq D\Bigl(\sup_{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\|\widetilde{s}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\Bigr)^{2}\cdot\Pr\bigl[\mathrm{dist}({\bm{X}}_{t},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\geq\varrho_{t}\bigr]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{-2}D^{2}\ell_{\rm off}n^{-A}.

Using the triangle inequality
\|a-b\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|a\|_{2}^{2}+2\|b\|_{2}^{2}
, we combine these bounds:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}}_{t},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\geq\varrho_{t}}\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t})-\widetilde{s}({\bm{x}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}}_{t},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\geq\varrho_{t}}\Big\|\frac{\nabla p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t})}{p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t})}\Big\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{x}}_{t}+\int_{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}}_{t},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\geq\varrho_{t}}\|\widetilde{s}({\bm{x}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{D}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}n^{-A}+\frac{D^{2}\ell_{\rm off}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}n^{-A}\lesssim\frac{D+D^{2}\ell_{\rm off}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}n^{-A}.

∎

Appendix D
Setup for Branchwise Score Approximation

To approximate the score near the forward-smeared manifold under a density lower bound, we resolve the geometry of the underlying data manifold
{\mathcal{M}}
and approximate the centered numerator ratio.
The setup section fixes the noise regimes, geometric charts, amplitudes, and chart-integral notation.
The large-noise component uses the tangent-cell construction in
Appendix

E
.
The small-noise component uses the unified projection–Laplace pipeline in

Appendix

F
; its internal subsections isolate active
geometry, finite-anchor projection networks, the Laplace expansion, and final
ratio-network assembly.
The learned-score section combines these two deterministic branches directly.
For readability, this appendix is best read in three passes. First read the definitions
of
{\mathcal{Q}},{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{U}}
in
Section

D.3.3
and the final branch
statements
Theorems

E.5
and
F.29
.
Second read the proof-architecture paragraphs at the starts of

Appendices

E
and
F
.
Finally consult the supporting lemmas for the particular denominator, projection, or
network-size estimate used in the main text.
For the VP forward process considered here,
{\bm{X}}_{t}=m_{t}{\bm{X}}_{0}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{Z}}
, the deterministic schedules satisfy
m_{t}\leq 1
and
\sigma_{t}\leq 1
.
On the truncated time interval
[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
we assume a positive lower bound for
m_{t}
, and we use the exact infimum of the noise schedule as the noise lower bound:

0<\underline{m}\leq m_{t}\leq\overline{m}\leq 1,\qquad\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}:=\inf_{t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}\sigma_{t},\qquad 0<\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}\leq\sigma_{t}\leq\overline{\sigma}\leq 1.

(11)

If the noise schedule is monotone increasing, this notation agrees with the endpoint value
\sigma({\underline{t}})
.
All schedule-approximation size bounds below are stated for the fixed compact time interval
[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
; logarithmic factors in
{\underline{t}}^{-1}
are absorbed into the constants unless explicitly displayed.

D.1
Noise regimes and branch-scale validity

Definition D.1

(Branch-scale small- and large-noise validity regions)
.

Fix constants
C_{\rm sm}\in(0,1]
and
c_{\rm lg}>0
satisfying

4c_{\rm lg}\leq C_{\rm sm}.

Their final numerical values are chosen after the uniform Laplace remainder constant is fixed; see
Equation

196
.
For every
0<\varepsilon<1
, define the small-noise validity region by

{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon):=\{t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]:\sigma_{t}\leq C_{\rm sm}\varepsilon^{1/\beta}\}.

(12)

and the large-noise validity region by

{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm lg}(\varepsilon):=\{t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]:\sigma_{t}\geq c_{\rm lg}\varepsilon^{1/\beta}\}.

(13)

These auxiliary validity regions need not be disjoint.
The parameter
\varepsilon
is a branch-scale parameter; target tolerances are named by role, such as coordinate accuracy
\varepsilon_{u}
, block accuracy
\varepsilon_{\rm blk}
, and scaled score accuracy
\varepsilon_{\rm sc}
.
In the final large-noise application, the branch scale is
n^{-\beta/(d+2\beta)}
, so the validity condition is
\sigma_{t}\gtrsim n^{-1/(d+2\beta)}
.

D.2
Branchwise approximation roadmap

There are two approximation mechanisms under
3
.
On large-noise slabs, the denominator
{\mathcal{Q}}
has an explicit lower bound on the near-manifold tube, so the tangent-cell moment approximation can be used directly and the network size is driven by the intrinsic cell count.
On the small-noise interval, the denominator is controlled after de-Gaussianization, and the proof uses a projection-centered Laplace expansion together with neural approximation of nearest-point projection maps.

For navigation, the formal dependency structure is:

Component

Formal role

Section

D.3

Build the projection atlas, partition of unity, chart integrals
{\mathcal{Q}},{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{U}}
, and the centered ratio
s^{*}=\sigma_{t}^{-1}{\mathcal{U}}/{\mathcal{Q}}
.

Appendix

E

Approximate
{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
on tangent cells at radius
r_{k}=c_{0}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}\wedge r_{\star,n}
under a direct tube lower bound for
{\mathcal{Q}}
, with
r_{\star,n}
defined in
Proposition

G.2
.

Theorem

E.5

Convert the large-noise chart-cell approximation and denominator floor into a global clipped score approximation on each large-noise slab.

Appendix

F

Develop the active projection geometry, Gauss–Newton projection networks, Laplace expansion, and final ratio-network assembly needed at small noise.

Lemmas

F.27
and
F.28

Remove the common normal Gaussian factor, prove a polynomial denominator floor, and approximate the de-Gaussianized chart integrals.

Corollary

F.29

Assemble the small-noise projection–Laplace networks into the final clipped score approximation on
[t_{0},t_{1}]
.

Appendices

G
and
H

Insert the deterministic comparison networks into the clipped ERM oracle and then into the slabwise
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
perturbation argument.

D.3
Projection atlas and chart-integral decomposition

D.3.1
Positive-reach projection charts

Let
{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{y}},r)
denote the open Euclidean ball with radius
r>0
and center
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
.
We will use projection charts whose validity follows from positive reach.

Lemma D.2

(Projection charts from positive reach)
.

Let
1
hold.
There is a numerical constant
c_{\rm ch}\in(0,1/8]
, depending only on
d
,
such that the chart radius may be chosen as

r_{\rm ch}:=c_{\rm ch}(1\wedge\kappa).

This radius has the following property.
For every
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{M}}
, if
{\bm{V}}_{{\bm{y}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D\times d}
has orthonormal columns spanning
T_{{\bm{y}}}{\mathcal{M}}
, then there is an open neighborhood
U_{{\bm{y}}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}
satisfying

{\mathcal{M}}\cap{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{y}},r_{\rm ch}/2)\subset U_{{\bm{y}}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}\cap{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{y}},r_{\rm ch}),

such that the tangent projection

\pi_{{\bm{y}}}:U_{{\bm{y}}}\to{\bm{V}}_{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}(U_{{\bm{y}}}-{\bm{y}})\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\qquad\pi_{{\bm{y}}}({\bm{z}}):={\bm{V}}_{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}({\bm{z}}-{\bm{y}}),

is a
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphism onto its image.
Moreover, for every
{\bm{z}}\in U_{{\bm{y}}}
and every
{\bm{v}}\in T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}
,

(1-2r_{\rm ch}/\kappa)\|{\bm{v}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|{\bm{V}}_{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|{\bm{v}}\|_{2}^{2}.

(14)

Proof.

We use the standard local-graph theorem for manifolds of positive reach.
For a closed embedded
C^{2}
manifold of reach at least
\kappa
, Federer
[
14
, Theorem 4.8(12)]
gives the tubular-neighborhood property for normal segments of length below
\kappa
, hence the usual tangent-ball exclusion, while
[
14
, Theorem 4.8(7)]
gives the quadratic distance-to-tangent-plane bound.
The tangent variation estimate recorded for instance in
[
3
, Corollary 3]
, together with the local inverse theorem, then gives a numerical
c_{\rm ch}\leq 1/8
, depending only on
d
, such that
{\mathcal{M}}\cap{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{y}},c_{\rm ch}\kappa)
is a
C^{\infty}
graph over
T_{{\bm{y}}}{\mathcal{M}}
.
If
\kappa\geq 1
, the smaller radius
c_{\rm ch}
is also admissible.
Thus
r_{\rm ch}=c_{\rm ch}(1\wedge\kappa)
is admissible uniformly in
{\bm{y}}
.
Equivalently, the orthogonal projection onto
T_{{\bm{y}}}{\mathcal{M}}
is one-to-one on this patch and is a
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphism onto its image.
Thus there is an open set
U_{{\bm{y}}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}
with

{\mathcal{M}}\cap{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{y}},r_{\rm ch}/2)\subset U_{{\bm{y}}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}\cap{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{y}},r_{\rm ch})

such that
\pi_{{\bm{y}}}
is a
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphism onto its image.

It remains to record the quantitative derivative bound used below.
A standard consequence of reach is the tangent-space variation estimate
[
3
, Corollary 3]

\|P_{T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}}-P_{T_{{\bm{y}}}{\mathcal{M}}}\|_{\rm op}\leq\frac{2\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\kappa},\qquad\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}<\kappa/2,

where
P_{T}
denotes the orthogonal projection onto a subspace
T
.
If
{\bm{z}}\in U_{{\bm{y}}}
and
{\bm{v}}\in T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}
with
\|{\bm{v}}\|_{2}=1
, then

\|P_{(T_{{\bm{y}}}{\mathcal{M}})^{\perp}}{\bm{v}}\|_{2}=\|(P_{T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}}-P_{T_{{\bm{y}}}{\mathcal{M}}}){\bm{v}}\|_{2}\leq\|P_{T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}}-P_{T_{{\bm{y}}}{\mathcal{M}}}\|_{\rm op}.

Because
r_{\rm ch}\leq\kappa/8
, this last quantity is at most
1
.
Hence

\displaystyle\|{\bm{V}}_{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|P_{T_{{\bm{y}}}{\mathcal{M}}}{\bm{v}}\|_{2}^{2}=1-\|P_{(T_{{\bm{y}}}{\mathcal{M}})^{\perp}}{\bm{v}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\|P_{T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}}-P_{T_{{\bm{y}}}{\mathcal{M}}}\|_{\rm op}\geq 1-\frac{2r_{\rm ch}}{\kappa}.

The upper bound in
Equation

14
is immediate because
{\bm{V}}_{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}
is an orthogonal projection.
Homogeneity gives the bound for general
{\bm{v}}
.
∎

By compactness, choose points
{\bm{y}}_{1},\dots,{\bm{y}}_{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\in{\mathcal{M}}
such that

{\mathcal{M}}\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\bigl({\mathcal{M}}\cap{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{y}}_{i},r_{\rm ch}/2)\bigr).

Let
U_{i}:=U_{{\bm{y}}_{i}}
and let
{\bm{V}}_{i}:={\bm{V}}_{{\bm{y}}_{i}}
.
The covering number may be chosen to satisfy the standard reach-volume bound

C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\leq C_{d}\,S_{{\mathcal{M}}}\,r_{\rm ch}^{-d}\leq C_{d}\,S_{{\mathcal{M}}}\,(1\vee\kappa^{-1})^{d},

(15)

where
C_{d}
depends only on
d
.
Indeed, take a maximal
r_{\rm ch}/4
-separated subset of
{\mathcal{M}}
; the balls
{\mathcal{M}}\cap{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{y}}_{i},r_{\rm ch}/2)
cover
{\mathcal{M}}
, while the intrinsic volumes of the disjoint smaller balls are bounded below by
c_{d}r_{\rm ch}^{d}
because the second fundamental form is bounded by
\kappa^{-1}
on the reach scale.

For any
{\bm{y}}\in U_{i}
, define the chart by applying the tangent projection from
Lemma

D.2
and then rescaling and translating the resulting coordinate image:

\phi_{i}({\bm{y}}):=\eta_{i}\bigl({\bm{V}}_{i}^{\top}({\bm{y}}-{\bm{y}}_{i})+{\bm{c}}_{i}\bigr),

(16)

where
\eta_{i}\in(0,1]
and
{\bm{c}}_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
are chosen so that
\phi_{i}(U_{i})\subseteq[0,1]^{d}
.
Indeed,

\phi_{i}({\bm{y}})=\eta_{i}\bigl(\pi_{{\bm{y}}_{i}}({\bm{y}})+{\bm{c}}_{i}\bigr),\qquad\pi_{{\bm{y}}_{i}}({\bm{y}})={\bm{V}}_{i}^{\top}({\bm{y}}-{\bm{y}}_{i}).

Since
\pi_{{\bm{y}}_{i}}
is a diffeomorphism on
U_{i}
by
Lemma

D.2
, and the map
{\bm{u}}\mapsto\eta_{i}({\bm{u}}+{\bm{c}}_{i})
is an invertible affine map of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, so is
\phi_{i}
.
Note that each
\phi_{i}
is the restriction of an affine map on
{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
, and therefore can be exactly represented by a one-hidden-layer ReLU network.

Let

\phi_{i}^{-1}:\phi_{i}(U_{i})\to U_{i}\subset{\mathcal{M}}.

The following proposition establishes a uniform bound on the volume distortion within each local chart:

Proposition D.3

(Uniform Jacobian bounds for projection charts)
.

Under
1
, the Jacobian determinant of the inverse chart
\phi_{i}^{-1}
satisfies, for every
{\bm{u}}\in\phi_{i}(U_{i})
,

\sqrt{\det(\nabla\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}})^{\top}\nabla\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}}))}\leq\frac{1}{\eta_{i}^{d}}\Bigl(1-\frac{2r_{\rm ch}}{\kappa}\Bigr)^{-d/2}.

(17)

Proof.

Fix
i
and write
{\bm{z}}=\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}})
.
The derivative of
\phi_{i}
at
{\bm{z}}
, restricted to
T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}
, is

{\mathrm{D}}\phi_{i}({\bm{z}})|_{T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}}=\eta_{i}\,{\bm{V}}_{i}^{\top}|_{T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}}.

By
Equation

14
, every singular value of
{\bm{V}}_{i}^{\top}|_{T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}}
is at least
(1-2r_{\rm ch}/\kappa)^{1/2}
and at most
1
.
Hence every singular value of
{\mathrm{D}}\phi_{i}({\bm{z}})|_{T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}}
is at least
\eta_{i}(1-2r_{\rm ch}/\kappa)^{1/2}
.
Since
\nabla\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}})
is the inverse linear map from
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
to
T_{{\bm{z}}}{\mathcal{M}}
, its singular values are bounded above by
\eta_{i}^{-1}(1-2r_{\rm ch}/\kappa)^{-1/2}
.
Multiplying the
d
singular values gives
Equation

17
.
∎

We define

B^{\rm Jac}:=\sup_{i}\eta_{i}^{-d}(1-2r_{\rm ch}/\kappa)^{-d/2}.

(18)

D.3.2
Partition-of-unity amplitudes and density regularity

Let
\{\rho_{i}\}_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}
be a
C^{\infty}
partition of unity subordinate to the finite projection atlas
\{U_{i}\}_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}
.
After a finite refinement of the projection cover, we choose it with compactly supported chart pieces

\mathsf{supp}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(\rho_{i})\Subset U_{i},\qquad i=1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}},

which is possible because the smaller sets
{\mathcal{M}}\cap{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{y}}_{i},r_{\rm ch}/2)
still cover the compact manifold and each is compactly contained in
U_{i}
.
Let

{\mathcal{S}}_{i}:=\mathsf{supp}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(\rho_{i})\Subset U_{i},\qquad i=1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}},

(19)

be the compact support of the partition of unity on chart
i
.
The refinement is chosen so that, for every resulting index, the coordinate support
\phi_{i}({\mathcal{S}}_{i})
is contained in nested axis-parallel coordinate boxes compactly contained in
\phi_{i}(U_{i})
.
We fix real numbers

a_{i,r}^{\Box}<a_{i,r}^{\circ}<b_{i,r}^{\circ}<b_{i,r}^{\Box},\qquad r=1,\dots,d,

so that, with

K_{i}^{\circ}:=\prod_{r=1}^{d}[a_{i,r}^{\circ},b_{i,r}^{\circ}],\qquad\Box_{i}:=\prod_{r=1}^{d}[a_{i,r}^{\Box},b_{i,r}^{\Box}],

(20)

we have

\phi_{i}({\mathcal{S}}_{i})\subset\operatorname{int}(K_{i}^{\circ})\subset K_{i}^{\circ}\Subset\operatorname{int}(\Box_{i})\subset\Box_{i}\Subset\phi_{i}(U_{i}).

(21)

These fixed boxes are used both by the tangent-cell construction and, under the density lower bound, by the projection-centered Laplace expansion.
This only changes the finite atlas cardinality by a geometry-dependent factor.
Since
\mathsf{supp}(\rho_{i})\subset U_{i}
, we may insert
1=\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\rho_{i}
into the manifold integral and then change variables chart by chart.
For each chart
i
, define the pulled-back partition weight

\bar{\rho}_{i}({\bm{u}}):=(\rho_{i}\circ\phi_{i}^{-1})({\bm{u}}),\qquad{\bm{u}}\in\phi_{i}(U_{i}).

(22)

For each chart
i\in\{1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\}
, define
{\bm{z}}_{i}:\phi_{i}(U_{i})\to U_{i},{\bm{J}}_{i}:\phi_{i}(U_{i})\to{\mathbb{R}}^{D\times d},{\bm{G}}_{i}:\phi_{i}(U_{i})\to{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
:

\displaystyle{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})

\displaystyle:=\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}})\in U_{i}\subset{\mathcal{M}},

(23)

\displaystyle{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})

\displaystyle:=\nabla{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D\times d},

(24)

\displaystyle{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})

\displaystyle:={\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{\top}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}.

(25)

Then, the density of
{\bm{X}}_{t}=m_{t}{\bm{X}}_{0}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{Z}},{\bm{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}({\bm{0}},{\bm{I}}_{D})
is

\displaystyle p_{t}({\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}}\int_{{\mathcal{M}}}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)p_{0}({\bm{y}}){\mathrm{d}}\mathrm{vol}_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{y}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\int_{{\mathcal{M}}}\rho_{i}({\bm{y}})\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)p_{0}({\bm{y}}){\mathrm{d}}\mathrm{vol}_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{y}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\bar{\rho}_{i}({\bm{u}})\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)p_{0}(\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}}))\sqrt{(\det\nabla\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}})^{\top}\nabla\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}}))}{\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\bar{\rho}_{i}({\bm{u}})\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)p_{0}({\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}))\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})}{\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}

(26)

Lemma D.4

(Hölder regularity in projection coordinates)
.

Assume
p_{0}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}({\mathcal{M}},B_{0})
in the sense of
Definition

2.2
.
Then each projection-coordinate representative
p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}
belongs to
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}(\phi_{i}(U_{i}))
, and

\max_{1\leq i\leq C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\|p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}(\phi_{i}(U_{i}))}<\infty.

The resulting bound depends on
B_{0}
,
\beta
, and finitely many
C^{\lceil\beta\rceil}
-norms of transition maps between the original smooth atlas and the projection atlas.

Proof.

Fix a projection chart
(U_{i},\phi_{i})
.
Since
\overline{U_{i}}
is compact in
{\mathcal{M}}
, it is covered by finitely many relatively compact chart neighborhoods
V_{\alpha}^{\prime}\Subset V_{\alpha}
, where
(V_{\alpha},\psi_{\alpha})
are charts from the atlas used to define
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}({\mathcal{M}},B_{0})
.
Let
W_{i,\alpha}:=U_{i}\cap V_{\alpha}^{\prime}
.
On
\phi_{i}(W_{i,\alpha})
,

p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}=(p_{0}\circ\psi_{\alpha}^{-1})\circ(\psi_{\alpha}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}).

The first factor has
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}
-norm at most
B_{0}
by assumption.
The transition map
\psi_{\alpha}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}
is
C^{\infty}
, and all of its derivatives up to order
\lceil\beta\rceil
are bounded on the compact closure of
\phi_{i}(W_{i,\alpha})
.
The standard composition theorem for Hölder classes, proved by the chain rule for derivatives up to order
\lfloor\beta\rfloor
and the Hölder bound for the top derivative, gives

\|p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}(\phi_{i}(W_{i,\alpha}))}\leq C_{i,\alpha}B_{0},

where
C_{i,\alpha}
depends only on the stated transition-map norms.

To pass from these local estimates to the full coordinate domain
\phi_{i}(U_{i})
, choose a smooth partition of unity
\{\chi_{i,\alpha}\}_{\alpha}
on
U_{i}
subordinate to the finite cover
\{W_{i,\alpha}\}_{\alpha}
, and write

p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}=\sum_{\alpha}\bigl((\chi_{i,\alpha}p_{0})\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}\bigr).

Each summand has compact support in
\phi_{i}(W_{i,\alpha})
.
Extending it by zero to
\phi_{i}(U_{i})
preserves its
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}
-norm up to a constant depending only on the cutoff derivatives and the positive buffer between
\mathsf{supp}\chi_{i,\alpha}
and
U_{i}\setminus W_{i,\alpha}
.
The algebra property of Hölder spaces controls multiplication by
\chi_{i,\alpha}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}
.
Since the number of subpatches is finite, summing the extended pieces gives a finite
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}(\phi_{i}(U_{i}))
bound.
Taking the maximum over the finite projection atlas gives the claim.
∎

By
Lemma

D.4
, the pulled-back density has a finite chartwise Hölder norm.
We write

B_{p}^{\rm proj}:=\max_{1\leq i\leq C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\|p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}(\phi_{i}(U_{i}))}<\infty.

(27)

The constant
B_{p}^{\rm proj}
depends on
B_{0}
,
\beta
, and the finite atlas transition maps; it is generally not equal to
B_{0}
.

Similarly to
p_{t}({\bm{x}})
, the gradient of the density function is given by

\displaystyle\nabla p_{t}({\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}}\int_{{\mathcal{M}}}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)\Bigl(-\frac{{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)p_{0}({\bm{y}}){\mathrm{d}}\mathrm{vol}_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{y}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\bar{\rho}_{i}({\bm{u}})\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)\Bigl(-\frac{{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)p_{0}({\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}))\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})}{\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}.

(28)

Define
a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}:\phi_{i}(U_{i})\to{\mathbb{R}}
and
{\bm{a}}_{i}^{({\mathcal{P}})}:\phi_{i}(U_{i})\to{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
:

a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}):=\bar{\rho}_{i}({\bm{u}})\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})}p_{0}({\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})),\qquad{\bm{a}}_{i}^{({\mathcal{P}})}({\bm{u}}):={\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}).

(29)

For
k=1,\dots,D
, write
a_{i}^{({\mathcal{P}}_{k})}:=a_{i,k}^{({\mathcal{P}})}
, and define the integral-type set

\mathcal{R}:=\{{\mathcal{Q}},{\mathcal{P}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{P}}_{D}\}.

Thus
R\in\mathcal{R}
denotes a type, not the already summed global integral;
a_{i}^{(R)}
denotes
a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}
when
R={\mathcal{Q}}
, and
a_{i}^{({\mathcal{P}}_{k})}
when
R={\mathcal{P}}_{k}
.

The following lemma shows that
a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}
and
{\bm{a}}_{i}^{({\mathcal{P}})}
are
\beta
-Hölder smooth on
\phi_{i}(U_{i})
:

Lemma D.5

(Chart-amplitude Hölder regularity)
.

Assume
1
and
2
hold.
There is a finite constant
B_{a}
such that

a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})},a_{i,k}^{({\mathcal{P}})}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}(\phi_{i}(U_{i}),B_{a}),\qquad k=1,\dots,D.

(30)

One may take

B_{a}=c_{\beta}B_{p}^{\rm proj}(1\vee B^{\rm Jac})\Biggl(1\vee\max_{i,k}\max_{\|{\bm{\alpha}}\|_{1}\leq\lceil\beta\rceil}\Bigl\{\|\partial^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\bar{\rho}_{i}\|_{\infty}\vee\|\partial^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}}\|_{\infty}\vee\|\partial^{{\bm{\alpha}}}z_{i,k}\|_{\infty}\Bigr\}\Biggr).

Moreover, because the manifold is embedded in
[0,1]^{D}
, we have the coordinate-wise bounds:

|a_{i,k}^{({\mathcal{P}})}({\bm{u}})|\leq a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}})\leq B_{p}^{\rm proj}B^{\rm Jac}.

(31)

Proof of
Lemma

D.5
.

Step 1: Uniform
L^{\infty}
bounds.

Because
p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}
is
\beta
-Hölder smooth on the parameter space
\phi_{i}(U_{i})\subset[0,1]^{d}
.
Specifically, its Hölder norm (and consequently its supremum norm) is bounded by:

p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}(\phi_{i}(U_{i}),B_{p}^{\rm proj}),\quad\text{for each }i=1,2,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}}.

(32)

By definition of the partition of unity,
0\leq\bar{\rho}_{i}({\bm{u}})\leq 1
and by
Proposition

D.3
, the Jacobian determinant is uniformly bounded by
B^{\rm Jac}
.
Multiplying these supremum bounds yields:

|a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}})|=|\bar{\rho}_{i}({\bm{u}})\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})}p_{0}({\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}))|\leq 1\cdot B^{\rm Jac}\cdot B_{p}^{\rm proj}.

Since the manifold is contained in
[0,1]^{D}
, we have
|z_{i,k}({\bm{u}})|\leq 1
, which immediately implies
|a_{i,k}^{({\mathcal{P}})}({\bm{u}})|\leq a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}})
, verifying
Equation

31
.

Step 2: Hölder regularity and the explicit bound
B_{a}
.

Because the manifold and the partition of unity are
C^{\infty}
, the geometric multipliers
\bar{\rho}_{i}
,
{\bm{z}}_{i}
, and
\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}}
(which is smooth since
{\bm{G}}_{i}
is strictly positive definite) possess uniformly bounded derivatives of all orders on the compact chart supports.

Let
C_{\rm geom}
be one plus the maximum
C^{\lceil\beta\rceil}
norm of these geometric factors across all finitely many charts
i\in\{1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\}
and coordinates
k\in\{1,\dots,D\}
:

C_{\rm geom}:=1\vee\max_{i,k}\max_{|{\bm{\alpha}}|\leq\lceil\beta\rceil}\Bigl\{\|\partial^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\bar{\rho}_{i}\|_{\infty}\vee\|\partial^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}}\|_{\infty}\vee\|\partial^{{\bm{\alpha}}}z_{i,k}\|_{\infty}\Bigr\}.

Consequently, the
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}
norms of
\bar{\rho}_{i}
,
\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}}
, and
z_{i,k}
are all strictly bounded by
C_{\rm geom}
.

We invoke the algebra property of Hölder spaces: for any
f,g\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}
, there exists a constant
c_{\beta}
such that
\|fg\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}}\leq c_{\beta}(\|f\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{\infty}+\|f\|_{\infty}\|g\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}})
.
Applying this iteratively to the product
a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}=(\bar{\rho}_{i}\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}})\cdot(p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i})
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\|a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\beta}^{2}\Bigl(\|\bar{\rho}_{i}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}}\|\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}}\|_{\infty}+\|\bar{\rho}_{i}\|_{\infty}\|\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}}\Bigr)\|p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}\|_{\infty}+c_{\beta}\|\bar{\rho}_{i}\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}}\|_{\infty}\|p_{0}\circ{\bm{z}}_{i}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\beta}^{2}\bigl(C_{\rm geom}B^{\rm Jac}+1\cdot C_{\rm geom}\bigr)B_{p}^{\rm proj}+c_{\beta}(1\cdot B^{\rm Jac})B_{p}^{\rm proj}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\beta}^{2}B_{p}^{\rm proj}\Bigl[C_{\rm geom}(B^{\rm Jac}+1)+B^{\rm Jac}\Bigr].

Similarly, for
a_{i,j}^{({\mathcal{P}})}=z_{i,j}a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}
, applying the product rule again yields:

\displaystyle\|a_{i,j}^{({\mathcal{P}})}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\beta}\Bigl(\|z_{i,j}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}}\|a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}\|_{\infty}+\|z_{i,j}\|_{\infty}\|a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq c_{\beta}\Bigl(C_{\rm geom}B^{\rm Jac}B_{p}^{\rm proj}+1\cdot\|a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}}\Bigr).

Taking the maximum of these explicit bounds over all charts and coordinates, we may choose the universal Hölder constant as:

\displaystyle B_{a}:=c_{\beta}^{\prime}B_{p}^{\rm proj}C_{\rm geom}(1\vee B^{\rm Jac}).

∎

D.3.3
Chart-integral representation of density and score

Define
F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}:\phi_{i}(U_{i})\to{\mathbb{R}}
:

F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}}):=\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}.

(33)

Fix
n\geq 2
.
In statements using polynomial regimes
\varepsilon\geq n^{-a_{\varepsilon}}
and
D\vee\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-1}\leq n^{a_{\rm par}}
, regard
a_{\varepsilon},a_{\rm par}
as fixed.
Choose once and for all a threshold
C_{\varrho}=C_{\varrho}(a_{\varepsilon},a_{\rm par})\geq 1
, independent of
D
,
n
,
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}
, and all numerical
\varepsilon
-values after those exponents are fixed, large enough that the off-manifold deviation and Fisher bounds in
Lemmas

C.1
and
C.2
hold with threshold
C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{D\vee\log n}
, with the polynomial tail exponent required by the branchwise approximation and estimation arguments.
This same constant determines the near-manifold domain used throughout the branchwise near-manifold approximation results.
Enlarge the fixed tube-volume constant
C_{\rm tube}
from
Equation

9
, if necessary, so that
C_{\rm tube}\geq C_{\varrho}
.
Dependence on the tube radius is tracked through the displayed quantity
M_{x}^{\varrho}
below; it is not hidden in constants that are declared independent of
D
and
n
.

\varrho_{t}:=C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{D\vee\log n},

(34)

For each
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
, define the time-indexed near-manifold tube

A_{t}:=\{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}:\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\leq\varrho_{t}\}.

(35)

Thus a condition such as
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
is always interpreted at the same time
t
.
Since
{\mathcal{M}}\subset[0,1]^{D}
,
m_{t}\leq 1
, and
\sigma_{t}\leq 1
, every
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
and
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
satisfy

\|{\bm{x}}\|_{\infty}\leq 1+C_{\varrho}\sqrt{D\vee\log n}.

We therefore write

M_{x}^{\varrho}:=1+C_{\varrho}\sqrt{D\vee\log n}.

(36)

For
i\in\{1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\}
, define the chart integrals for all pairs
({\bm{x}},t)
with
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
and
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)

\displaystyle:=\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr){\bm{a}}_{i}^{({\mathcal{P}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}},

(37)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)

\displaystyle:=\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}},

(38)

Let

{\mathcal{P}}({\bm{x}},t):=\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}{\mathcal{P}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D},\qquad{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t):=\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathbb{R}}.

Define the centered local and global numerator integrals

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)

\displaystyle:={}\frac{m_{t}{\mathcal{P}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{x}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)}{\sigma_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\frac{m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})-{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}},

(39)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}{\mathcal{U}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=\frac{m_{t}{\mathcal{P}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{x}}{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)}{\sigma_{t}}.

(40)

Then

s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)=\nabla\log p_{t}({\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}}\frac{{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)}{{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)}\qquad(p_{t}({\bm{x}})>0).

(41)

For a chart-integral floor
C_{t}>0
, the centered floor-regularized score is

s_{{\rm reg},C_{t}}({\bm{x}},t):=\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}}\frac{{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)}{{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)\vee C_{t}}.

(42)

For reference, by
Equations

26
and
28
, the same regularized score can also be written in the uncentered form

\displaystyle\frac{\nabla p_{t}({\bm{x}})}{p_{t}({\bm{x}})\vee\rho_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{1}{(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\bigl(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\bigr)\bigl(-\frac{{\bm{x}}-m_{t}\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\bigr)a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}}{\frac{1}{(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\bigl(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}\phi_{i}^{-1}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\bigr)a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}\vee\rho_{t}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\bigl(-\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\bigr)a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\bigl(-\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\bigr)a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}\vee(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}\rho_{t}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{m_{t}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\bigl(-\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\bigr){\bm{a}}_{i}^{({\mathcal{P}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\bigl(-\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\bigr)a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}\vee(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}\rho_{t}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\frac{{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)}{{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)\vee C_{t}}+\frac{m_{t}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}({\bm{x}},t)}{{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)\vee C_{t}},

(43)

where

C_{t}:=\sigma_{t}^{-d}(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}\rho_{t}.

(44)

Conversely, choosing the chart-integral floor
C_{t}
fixes the density-level floor as

\rho_{t}=(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{-D/2}\sigma_{t}^{d}C_{t}.

(45)

Lemma D.6

(Uniform bounds for chart moment and density integrals)
.

Let

V_{\rm chart}:=1\vee\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\mathrm{vol}(\phi_{i}(U_{i}))<\infty.

There is a finite
M_{0}:=B_{p}^{\rm proj}B^{\rm Jac}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-d}V_{\rm chart}
such that

0\leq{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)\leq M_{0},\qquad|{\mathcal{P}}_{k}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)\leq M_{0},\quad k=1,\dots,D.

(46)

Proof of
Lemma

D.6
.

We first show
|{\mathcal{P}}_{k}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)
.
Since
{\mathcal{M}}\subset[0,1]^{D}
,
0\leq z_{i,k}({\bm{u}})\leq 1
, so
|a_{i,k}^{({\mathcal{P}})}({\bm{u}})|\leq a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}})
and
|{\mathcal{P}}_{i,k}|\leq{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
.
Summing over
i
gives
|{\mathcal{P}}_{k}|\leq{\mathcal{Q}}
.

We next prove the uniform upper bound for
{\mathcal{Q}}
.
Note that
\exp\bigl(-F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})/\sigma_{t}^{2}\bigr)\leq 1
,
\sigma_{t}^{-d}\leq\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-d}
, and by
Equation

31
we have

{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\leq B_{p}^{\rm proj}B^{\rm Jac}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-d}\mathrm{vol}(\phi_{i}(U_{i})).

Summing over charts gives the displayed
M_{0}
.
∎

D.4
Centered ratio target and stability

We use the integral-type set
\mathcal{R}=\{{\mathcal{Q}},{\mathcal{P}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{P}}_{D}\}
fixed after
Equation

29
.
In this subsection
R_{i}
denotes the corresponding local chart component:
R_{i}={\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
when
R={\mathcal{Q}}
, and
R_{i}={\mathcal{P}}_{i,k}
when
R={\mathcal{P}}_{k}
.
The common cell notation below is used by the tangent-cell construction and by the density-lower-bound projection–Laplace construction.
The method-specific approximation results are stated in the following sections.

D.4.1
Intrinsic cell notation and density floors

For each chart, fix the common compact support envelope

K_{i}^{\rm cell}:=\phi_{i}({\mathcal{S}}_{i}).

(47)

By
Equation

21
,
K_{i}^{\rm cell}\Subset\Box_{i}
.
Moreover, for every
R\in\mathcal{R}
, the amplitude
a_{i}^{(R)}
vanishes outside
K_{i}^{\rm cell}
, because each amplitude contains the factor
\bar{\rho}_{i}
.

For
R\in\mathcal{R}
, define the chart integral

R_{i}({\bm{x}},t):=\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{K_{i}^{\rm cell}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}},

(48)

We use the same amplitude as a function on
\Box_{i}
; by
Lemma

D.5
on
\phi_{i}(U_{i})
and the restriction
\Box_{i}\Subset\phi_{i}(U_{i})
, its restriction satisfies
a_{i}^{(R)}|_{\Box_{i}}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}(\Box_{i},B_{a})
.
Since
K_{i}^{\rm cell}\Subset\Box_{i}
, set

\delta_{i}^{\rm cell}:=\mathrm{dist}(K_{i}^{\rm cell},\partial\Box_{i})>0.

For every
0<h\leq\delta_{i}^{\rm cell}/(2\sqrt{d})
, let
\mathcal{Q}_{i,h}
be the finite family of half-open cubes

Q_{\nu}={\bm{u}}_{\nu}+h[0,1)^{d}

from the standard
h
-lattice whose closures intersect
K_{i}^{\rm cell}
.
Then

K_{i}^{\rm cell}\subset\bigcup_{\nu\in\mathcal{Q}_{i,h}}\overline{Q}_{\nu}\subset\Box_{i},\qquad|\mathcal{Q}_{i,h}|\leq C_{i}h^{-d}.

Indeed, every point in such a cube is within distance
\sqrt{d}\,h
of
K_{i}^{\rm cell}
, and the cardinality bound follows by comparing the volume of the
\sqrt{d}\,h
-neighborhood of the fixed compact set
K_{i}^{\rm cell}
with the cube volume
h^{d}
.
Since
a_{i}^{(R)}
vanishes outside
K_{i}^{\rm cell}
, integration over
K_{i}^{\rm cell}
is equivalent to the sum of the integrals over these full cubes; cube boundaries have Lebesgue measure zero.

Appendix E
Large-Noise Tangent-Cell Score Approximation

With a density lower bound, the unrefactored denominator
{\mathcal{Q}}
has an explicit tube lower bound in the large-noise regime.
Thus the separated tangent-cell moment approximation can be used directly for
{\mathcal{Q}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
, followed by an ordinary reciprocal network.

Large-noise proof architecture.

The target result is
Theorem

E.5
. The proof has four
modules: derive tangent-cell phase bounds; approximate each chart integral with cells of
radius
r_{k}=c_{0}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}\wedge r_{\star,n}
in the statistical application
(
Proposition

G.2
); use the density lower bound to construct the
reciprocal of
{\mathcal{Q}}
; and assemble the centered ratio
{\mathcal{U}}/{\mathcal{Q}}
with clipping. The
important size fact is that the cell count is explicitly recorded as

\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+r_{\star,n}^{-d}
rather than hidden in asymptotic notation.

E.1
Tangent-cell phase decomposition and gated chart approximation

The tangent-cell construction follows the tangent-space moment idea.
The network is fixed in advance: every cell is represented by a gated subnetwork, and the gate is an explicit ReLU ramp in a computable phase proxy.
Thus no input-dependent choice of active cells is made after the network has been constructed.

Lemma E.1

(Tangent-cell Gaussian phase bounds)
.

Let
\nu
be a cell index in a finite coordinate-cube partition of
\Box_{i}
.
Let
Q_{\nu}\subset\Box_{i}
be the corresponding cube of side length
h
, let
{\bm{u}}_{\nu}
be its center, and set
{\bm{y}}_{\nu}={\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{\nu})
.
Let
\mathsf{P}_{\nu}^{\rm tan}
be the orthogonal linear projection in
{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
onto the tangent space
T_{{\bm{y}}_{\nu}}{\mathcal{M}}
.
Define

\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t):=m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu}+\mathsf{P}_{\nu}^{\rm tan}({\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu}),\qquad\Phi_{\nu}^{0}({\bm{x}},t):=\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

The symbol
\mathsf{T}_{\nu}
denotes an affine tangent-plane linearization center; it is
not the nearest-point projection
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}
used in the small-noise branch.
There are constants
c_{\rm ph},C_{\rm ph}>0
, depending only on the fixed atlas and on
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}
, such that if
h\leq c_{\rm ph}\sigma_{t}
, then the following hold for all
{\bm{u}}\in Q_{\nu}
,
{\bm{y}}={\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})
.
First, if
\Phi_{\nu}^{0}({\bm{x}},t)\leq H
with
H\geq 1
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\sigma_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm ph}H^{1/2},

(49)

\displaystyle\frac{|T_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}},t)|}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm ph}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}\,h\,H^{1/2},

(50)

\displaystyle\frac{m_{t}^{2}D_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}},t)}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm ph}H.

(51)

Second, if
\Phi_{\nu}^{0}({\bm{x}},t)\geq AH
for a sufficiently large numerical constant
A\geq A_{\rm ph}
, then

\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\geq c_{\rm ph}AH.

(52)

Proof.

The chart map is
C^{2}
with norm bounded by
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}
.
Hence, for
{\bm{u}}\in Q_{\nu}
,

{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})={\bm{y}}_{\nu}+{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{\nu})({\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}_{\nu})+{\bm{e}}_{\nu}({\bm{u}}),\qquad\|{\bm{e}}_{\nu}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}h^{2}.

The vector
{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{\nu})({\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}_{\nu})
lies in
T_{{\bm{y}}_{\nu}}{\mathcal{M}}
, while
{\bm{x}}-\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)
is orthogonal to this tangent space.
Therefore

T_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}},t)=\langle{\bm{x}}-\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t),\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\rangle

only sees the quadratic normal error
{\bm{e}}_{\nu}
in the cell, and

|T_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}},t)|\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}\,m_{t}h^{2}\|{\bm{x}}-\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}.

If
\Phi_{\nu}^{0}\leq H
, then

\|{\bm{x}}-\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{2H}\,\sigma_{t}.

Since
h\leq c_{\rm ph}\sigma_{t}
and
m_{t}\leq 1
, this proves
Equation

50
.
The same triangle inequality gives

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu}\|_{2}+m_{t}\|{\bm{y}}-{\bm{y}}_{\nu}\|_{2}\leq C\sigma_{t}H^{1/2},

after decreasing
c_{\rm ph}
, which proves
Equation

49
.
Moreover, since
m_{t}^{2}D_{\nu}=\|\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}
, we avoid any division by the possibly small schedule value
m_{t}
.
Indeed,

\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)-m_{t}{\bm{y}}=\mathsf{P}_{\nu}^{\rm tan}({\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu})-m_{t}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{\nu})({\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}_{\nu})-m_{t}{\bm{e}}_{\nu}({\bm{u}}),

so the right-hand side is bounded by
C(\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu}\|_{2}+h)
.
This gives
Equation

51
because
h\leq c_{\rm ph}\sigma_{t}
and
\Phi_{\nu}^{0}\leq H
.

For the tail statement, if
\Phi_{\nu}^{0}\geq AH
, then

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\geq\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu}\|_{2}-m_{t}\|{\bm{y}}-{\bm{y}}_{\nu}\|_{2}\geq\sigma_{t}\sqrt{2AH}-Ch.

Choosing
A_{\rm ph}
large and
c_{\rm ph}
small gives
\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})\geq c_{\rm ph}AH
.
∎

Lemma E.2

(Scalar exponential Taylor bounds)
.

There are universal constants
A_{\exp},c_{\exp}>0
such that the following hold.
If
0\leq z\leq H
and
J\geq A_{\exp}H
, then

\left|e^{-z}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{J}\frac{(-z)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right|\leq e^{-c_{\exp}J}.

(53)

If
|z|\leq a\leq 1
, then for every integer
K\geq 0
,

\left|e^{-z}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{K}\frac{(-z)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right|\leq C_{K}a^{K+1}e^{a}.

(54)

Proof.

For
Equation

54
, Taylor’s theorem with integral remainder gives the claim.
For
Equation

53
, the Lagrange remainder is bounded by

\frac{z^{J+1}}{(J+1)!}\leq\left(\frac{eH}{J+1}\right)^{J+1}.

Taking
J\geq A_{\exp}H
with
A_{\exp}>2e
makes the last display at most
e^{-c_{\exp}J}
.
∎

E.2
Scale choices for tangent-cell large-noise approximation

On a large-noise slab
I_{k}=[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
, the cell radius is chosen at the slab
noise scale until it reaches the accuracy-limited radius required by the fixed-order
tangent-cell expansion. In
Proposition

G.2
this is implemented as

r_{k}=c_{0}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}\wedge r_{\star,n}
, so the per-slab cell count is controlled by

\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+r_{\star,n}^{-d}
.
The centered residual structure then permits the sharper scaled residual tolerance
n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}
, so the score error is
\sigma_{t}^{-2}n^{-2(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}
rather than
\sigma_{t}^{-2}n^{-2\beta/(d+2\beta)}
.
Under
3
, the denominator lower bound in

Lemma

E.4
stabilizes the ratio step; the remaining
accuracy dependence enters through the explicit finite-order cell-radius cap.
Consequently the switched large-noise network has total leading intrinsic size

\sum_{k}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}
plus the accuracy-cap contribution. The latter is
explicitly propagated through the learned-score and
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
bounds rather than hidden
inside a polynomial factor.

Proposition E.3

(Tangent-cell chart approximation with separated cell scale and accuracy)
.

Assume
1
and
2
.
Let
q_{\rm geom}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}
be as in
Definition

B.5
.
Fix a cell-scale parameter
0<\varepsilon_{\rm cell}\leq 1/2
, a denominator floor
q_{\rm floor}\in(0,1]
, and an accuracy parameter
0<\varepsilon_{\rm rel}\leq 1/16
.
Put

r_{\rm cell}:=\varepsilon_{\rm cell}^{1/\beta},\qquad{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm lg}^{\rm cell}:=\{t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]:\sigma_{t}\geq c_{\rm lg}r_{\rm cell}\},

and define

H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}:=1+\log\!\left(C_{\rm lg}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}B_{0}^{C_{\rm lg}}(D\vee\log n)^{C_{\rm lg}}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-d}q_{\rm floor}^{-1}\varepsilon_{\rm rel}^{-1}\right).

(55)

Assume the fixed-order geometry remainder is below the requested tolerance and the
linear tangent phase is kept in the small-argument regime, for a sufficiently small
numerical constant
c_{\rm ph,cell}>0
:

\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}r_{\rm cell}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{1/2}\leq c_{\rm ph,cell},\qquad\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}r_{\rm cell}^{q_{\rm geom}-2}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C}\leq c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}.

(56)

Then, for every chart
i
, there exist ReLU networks
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}^{\rm lg}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{i,k}^{\rm lg}
,
k=1,\dots,D
, such that, uniformly for
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm lg}^{\rm cell}
and
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
,

|{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}^{\rm lg}({\bm{x}},t)|\vee\max_{1\leq k\leq D}|{\mathcal{U}}_{i,k}({\bm{x}},t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{i,k}^{\rm lg}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq\varepsilon_{\rm rel}\bigl({\mathcal{Q}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)+q_{\rm floor}/C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\bigr).

(57)

Moreover, the depth, log-weight bound, width, and sparsity satisfy the following bounds, with the direct ambient powers displayed explicitly.

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm lg}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C},

(58)

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm lg}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C},

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm lg}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}D^{4(q_{\rm geom}+2)}(D\vee\log n)^{4(q_{\rm geom}+2)}\varepsilon_{\rm cell}^{-d/\beta}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C},

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm lg}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}D^{4(q_{\rm geom}+2)}(D\vee\log n)^{4(q_{\rm geom}+2)}\varepsilon_{\rm cell}^{-d/\beta}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C}.

Thus, for this separated tangent-cell moment approximation, the cell count is determined by
r_{\rm cell}
, and the conclusion is valid exactly on the range where
Equation

56
holds.
If the slab-scale radius does not satisfy that condition, the cell radius must be capped by the accuracy-limited radius used in
Proposition

G.2
.
When
r_{\rm cell}
is specialized to

c_{0}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}\wedge r_{\star,n}
in the large-noise estimation theorem, the
resulting additional term
r_{\star,n}^{-d}
is recorded explicitly in the learned-score
stochastic bound.

Proof.

Fix a chart
i
.
Choose once and for all a fixed number
c_{h}>0
such that

c_{h}\leq 1\wedge c_{\rm ph}c_{\rm lg}\wedge\min_{1\leq j\leq C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\frac{\delta_{j}^{\rm cell}}{2\sqrt{d}}.

This choice depends only on the fixed atlas,
q_{\rm geom}
, and the branch constants, and is independent of
\varepsilon_{\rm cell}
,
t
,
{\bm{x}}
, and
n
.
Partition the fixed compact coefficient support
K_{i}^{\rm cell}\Subset\Box_{i}
into coordinate cubes
\{Q_{\nu}:\nu\in\mathcal{V}_{i}\}
of side length
h=c_{h}r_{\rm cell}
.
Here
\nu
is a cell label in the finite index set
\mathcal{V}_{i}
.
The number of cells is
N_{\rm cell}\leq C\varepsilon_{\rm cell}^{-d/\beta}
.
For every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm lg}^{\rm cell}
, the definition of
c_{h}
gives
h=c_{h}r_{\rm cell}\leq c_{\rm ph}\sigma_{t}
, so
Lemma

E.1
applies on every retained cell.
The same choice gives
h\leq\delta_{i}^{\rm cell}/(2\sqrt{d})
, so every cell whose closure intersects
K_{i}^{\rm cell}
is contained in
\Box_{i}
.
Let
{\bm{u}}_{\nu}
be the cell center,
{\bm{y}}_{\nu}={\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{\nu})
, and
\mathsf{P}_{\nu}^{\rm tan}
the orthogonal projection onto
T_{{\bm{y}}_{\nu}}{\mathcal{M}}
.
Define

\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)=m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu}+\mathsf{P}_{\nu}^{\rm tan}({\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu}).

For
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{M}}
, the identity

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}=\|{\bm{x}}-\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}+2T_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}},t)+m_{t}^{2}D_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}},t)

holds with

T_{\nu}=\langle{\bm{x}}-\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t),\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\rangle,\qquad D_{\nu}=\|\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)/m_{t}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}.

For
{\bm{y}}={\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})
, write the exact cell exponential as

E_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{u}},t):=\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)

(59)

and the tangent Gaussian envelope as

G_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t):=\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right).

(60)

The distance identity gives the exact factorization

E_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{u}},t)=G_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)\exp\!\left(-\frac{T_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}),t)}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\exp\!\left(-\frac{m_{t}^{2}D_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}),t)}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right).

(61)

Hence the exact chart-cell pieces are

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{i,\nu}({\bm{x}},t)

\displaystyle:=\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{Q_{\nu}}E_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{u}},t)a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}},

(62)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{i,\nu,k}({\bm{x}},t)

\displaystyle:=\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{Q_{\nu}}\frac{m_{t}z_{i,k}({\bm{u}})-x_{k}}{\sigma_{t}}E_{\nu}({\bm{x}},{\bm{u}},t)a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}.

Step 1: fixed gates and tails.

Use the center-phase proxy

\Phi_{\nu}^{0}({\bm{x}},t)=\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

Let
\theta_{\rm lg}:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
be the fixed piecewise-affine ReLU ramp with
\theta_{\rm lg}(u)=1
for
u\leq A_{0}
and
\theta_{\rm lg}(u)=0
for
u\geq 2A_{0}
, where
A_{0}\geq A_{\rm ph}
is a large numerical constant.
Define the exact cell gate

\Theta_{\nu}^{\rm ex}({\bm{x}},t):=\theta_{\rm lg}\!\left(\Phi_{\nu}^{0}({\bm{x}},t)/H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}\right).

The network gate
\Theta_{\nu}
is obtained by approximating
m_{t}
,
\sigma_{t}^{-2}
, the squared norm, and the final ramp.
Its arithmetic accuracy is chosen so that, outside the ramp transition band,

|\Theta_{\nu}-\Theta_{\nu}^{\rm ex}|\leq c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}(N_{\rm cell}C_{{\mathcal{M}}})^{-1}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{-C}.

Inside the transition band, the exact proxy is between
A_{0}H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}
and
2A_{0}H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}
, so the true cell contribution is controlled by the explicit tail estimate below; the gate implementation error is assigned to that tail budget.
If the exact proxy is above
A_{0}H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}
,
Lemma

E.1
and the Gaussian tail estimate give

\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{Q_{\nu}}e^{-\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})}a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}\leq C\sigma_{t}^{-d}h^{d}e^{-cA_{0}H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}}.

The centered factor in
{\mathcal{U}}_{i,k}
is bounded on the non-negligible tail by
C(D\vee\log n)(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{1/2}
.
Summing over all cells and using
\sigma_{t}^{-d}\leq\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-d}
,
N_{\rm cell}h^{d}\leq C
, and
Equation

55
, with
A_{0}
and
C_{\rm lg}
large enough, gives the explicit exponential budget

C(D\vee\log n)(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{1/2}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-d}\exp(-cA_{0}H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})\leq c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}q_{\rm floor}/C_{{\mathcal{M}}}.

Thus all gated-away and gate-transition contributions are bounded by
c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}q_{\rm floor}/C_{{\mathcal{M}}}
.

Step 2: fixed-order geometric replacement.

On cells with
\Theta_{\nu}^{\rm ex}\neq 0
,
Lemma

E.1
yields, for every
{\bm{u}}\in Q_{\nu}
,

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}/\sigma_{t}\leq C(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{1/2},

|T_{\nu}|/\sigma_{t}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}h(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{1/2},\qquad m_{t}^{2}D_{\nu}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})\leq CH_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}.

Replace
{\bm{z}}_{i}
in
T_{\nu},D_{\nu}
, and in the centered factor
(m_{t}z_{i,k}({\bm{u}})-x_{k})/\sigma_{t}
, by its Taylor polynomial at
{\bm{u}}_{\nu}
of fixed degree
q_{\rm geom}-2
.
The exponent and centered-factor perturbations are bounded by

C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}r_{\rm cell}^{q_{\rm geom}-2}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C},

which is at most
c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}
by
Equation

56
.
The phase-smallness part of the same condition gives

|T_{\nu}|/\sigma_{t}^{2}\leq 1/2
, while
m_{t}^{2}D_{\nu}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})\geq 0
; hence the derivative
of the non-Gaussian exponential factor is bounded by an absolute constant on the
interpolation between the exact and polynomialized phases.
The mean-value theorem for
e^{-z}
then gives a cell error bounded by

c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}\,\sigma_{t}^{-d}e^{-\|{\bm{x}}-\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)\|^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})}\int_{Q_{\nu}}a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}+c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}q_{\rm floor}(C_{{\mathcal{M}}}N_{\rm cell})^{-1}.

From this point through Step 4,
T_{\nu}
,
D_{\nu}
, and the centered factor are understood to be their polynomialized versions; the exact-to-polynomialized replacement error is the displayed error above.

Step 3: scalar Taylor expansions.

For the polynomialized geometry, expand the first non-Gaussian exponential in
Equation

61
,

\exp\!\left(-T_{\nu}/\sigma_{t}^{2}\right),

to the fixed order
q_{\rm geom}-3
.
The small-argument hypothesis needed for this Taylor expansion is the first, explicit
phase-smallness inequality in
Equation

56
: using

Equation

50
and the choice of the cell side length,

|T_{\nu}|/\sigma_{t}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}r_{\rm cell}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{1/2}\leq 1/2

after decreasing
c_{\rm ph,cell}
.
The Taylor remainder is controlled by the second, high-order geometry inequality in

Equation

56
; indeed, after enlarging the harmless
exponent
C
,

\left|\frac{T_{\nu}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right|^{q_{\rm geom}-2}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}r_{\rm cell}^{q_{\rm geom}-2}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C}\leq c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}.

Hence
Equation

54
gives the explicit Taylor remainder

\left|\exp\!\left(-T_{\nu}/\sigma_{t}^{2}\right)-\sum_{\ell=0}^{q_{\rm geom}-3}\frac{(-T_{\nu}/\sigma_{t}^{2})^{\ell}}{\ell!}\right|\leq c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}.

Multiplication by the explicit Gaussian envelope
G_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)
gives, for
{\mathcal{Q}}
, a contribution bounded by

c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}\,\sigma_{t}^{-d}G_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)\int_{Q_{\nu}}a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}},

while for
{\mathcal{U}}
the centered factor is bounded on gated cells by
C(D\vee\log n)(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{1/2}
and is absorbed into the
(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C}
budgets above.
Expand the second non-Gaussian exponential in
Equation

61
,

\exp\!\left(-\frac{m_{t}^{2}D_{\nu}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right),

to order
J=\lceil C_{J}H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}\rceil
.
By
Lemma

E.2
, choosing
C_{J}
large enough makes the remainder explicitly

\left|\exp\!\left(-\frac{m_{t}^{2}D_{\nu}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)-\sum_{j=0}^{J}\frac{[-m_{t}^{2}D_{\nu}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})]^{j}}{j!}\right|\leq e^{-c_{\exp}J}.

Since
J=\lceil C_{J}H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}\rceil
,
N_{\rm cell}h^{d}\leq C
, and
\sigma_{t}^{-d}\leq\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-d}
,
Equation

55
gives, for
C_{J}
and
C_{\rm lg}
large enough,

\sigma_{t}^{-d}h^{d}e^{-c_{\exp}J}\leq c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}q_{\rm floor}(C_{{\mathcal{M}}}N_{\rm cell})^{-1}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{-C}.

This is the absolute cell budget used in the final summation.

Step 4: stored moments and neural implementation.

After the two scalar expansions, each cell contribution is a finite sum of atoms consisting of powers of
m_{t}
, powers of
\sigma_{t}^{-1}
, the tangent coordinates
\mathsf{P}_{\nu}^{\rm tan}({\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu})
, fixed-degree normal residual terms, and the explicit Gaussian envelope

G_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)=\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-\mathsf{T}_{\nu}({\bm{x}},t)\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right).

The coefficients are fixed moments of the form

\int_{Q_{\nu}}({\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}_{\nu})^{\alpha}a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}

for
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
, and for
{\mathcal{U}}_{i,k}
the same moments together with

\int_{Q_{\nu}}({\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}_{\nu})^{\alpha}z_{i,\nu,k}^{(q)}({\bm{u}})a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}},

where
z_{i,\nu,k}^{(q)}
is the fixed Taylor polynomial.
The term
-x_{k}/\sigma_{t}
multiplies the
{\mathcal{Q}}
-moment family.
Hence the centered numerator is expanded directly and no amplitude
a_{i}^{({\mathcal{U}}_{k})}
is introduced.
The magnitudes of these stored moments are bounded by fixed powers of
B_{0}
and the geometry constants through
Lemma

D.5
.
This is why
Equations

55
and
58
display the density-amplitude factor explicitly.

There are at most
(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C}
atoms per cell; the dependence on the fixed order
q_{\rm geom}-3
is absorbed into the fixed polynomial powers of
D
,
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}
, and
H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}
.
More explicitly, for one cell atom:

•

the affine projection
\mathsf{P}_{\nu}^{\rm tan}({\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\nu})
, squared norm, and coordinate summations contribute a multiplicative factor at most
D^{4}
;

•

the fixed Taylor replacement of order at most
q_{\rm geom}
contributes a multiplicative factor at most
D^{4q_{\rm geom}}
, while all intrinsic monomial counts depend only on
d
and
q_{\rm geom}
;

•

the input ranges on
A_{t}
, the centered numerator factor, and the scalar arithmetic subnetworks contribute a multiplicative factor at most
(D\vee\log n)^{4(q_{\rm geom}+2)}
, with the remaining dependence absorbed into
(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C}
.

Thus the whole cellwise implementation is bounded by

D^{4q_{\rm geom}+4}(D\vee\log n)^{4(q_{\rm geom}+2)}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{C}

per cell after harmless enlargement of constants, which is covered by the exponent choice in the proposition statement.
Reciprocal, schedule-power, scalar exponential, monomial, and product networks are assigned relative accuracy
c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{-C}
and absolute accuracy
c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}q_{\rm floor}(C_{{\mathcal{M}}}N_{\rm cell})^{-1}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec})^{-C}
.
Summing the atom errors, the gate tails, the geometric replacement error, and the Taylor remainders gives
Equation

57
.
Parallelizing over
N_{\rm cell}
cells gives
Equation

58
.
∎

Lemma E.4

(Large-noise denominator lower bound)
.

Assume
1
,
2
and
3
.
There are constants
c_{\rm lb},C_{\rm lb}>0
, depending only on the displayed finite
geometry controls, such that

c_{\rm lb}^{-1}\vee C_{\rm lb}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}

after enlarging the finite-order geometry constant from

Definition

B.5
. For every
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
and every

{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}=\{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\leq C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{D\vee\log n}\},

{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)\geq q_{\rm lb}:=c_{\rm lb}p_{\min}\exp\{-C_{\rm lb}C_{\varrho}^{2}(D\vee\log n)\}.

(63)

Moreover

\frac{\|{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}}{{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)}\leq R_{\rm lb}:=1+\Bigl[2\log\!\bigl(e\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-d}q_{\rm lb}^{-1}\bigr)+C_{\rm lb}(D\vee\log n)\Bigr]^{1/2}.

(64)

Proof.

Step 1: lower-bound the local mass in one intrinsic ball.

Fix
({\bm{x}},t)
in the displayed tube and choose
{\bm{y}}_{0}\in{\mathcal{M}}
with

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{0}\|_{2}=\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})
.
Let
a_{\rm lb}\in(0,1]
be smaller than the injectivity and coordinate-buffer radii of
the projection atlas. By the finite-order geometry convention and

Lemma

F.3
, it can be chosen so that

a_{\rm lb}^{-1}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}
, and the small-ball volume bound

\mathrm{vol}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(B_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{y}},a_{\rm lb}r))\geq C^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{-C}r^{d},\qquad 0<r\leq 1,

holds uniformly in
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{M}}
. Since
0<\sigma_{t}\leq 1
, the ball

B_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{y}}_{0},a_{\rm lb}\sigma_{t})
remains in a single normal coordinate patch.
For
{\bm{y}}\in B_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{y}}_{0},a_{\rm lb}\sigma_{t})
,

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{D\vee\log n},

after decreasing
a_{\rm lb}
, because
m_{t}\leq 1
and
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
.
The density lower bound and the uniform atlas/Jacobian bounds therefore give

{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)=\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{{\mathcal{M}}}e^{-\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})}p_{0}({\bm{y}}){\mathrm{d}}\mathrm{vol}_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{y}})\geq cp_{\min}e^{-CC_{\varrho}^{2}(D\vee\log n)}\sigma_{t}^{-d}\mathrm{vol}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(B_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{y}}_{0},a_{\rm lb}\sigma_{t})).

The displayed small-ball volume lower bound gives

\mathrm{vol}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(B_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{y}}_{0},a_{\rm lb}\sigma_{t}))\geq C^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{-C}\sigma_{t}^{d}
.
This proves
Equation

63
with

c_{\rm lb}^{-1}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}
.

Step 2: bound the centered ratio.

The identity
s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)=\sigma_{t}^{-1}{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)/{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)
and

p_{t}({\bm{x}})=(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{-D/2}\sigma_{t}^{d}{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)

give the required bound on the score norm as follows.
Let
{\bm{Y}}\sim P_{0}
, put

{\bm{z}}({\bm{y}})=({\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}})/\sigma_{t}
, and define the posterior measure

{\mathrm{d}}\nu_{{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{y}})=\frac{\exp(-\|{\bm{z}}({\bm{y}})\|_{2}^{2}/2)}{\int_{\mathcal{M}}\exp(-\|{\bm{z}}({\bm{y}}^{\prime})\|_{2}^{2}/2){\mathrm{d}}P_{0}({\bm{y}}^{\prime})}{\mathrm{d}}P_{0}({\bm{y}}).

If
Z_{{\bm{x}},t}
denotes the denominator in this display, then

\sigma_{t}s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)=-\int_{\mathcal{M}}{\bm{z}}({\bm{y}}){\mathrm{d}}\nu_{{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{y}}).

Moreover

\mathrm{KL}(\nu_{{\bm{x}},t}\|P_{0})=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\|{\bm{z}}({\bm{y}})\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\nu_{{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{y}})-\log Z_{{\bm{x}},t}\geq 0,

and hence, by Jensen’s inequality,

\|\sigma_{t}s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\int_{\mathcal{M}}\|{\bm{z}}({\bm{y}})\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\nu_{{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{y}})\leq 2\log(Z_{{\bm{x}},t}^{-1}).

Since
Z_{{\bm{x}},t}=(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{D/2}p_{t}({\bm{x}})=\sigma_{t}^{d}{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)
, we obtain

\frac{\|{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)^{2}}=\sigma_{t}^{2}\|s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\log\!\left(\frac{(2\pi\sigma_{t}^{2})^{-D/2}}{p_{t}({\bm{x}})}\right)+1=2\log\!\left(\sigma_{t}^{-d}{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)^{-1}\right)+1.

Using
\sigma_{t}\geq\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}
,
{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)\geq q_{\rm lb}
, and enlarging
C_{\rm lb}
gives

Equation

64
.
∎

Theorem E.5

(Global tangent-cell large-noise score approximation)
.

Assume
1
,
2
and
3
.
Fix
0<\varepsilon_{\rm cell}\leq 1/2
and
0<\varepsilon_{\rm sc}\leq 1
.
Let
q_{\rm geom}
be as in
Equation

1
, and let
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}
be the finite geometry constant from
Definition

B.5
.
Let
r_{\rm cell}=\varepsilon_{\rm cell}^{1/\beta}
and

{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm lg}^{\rm cell}:=\{t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]:\sigma_{t}\geq c_{\rm lg}r_{\rm cell}\}.

Let
q_{\rm lb}
and
R_{\rm lb}
be defined in

Equations

63
and
64
, and set

C_{\rm den}:=q_{\rm lb}/8,\qquad\varepsilon_{\rm rel}:=c_{\rm glob}\frac{\varepsilon_{\rm sc}}{1+R_{\rm lb}},

H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb}:=1+\log\!\left(C_{\rm lg}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}B_{0}^{C_{\rm lg}}(D\vee\log n)^{C_{\rm lg}}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-d}C_{\rm den}^{-1}(1+R_{\rm lb})\varepsilon_{\rm sc}^{-1}\right).

After decreasing
c_{\rm lb}
, we assume
q_{\rm lb}\leq 1
, so

C_{\rm den}\in(0,1]
.
Choose the numerical constant
c_{\rm glob}\leq 1/32
, so that

\varepsilon_{\rm rel}\leq 1/16
and the separated tangent-cell proposition is applicable.
Assume the large-noise cell scale obeys the phase-smallness and fixed-order geometry
conditions, with the same sufficiently small numerical constant
c_{\rm ph,cell}
:

\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}r_{\rm cell}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb})^{1/2}\leq c_{\rm ph,cell},\qquad\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}r_{\rm cell}^{q_{\rm geom}-2}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb})^{C}\leq c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}.

(65)

Then there exists a ReLU network
\widetilde{s}_{\rm lb}^{\rm lg}
such that, uniformly over

t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm lg}^{\rm cell}
and
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
,

\sigma_{t}\|\widetilde{s}_{\rm lb}^{\rm lg}({\bm{x}},t)-s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm sc}.

(66)

Its depth
L
, maximum width
\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}
, sparsity
S
, and log-weight bound
\log B
satisfy

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb})^{C},

(67)

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb})^{C},

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq CD^{4(q_{\rm geom}+2)}(D\vee\log n)^{4(q_{\rm geom}+2)}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}\varepsilon_{\rm cell}^{-d/\beta}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb})^{C},

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\leq CD^{4(q_{\rm geom}+2)}(D\vee\log n)^{4(q_{\rm geom}+2)}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}\varepsilon_{\rm cell}^{-d/\beta}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb})^{C}.

In particular, the width and sparsity contain the intrinsic tangent-cell count

\varepsilon_{\rm cell}^{-d/\beta}
. In the slabwise application below, the fixed-order
geometry condition forces an accuracy-dependent cap on
\varepsilon_{\rm cell}
:

\varepsilon_{\rm cell}^{-d/\beta}=r_{k}^{-d}\leq C(\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+r_{\star,n}^{-d})
, and the

r_{\star,n}^{-d}
term is carried explicitly through the large-noise oracle and

{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
bounds.

Proof.

Step 1: approximate the unrefactored chart integrals.

Apply
Proposition

E.3
with
q_{\rm floor}=C_{\rm den}
and

\varepsilon_{\rm rel}
as above.
The definition of
H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb}
dominates the logarithmic scale

H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}
, and

Equation

65
implies

Equation

56
.
After summing over the fixed number of charts,

|\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)|\vee\max_{k}|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{k}({\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{U}}_{k}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq C\varepsilon_{\rm rel}\bigl({\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)+C_{\rm den}\bigr).

By
Equation

63
,
C_{\rm den}\leq{\mathcal{Q}}/8
, so after decreasing

c_{\rm glob}
,

|\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}-{\mathcal{Q}}|\vee\|\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-{\mathcal{U}}\|_{\infty}\leq c\varepsilon_{\rm rel}{\mathcal{Q}}.

(68)

Step 2: pass from integral approximation to ratio approximation.

The map
q\mapsto q\vee C_{\rm den}
is ReLU-realizable, and on the present domain

{\mathcal{Q}}\geq 8C_{\rm den}
.
Thus
\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}\vee C_{\rm den}\geq{\mathcal{Q}}/2
.
Using
Equations

64
and
68
,

\left\|\frac{\widehat{\mathcal{U}}}{\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}\vee C_{\rm den}}-\frac{{\mathcal{U}}}{{\mathcal{Q}}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C(1+R_{\rm lb})\varepsilon_{\rm rel}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm sc}/4.

The reciprocal network is applied only on the interval

[C_{\rm den},2M_{0}]
, where
M_{0}
is the uniform chart-integral bound from

Lemma

D.6
; the preceding approximation and
{\mathcal{Q}}\leq M_{0}
put

\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}\vee C_{\rm den}
in this interval after decreasing
c_{\rm glob}
.
Its accuracy is chosen

c\varepsilon_{\rm sc}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb})^{-C}

and its depth, sparsity, and log-weight are absorbed into the displayed powers of

H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb}
.
The final multiplication by
\sigma_{t}^{-1}
is implemented with the same arithmetic accuracy.
This proves
Equation

66
.

Step 3: record size.

The chart networks have the size in

Equation

58
with
H_{\rm lg}^{\rm dec}\leq H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb}
.
The reciprocal, products, clipping, and the final schedule multiplier add only fixed powers of

H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb}
and do not introduce any additional intrinsic net.
Therefore the width and sparsity scale as the cell count

N_{\rm cell}\leq C\varepsilon_{\rm cell}^{-d/\beta},

times the explicit ambient and logarithmic polynomial factor, giving

Equation

67
.
∎

Appendix F
Score Approximation in the Small-Noise Regime

With a positive density lower bound, the small-noise denominator can be controlled uniformly after de-Gaussianization.
This route uses the projection-centered Laplace expansion and the neural approximation of the nearest-point projection.
This section packages the whole small-noise approximation pipeline. It has four
submodules: active-chart geometry in
Section

F.1
, the
dimension-explicit projection-network primitive in

Section

F.2
, the projection-centered Laplace
expansion in
Section

F.3
, and the final network assembly in

Section

F.4
.

F.1
Proof architecture and active-chart geometry

Small-noise proof architecture.

The target result is
Corollary

F.29
.
The proof first localizes to active charts and discards inactive Gaussian tails.
It then uses reach to make the nearest projection single-valued, expands the de-Gaussianized chart integrals in projection-centered tangent coordinates, implements the projection coordinate by finite-anchor Gauss–Newton networks, and finally builds the stable ratio
\bar{\mathcal{U}}/\bar{\mathcal{Q}}
.
The main denominator step is
Lemma

F.27
; the main network-assembly step is
Lemma

F.28
, while
Proposition

F.25
records the local chart-level Laplace network on its certified active set.

F.1.1
Active-chart localization and inactive-tail bounds

The first task is to decide when a chart can matter.
The compact set
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
was fixed in
Equation

19
; it is the part of the manifold on which the
i
-th partition function is active.
If no point of
m_{t}{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
lies near
{\bm{x}}
, the Gaussian factor is uniformly small on the whole chart support, and the corresponding chart integral can be discarded at the target accuracy.

Let

C_{\rm tail}^{\max}:=1\vee 2B_{p}^{\rm proj}B^{\rm Jac}\max_{1\leq i\leq C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\sup_{0<\sigma\leq 1,\ {\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\sigma^{-d}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}\right){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}.

(69)

This constant is finite by the chart bi-Lipschitz bounds and the annular estimate used in the proof of
Lemma

F.1
; importantly, it is independent of
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}
.
Choose the default localization constant
c_{\star}\geq 1
large enough for the finite collection of inactive Gaussian-tail estimates used below, and also so that
c_{\star}\geq 2C_{\varrho}
.
More precisely, whenever a bound below has the form

C_{\rm tail}^{\max}C\bigl(1+\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{M}\varepsilon^{c_{\star}^{2}/C},\qquad 0<\varepsilon\leq 1/2,

with one of the finitely many constants
C,M
arising from the fixed atlas, amplitudes, and retained Laplace monomials, our choice of
c_{\star}
makes this quantity at most
\varepsilon/12
.
The same choice is also large enough that, for every
H\geq 1
, the
H
-localized
de-Gaussianized inactive-tail bounds below have the form

C_{\rm tail}^{\max}C(1+H)^{M}e^{-cc_{\star}^{2}H}\leq e^{-H}

for the finite collection of constants
C,M,c
used there.
Such a choice is possible because
(1+\log(e/\varepsilon))^{M}\varepsilon^{\gamma}\leq C_{\gamma,M}\varepsilon
on
(0,1/2]
for all sufficiently large
\gamma
, and because, for each fixed triple
C,M,c
,

\sup_{H\geq 1}C_{\rm tail}^{\max}C(1+H)^{M}\exp\{-(cc_{\star}^{2}-1)H\}\to 0\qquad\text{as }c_{\star}\to\infty.

The collection of such triples is finite, so one
c_{\star}
satisfies all of them.
No lower-noise prefactor appears in the annular tail estimate.
For any auxiliary radius multiplier
c_{a}>0
and any
0<\varepsilon<1
, define the parameterized active region

{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon):=\bigl\{({\bm{x}},t):t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}],\ {\bm{x}}\in A_{t},\exists{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\text{ s.t. }\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq c_{a}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\varepsilon^{-1}}\bigr\}.

(70)

The default active set is

{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon):={\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{\star})}(\varepsilon).

(71)

Thus
c_{\star}
is used for the default active set throughout;
c_{a}
only denotes a temporary enlargement factor in statements involving
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
.

Lemma F.1

(Inactive-chart Gaussian tail bounds for
{\mathcal{P}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
)
.

Fix any chart
i\in\{1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\}
and every
0<\varepsilon<1
.
Let
c_{a}>0
,
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
, and
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
.
If
({\bm{x}},t)\notin{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
, then for every
{\bm{u}}\in\phi_{i}(U_{i})
with
\bar{\rho}_{i}({\bm{u}})\neq 0
,

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}>c_{a}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\varepsilon^{-1}}.

(72)

Consequently, for a constant
C_{\rm tail}
depending only on the fixed atlas, amplitude bounds,
d
, and the schedule bound
\underline{m}
, but not on
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}
,

|{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)|+\max_{1\leq k\leq D}|{\mathcal{P}}_{i,k}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq C_{\rm tail}\bigl(1+c_{a}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}\bigr)^{d}\varepsilon^{c_{a}^{2}/8}.

(73)

Proof of
Lemma

F.1
.

If
\bar{\rho}_{i}({\bm{u}})\neq 0
, then
{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
.
Failure of membership in
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
means that no
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
satisfies the active distance condition.
This gives
Equation

72
.

Put
R:=c_{a}\sqrt{\log\varepsilon^{-1}}
.
The chart maps are uniformly bi-Lipschitz on their compact supports and
m_{t}\geq\underline{m}>0
.
Hence there is a constant
C_{i}
, independent of
t
,
\sigma_{t}
, and
{\bm{x}}
, such that for every
r>0
,

\mathrm{vol}_{d}\{{\bm{u}}\in\phi_{i}(U_{i}):\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}\leq r\}\leq C_{i}(1+r/\sigma_{t})^{d}\sigma_{t}^{d}.

(74)

For
r\leq 1
, this follows from the local bi-Lipschitz bounds after covering
m_{t}{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
by a bounded number of coordinate balls; for
r>1
, the right side dominates the fixed chart volume after increasing
C_{i}
.

Decompose the support into annuli

A_{\ell}:=\{{\bm{u}}:\ R+\ell<\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}/\sigma_{t}\leq R+\ell+1\},\qquad\ell=0,1,2,\dots.

By
Equation

74
,
\mathrm{vol}_{d}(A_{\ell})\leq C_{i}\sigma_{t}^{d}(1+R+\ell)^{d}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\ind\{\bar{\rho}_{i}({\bm{u}})\neq 0\}{\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{i}\sum_{\ell\geq 0}(1+R+\ell)^{d}e^{-(R+\ell)^{2}/2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{i}^{\prime}(1+R)^{d}e^{-R^{2}/8}.

The last inequality is the standard Gaussian-annulus summation bound; it follows, for instance, by comparing the sum with
e^{-R^{2}/8}\sum_{\ell\geq 0}(1+\ell)^{d}e^{-\ell^{2}/8}
.
Since
|a_{i,k}^{({\mathcal{P}})}|\leq|a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}|\leq B_{p}^{\rm proj}B^{\rm Jac}
by
Equation

31
and
\|{\bm{z}}_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, the same estimate applies to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
and to every coordinate of
{\mathcal{P}}_{i}
.
Because
e^{-R^{2}/8}=\varepsilon^{c_{a}^{2}/8}
and
1+R\leq 1+c_{a}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}
, we obtain
Equation

73
.
∎

By the fixed choice of
c_{\star}
, the bound in
Equation

73
with
c_{a}=c_{\star}
, and also with any larger fixed multiplier, is below the allocated
{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon)
tail budget after multiplication by the finitely many logarithmic factors used later.
Consequently, the projection–Laplace approximation only has to be constructed on the active region
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
.
It is used only during the
small-noise regime

t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
, where
\sigma_{t}\lesssim\varepsilon^{1/\beta}
.

F.1.2
Projection geometry on active small-noise charts

The next lemmas turn the active-distance condition into usable local coordinates.
The buffered boxes keep all projected active points away from chart boundaries, while the reach-tube estimates ensure that the nearest-point projection and the projection-centered objective have stable curvature.
These facts are the analytic foundation for both the Laplace expansion and the neural Gauss–Newton projection network.

Buffered coordinate set.

The compact support
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
and buffered boxes
K_{i}^{\circ}\Subset\Box_{i}
were fixed in
Equations

19
,
20
and
21
.
Since
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
is compact and
\phi_{i}({\mathcal{S}}_{i})\subset\operatorname{int}(K_{i}^{\circ})
, we can define the strictly positive geometric buffer

B_{i}^{\rm buff}:=1\wedge\mathrm{dist}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\Bigl({\mathcal{S}}_{i},\,{\mathcal{M}}\setminus\phi_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\operatorname{int}(K_{i}^{\circ})\bigr)\Bigr)>0.

(75)

with the convention
\mathrm{dist}(A,\emptyset)=+\infty
.

Auxiliary compact chart domains.

Set

d_{i}^{\Box}:=\mathrm{dist}(K_{i}^{\circ},\partial\Box_{i})>0.

(76)

On this single compact set
\Box_{i}
, define

\displaystyle L_{i}^{(+)}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}}\|{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{\rm op},\qquad L_{i}^{(-)}:=\Bigl(\sup_{{\bm{y}}\in\phi_{i}^{-1}(\Box_{i})}\|{\mathrm{D}}\phi_{i}({\bm{y}})\|_{\rm op}\Bigr)^{-1},

(77)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}^{(-)}

\displaystyle:=\inf_{{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}}\lambda_{\min}\bigl({\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\bigr),\qquad\lambda_{i}^{(+)}:=\sup_{{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}}\lambda_{\max}\bigl({\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\bigr),

(78)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{M}}_{i}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=1\vee\sup_{{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{D}\|\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}})\|_{\rm op}.

(79)

Since
{\bm{z}}_{i}=\phi_{i}^{-1}
is smooth on
\phi_{i}(U_{i})
and
\Box_{i}\Subset\phi_{i}(U_{i})
, all these constants are finite.
Moreover, because
{\bm{z}}_{i}
is an immersion,
{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})={\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{\top}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})
is symmetric positive definite for every
{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}
.
Hence

0<L_{i}^{(-)}\leq L_{i}^{(+)}<\infty,\qquad 0<\lambda_{i}^{(-)}\leq\lambda_{i}^{(+)}<\infty.

Define

{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}}):={\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{-1}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{\top},\qquad{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}.

(80)

Again, because
{\bm{z}}_{i}
is smooth on
\Box_{i}
and
{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})
is uniformly invertible there,
{\bm{A}}_{i}
is smooth on
\Box_{i}
.
Hence

L_{A,i}:=1\vee\sup_{{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}}\|{\mathrm{D}}{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{\rm op}<\infty.

(81)

Lemma F.2

(Uniform chart geometry on compact coordinate patches)
.

For each chart index
i
, for all
{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}\in\Box_{i}
,

L_{i}^{(-)}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}}\|_{2}\leq\|{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{v}})\|_{2}\leq L_{i}^{(+)}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}}\|_{2},

(82)

and for all
{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}
,

\lambda_{i}^{(-)}{\bm{I}}_{d}\preceq{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\preceq\lambda_{i}^{(+)}{\bm{I}}_{d}.

(83)

Proof.

For the upper Lipschitz bound, by the fundamental theorem of calculus,

\displaystyle{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{v}})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}{\bm{J}}_{i}\bigl({\bm{v}}+s({\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}})\bigr)({\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}}){\mathrm{d}}s.

Since
\Box_{i}
is convex,
{\bm{v}}+\theta({\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}})\in\Box_{i}
for every
\theta\in[0,1]
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\|{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{v}})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\|{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{v}}+\theta({\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}}))\|_{\rm op}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}}\|_{2}{\mathrm{d}}\theta

\displaystyle\leq L_{i}^{(+)}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}}\|_{2}.

For the lower Lipschitz bound, apply the same argument to the inverse map
\phi_{i}
on the compact set
\phi_{i}^{-1}(\Box_{i})\subset U_{i}
:

\displaystyle\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|\phi_{i}({\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}))-\phi_{i}({\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{v}}))\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{{\bm{y}}\in\phi_{i}^{-1}(\Box_{i})}\|{\mathrm{D}}\phi_{i}({\bm{y}})\|_{\rm op}\|{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{v}})\|_{2}.

By the definition of
L_{i}^{(-)}
, this gives

L_{i}^{(-)}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}}\|_{2}\leq\|{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{v}})\|_{2}.

Finally,
{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})
