is continuous and symmetric positive definite on the compact set
\Box_{i}
.
Hence its smallest and largest eigenvalues attain finite extrema on
\Box_{i}
, and the definitions of
\lambda_{i}^{(-)}
and
\lambda_{i}^{(+)}
give
Equation

83
.
∎

Lemma F.3

(Finite-order geometry from compact smoothness)
.

Assume
1
.
Fix an integer
q\geq 1
.
For the finite projection atlas
\{(U_{i},\phi_{i})\}_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}
, the partition of unity
\{\rho_{i}\}
, the compact coordinate boxes
\Box_{i}
, and the compact tubular domains used in the construction, there exists a finite constant
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q}\geq e
such that, uniformly over the chart index
i
,

\displaystyle\max_{|{\bm{\alpha}}|\leq q+4}\|\partial^{{\bm{\alpha}}}{\bm{z}}_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Box_{i})}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q},

(84)

\displaystyle\max_{|{\bm{\alpha}}|\leq q+4}\|\partial^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\bar{\rho}_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Box_{i})}+\max_{|{\bm{\alpha}}|\leq q+4}\|\partial^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Box_{i})}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q}.

(85)

After enlarging
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q}
, the same bound holds for transition maps between overlapping projection charts, for
{\bm{A}}_{i}={\bm{G}}_{i}^{-1}{\bm{J}}_{i}^{\top}
, for the nearest-projection coordinate maps

{\bm{x}}\mapsto\phi_{i}(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}))

on the compact reach tubes where they are used, and for the corresponding projection residual maps.
Schedule-dependent maps such as
({\bm{x}},t)\mapsto\phi_{i}(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t}))
are controlled by composing these geometric maps with the separately approximated schedule
m_{t}^{-1}
; their constants therefore depend on
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q}
and the fixed lower bound
\underline{m}
, not on any new geometry assumption.
The constant can also be chosen to dominate the finite atlas cardinality, fixed coordinate buffers, tube constants, chart-volume constants, and their reciprocals.
With
\mathcal{K}_{{\mathcal{M}},q}
denoting the high-order atlas envelope from

Definition

B.5
, it may be chosen so that

\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q}\leq C_{d,q}(1\vee S_{{\mathcal{M}}})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}(q)}(1\vee\kappa^{-1})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}(q)}\mathcal{K}_{{\mathcal{M}},q}^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}(q)},

where
C_{d,q}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}(q)
depend only on
d
and
q
.

Proof.

For each
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{M}}
, let
{\bm{V}}_{\bm{y}}
be an orthonormal frame for
T_{\bm{y}}{\mathcal{M}}
.
The map
\phi_{\bm{y}}({\bm{z}})={\bm{V}}_{\bm{y}}^{\top}({\bm{z}}-{\bm{y}})
, restricted to
{\mathcal{M}}
, has differential equal to the identity at
{\bm{y}}
.
The inverse function theorem therefore gives a
C^{\infty}
projection chart near
{\bm{y}}
.
Compactness of
{\mathcal{M}}
gives a finite subcover, and the construction in
Section

D.3.1
shrinks these charts to compact coordinate boxes
\Box_{i}\Subset\phi_{i}(U_{i})
.

Each inverse chart
{\bm{z}}_{i}=\phi_{i}^{-1}
is
C^{\infty}
.
Hence every partial derivative
\partial^{{\bm{\alpha}}}{\bm{z}}_{i}
,
|{\bm{\alpha}}|\leq q+4
, is continuous on the compact set
\Box_{i}
and is bounded.
Taking the maximum over the finitely many charts proves
Equation

84
.
The functions
\bar{\rho}_{i}
are smooth because the partition of unity is smooth, and
\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i}}
is smooth because
{\bm{G}}_{i}={\bm{J}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{J}}_{i}
is smooth and uniformly positive definite on
\Box_{i}
.
Their derivatives up to order
q+4
are therefore bounded on compact boxes, proving
Equation

85
after increasing
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q}
.

Transition maps and
{\bm{A}}_{i}={\bm{G}}_{i}^{-1}{\bm{J}}_{i}^{\top}
are compositions, products, and matrix inverses of smooth maps on compact sets where the relevant determinants are bounded away from zero, so their derivatives up to order
q+4
are bounded.
Since
{\mathcal{M}}
has positive reach
\kappa
, the nearest projection
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}
is
C^{\infty}
on every closed tube of radius strictly smaller than
\kappa
.
The construction only evaluates
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}
on such compact tubes.
Therefore
{\bm{x}}\mapsto\phi_{i}(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}))
and the associated residual maps have bounded derivatives up to order
q+4
on those tubes.
If the argument is
{\bm{x}}/m_{t}
, the chain rule only adds fixed powers of
m_{t}^{-1}
, which are bounded by
\underline{m}^{-1}
on
[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
.
The atlas cardinality and tube constant obey the reach-volume bounds

C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\leq C_{d}S_{{\mathcal{M}}}(1\vee\kappa^{-1})^{d},\qquad C_{\rm tube}\leq C_{d}(1\vee S_{{\mathcal{M}}})(1\vee\kappa^{-1})^{d}.

All remaining auxiliary constants are among the finite
C^{q+4}
-norms and buffer
reciprocals dominated by
\mathcal{K}_{{\mathcal{M}},q}
, or are obtained from them by finitely many
products, compositions, and matrix inverses of dimension at most
d
.
Enlarging
C_{d,q}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}(q)
gives the displayed explicit
bound for
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q}
, and completes the proof.
∎

Let

\delta_{\rm act}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon,t):=c_{a}\frac{\sigma_{t}}{m_{t}}\sqrt{\log\varepsilon^{-1}},\qquad{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t):=\frac{{\bm{x}}}{m_{t}}.

(86)

We write

\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t):=\delta_{\rm act}^{(c_{\star})}(\varepsilon,t)

(87)

Since the atlas is finite, define the uniform projection and Gauss–Newton margin

\eta_{\Pi}:=\min_{1\leq i\leq C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\min\Bigl\{\kappa,\frac{1}{2}B_{i}^{\rm buff},\frac{\lambda_{i}^{(-)}}{{\mathsf{M}}_{i}^{(2)}},\frac{1}{2L_{A,i}}\Bigr\}>0.

(88)

All projection-centered arguments below are used only for
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
.
Choose
\varepsilon>0
small enough that, for every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
,

\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t)<\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\Pi}.

(89)

For a fixed
c_{a}>0
, the corresponding
c_{a}
-active localization condition is the same display with
\delta_{\rm act}^{(c_{a})}
in place of
\delta_{\rm act}
.

For the Laplace localization argument, define the compact ambient set

\mathcal{X}_{i}:=\{{\bm{\zeta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}:\mathrm{dist}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}({\bm{\zeta}},{\mathcal{S}}_{i})\leq\eta_{\Pi}/2\}.

(90)

Since
{\bm{z}}_{i}
is smooth on
\Box_{i}
and
\mathcal{X}_{i}
is compact, there is a finite constant
H_{i}\geq 1
such that, for
\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{\zeta}}):=\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{\zeta}}-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}
,

\bigl\|\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{\zeta}})-\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}\Phi_{i}({\bm{v}};{\bm{\zeta}})\bigr\|_{\rm op}\leq H_{i}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{v}}\|_{2}

(91)

for all
{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}\in\Box_{i}
and
{\bm{\zeta}}\in\mathcal{X}_{i}
.
Define

r_{i}:=\min\Bigl\{d_{i}^{\Box}/2,\,\lambda_{i}^{(-)}/[4(1+H_{i})]\Bigr\}>0.

(92)

For each chart
i
, define the fixed chart-geometry constant

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{i}

\displaystyle:=1\vee L_{i}^{(+)}\vee(L_{i}^{(-)})^{-1}\vee\lambda_{i}^{(+)}\vee(\lambda_{i}^{(-)})^{-1}\vee{\mathsf{M}}_{i}^{(2)}\vee L_{A,i}\vee H_{i}\vee(d_{i}^{\Box})^{-1}\vee r_{i}^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\vee\max_{1\leq\ell\leq D}\|{\bm{z}}_{i,\ell}\|_{C^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+3}(\Box_{i})}\vee\|{\bm{G}}_{i}\|_{C^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2}(\Box_{i})}\vee\|{\bm{G}}_{i}^{-1}\|_{C^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2}(\Box_{i})}.

Since
\Box_{i}\Subset\phi_{i}(U_{i})
and
{\bm{z}}_{i}
is smooth, this quantity is finite.
Since the atlas is finite, set

\mathfrak{C}_{{\mathcal{M}}}:=\max_{1\leq i\leq C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}\mathfrak{C}_{i}<\infty.

(93)

Lemma F.4

(Nearest-point projection on the active small-noise chart)
.

Assume
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and
Equation

89
.
If
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, then the global nearest-point projection
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))
is well-defined and unique.
Moreover,
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))\in U_{i}
, and

{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t):=\phi_{i}\bigl(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))\bigr)\in K_{i}^{\circ}.

(94)

Furthermore, with

{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t):={\bm{x}}-m_{t}\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)),

we have the orthogonality relation

{\bm{J}}_{i}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr)^{\top}{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=0.

(95)

Proof.

Write
{\bm{\xi}}={\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)
.
Since
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, there exists
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
such that

\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t).

(96)

Because
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{M}}
, we have

\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}},{\mathcal{M}})\leq\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t)<\kappa.

(97)

Thus
{\bm{\xi}}
lies inside the reach tube of
{\mathcal{M}}
, so the nearest-point projection
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})
is well-defined and unique.

Next, since
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})
is the closest point on
{\mathcal{M}}
to
{\bm{\xi}}
,

\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}=\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}},{\mathcal{M}})\leq\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})-{\bm{\xi}}\|_{2}+\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}},{\mathcal{M}})+\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq 2\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t)<\frac{1}{2}B_{i}^{\rm buff}.

Hence

\mathrm{dist}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}),{\mathcal{S}}_{i})<\frac{1}{2}B_{i}^{\rm buff}.

By the definition of
B_{i}^{\rm buff}
, this implies
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\in\phi_{i}^{-1}(\operatorname{int}(K_{i}^{\circ}))
.
Hence

{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)=\phi_{i}(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}))\in K_{i}^{\circ}.

In particular,
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\in U_{i}
.

It remains to prove the orthogonality statement.
Since
{\bm{\xi}}
lies inside the reach tube, the metric projection residual is normal to the tangent space:

{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\perp T_{\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})}{\mathcal{M}}.

The columns of
{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})=\nabla{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})
form a basis of this tangent space.
Therefore,

{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})^{\top}\bigl({\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\bigr)=0.

Since
{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)={\bm{x}}-m_{t}\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})=m_{t}\bigl({\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\bigr)
, multiplying the previous display by
m_{t}
gives

{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})^{\top}{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=0.

∎

For
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, define

\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t):=\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2},\qquad{\bm{g}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t):=\nabla_{\bm{u}}\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t).

(98)

Since

\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{D}\bigl(\xi_{\ell}({\bm{x}},t)-z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}})\bigr)^{2},

we have

{\bm{g}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)={\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{\top}\bigl({\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})-{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr).

(99)

Differentiating once more gives

\nabla_{\bm{u}}{\bm{g}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)={\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})-\sum_{\ell=1}^{D}\bigl(\xi_{\ell}({\bm{x}},t)-z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}})\bigr)\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}).

(100)

At
{\bm{u}}={\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)
, we have
{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t))=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))
.
Hence, using
Equation

95
,

\displaystyle{\bm{g}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t);{\bm{x}},t)

\displaystyle={\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})^{\top}\bigl(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))-{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr)

\displaystyle=-m_{t}^{-1}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})^{\top}{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=0.

(101)

Lemma F.5

(Uniform invertibility near the projection)
.

Assume
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and
Equation

89
.
If
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, then

\nabla_{\bm{u}}{\bm{g}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t);{\bm{x}},t)\succeq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{i}^{(-)}{\bm{I}}_{d}.

(102)

Proof.

Write
{\bm{\xi}}={\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)
and
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}={\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)
.
By
Lemma

F.4
,

{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}\in K_{i}^{\circ}\subset\Box_{i},\qquad{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}).

Fix any
{\bm{w}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
By
Equation

100
,

{\bm{w}}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{u}}{\bm{g}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi};{\bm{x}},t){\bm{w}}={\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{D}\bigl(\xi_{\ell}-z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})\bigr){\bm{w}}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}.

(103)

Because
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}\in\Box_{i}
,
Equation

83
gives
{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}\geq\lambda_{i}^{(-)}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}
.
For the perturbation term, use
{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})
and the definition of
{\mathsf{M}}_{i}^{(2)}
:

\displaystyle\Biggl|\sum_{\ell=1}^{D}\bigl(\xi_{\ell}-z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})\bigr){\bm{w}}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}\Biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{D}|\xi_{\ell}-z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})|\|\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})\|_{\rm op}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{D}\|\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}z_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})\|_{\rm op}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}},{\mathcal{M}})\,{\mathsf{M}}_{i}^{(2)}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}.

(104)

Since
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, there exists
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\subset{\mathcal{M}}
such that
\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t)
.
Therefore
\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}},{\mathcal{M}})\leq\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t)
.
By
Equations

89
and
88
,

\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t){\mathsf{M}}_{i}^{(2)}<\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{i}^{(-)}.

Combining this with
Equations

103
and
104
and
{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}\geq\lambda_{i}^{(-)}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}
, we obtain

{\bm{w}}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{u}}{\bm{g}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi};{\bm{x}},t){\bm{w}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{i}^{(-)}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Since this holds for every
{\bm{w}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, the matrix inequality

\nabla_{\bm{u}}{\bm{g}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi};{\bm{x}},t)\succeq\lambda_{i}^{(-)}{\bm{I}}_{d}/2

∎

F.2
Finite-anchor projection networks

This section isolates the neural implementation of the nearest-point projection.
It is the only nonlinear geometric approximation step that could otherwise create a
hidden ambient-dimensional cost. The construction works strictly in intrinsic chart
coordinates: finitely many anchors initialize local Gauss–Newton iterations, and a
ReLU objective gate selects near-minimizing candidates. Later network-assembly sections
reuse these projection-coordinate networks together with standard schedule, product,
reciprocal, and exponential subnetworks.

Throughout this section, every construction involving
m_{t}
,
\sigma_{t}
, or powers of their reciprocals uses
Lemma

I.2
and the derived schedule lemmas stated below.

For orientation, the projection-centered network assembly uses the following objects in
this order:

(1)

{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)={\bm{x}}/m_{t}
,

(2)

the global nearest-point projection
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))
,

(3)

the local coordinate
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)=\phi_{i}\bigl(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))\bigr)
,

(4)

the normalized residual
{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=\sigma_{t}^{-1}{\bm{r}}_{i}=\sigma_{t}^{-1}\bigl({\bm{x}}-m_{t}\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))\bigr)
,

(5)

the Gaussian factor
\exp(-\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}/2)
,

(6)

and the coefficient functions
A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),t\bigr)
.

The projection-coordinate networks are the only part that uses the manifold geometry in a substantial way.
Once the projection center is available, the residual, Gaussian factor, and Laplace coefficients are obtained by combining standard schedule, Hölder, multiplication, reciprocal, and exponential subnetworks with carefully allocated errors.

In the following, we construct subnetworks to approximate the components of
Equation

188
:

I.2

t\mapsto\sigma_{t}^{k}

F.25

active chart expansion for
{\mathcal{P}}_{i}

F.2.1
Gauss–Newton networks for nearest-point projection

We approximate the nearest-point projection in local intrinsic coordinates.
The nonlinear unknown is the chart coordinate
{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, whereas the ambient variable enters the iteration only through
{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)={\bm{x}}/m_{t}
and matrix-vector operations.
The lemmas in this block first prove a stable intrinsic Gauss–Newton step, then initialize it from finitely many anchors, and finally combine all chart-anchor candidates by an objective gate.
This produces coordinate-valued and ambient-valued projection networks without ever applying a chart map to an approximate ambient point.

We define the coordinate-wise clipping map:

\Pi_{i}^{(\rm box)}({\bm{u}}):=\bigl(a_{i,j}^{\Box}+{\mathsf{ReLU}}(u_{j}-a_{i,j}^{\Box})-{\mathsf{ReLU}}(u_{j}-b_{i,j}^{\Box})\bigr)_{j=1}^{d}.

(105)

\Pi_{i}^{(\rm box)}
explicitly translates standard
\min/\max
bounding operations into exact ReLU arithmetic.
Consequently,
\Pi_{i}^{(\rm box)}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to\Box_{i}
is exactly representable by a shallow ReLU network without any approximation error.
Furthermore, because it is a projection onto a convex set, it is inherently
1
-Lipschitz.

Lemma F.6

(Local Gauss–Newton contraction for nearest-point projection)
.

Define the intrinsic Gauss–Newton map

{\mathcal{T}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t):=\Pi_{i}^{({\rm box})}\bigl({\bm{u}}+{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\bigl({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\bigr)\bigr),\qquad\text{where }{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})={\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{-1}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{\top}.

(106)

Set

r_{i}^{\rm GN}:=\min\Bigl\{1,\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{dist}(K_{i}^{\circ},\partial\Box_{i}),\frac{1}{4L_{A,i}L_{i}^{(+)}}\Bigr\}.

(107)

Assume
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
,
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, and
Equation

89
.
Then

\|{\mathcal{T}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}

(108)

whenever

{\bm{u}}\in{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),r_{i}^{\rm GN})\cap\Box_{i}.

Proof.

Write
{\bm{\xi}}:={\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t),{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}:={\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)
.
Before clipping, define

\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t):={\bm{u}}+{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\bigl({\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\bigr).

Since

{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}}){\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})={\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{-1}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{\top}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})={\bm{I}}_{d},

differentiating with respect to
{\bm{u}}
gives, for every
{\bm{h}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{D}}_{\bm{u}}\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)[{\bm{h}}]

\displaystyle={\bm{h}}+{\mathrm{D}}{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})[{\bm{h}}]\bigl({\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\bigr)-{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}}){\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}){\bm{h}}

\displaystyle={\mathrm{D}}{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})[{\bm{h}}]\bigl({\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\bigr).

(109)

By
Lemma

F.4
,

{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}),\qquad{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})^{\top}\bigl({\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\bigr)=0.

Therefore

{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})\bigl({\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})\bigr)={\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})^{-1}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})^{\top}\bigl({\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\bigr)=0,

and hence

\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi};{\bm{x}},t)={\bm{u}}^{\Pi}.

(110)

Since
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\in K_{i}^{\circ}\subset\Box_{i}
, the clipping map fixes
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}
:

\Pi_{i}^{({\rm box})}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})={\bm{u}}^{\Pi}.

(111)

Now let
{\bm{u}}\in{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},r_{i}^{\rm GN})\cap\Box_{i}
.
For
s\in[0,1]
, set

{\bm{u}}_{s}:={\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+s({\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}).

Because
\Box_{i}
is convex and both
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}
, we have
{\bm{u}}_{s}\in\Box_{i}
.
Also,

\|{\bm{u}}_{s}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}\leq\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}\leq r_{i}^{\rm GN}.

Using
Equation

89
, the definition of
\eta_{\Pi}
, and the fact that
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, we have

\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}},{\mathcal{M}})\leq\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t)<\frac{1}{4L_{A,i}}.

(112)

Therefore, by the chart Lipschitz bound
Equation

82
,

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{s})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}+\|\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{s})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}},{\mathcal{M}})+\|{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{s})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t)+L_{i}^{(+)}\|{\bm{u}}_{s}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{4L_{A,i}}+L_{i}^{(+)}r_{i}^{\rm GN}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4L_{A,i}}+\frac{1}{4L_{A,i}}=\frac{1}{2L_{A,i}}.

(113)

Combining
Equations

109
,
81
and
113
, we get

\|{\mathrm{D}}_{\bm{u}}\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{s};{\bm{x}},t)\|_{\rm op}\leq L_{A,i}\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{s})\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

By the fundamental theorem of calculus and
Equation

110
,

\displaystyle\bigl\|\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)-\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi};{\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\|{\mathrm{D}}_{\bm{u}}\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{s};{\bm{x}},t)\|_{\rm op}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}.

Finally,
\Pi_{i}^{({\rm box})}
is
1
-Lipschitz and fixes
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{T}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\Pi_{i}^{({\rm box})}(\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t))-\Pi_{i}^{({\rm box})}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\bigr\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}.

∎

Lemma F.7

(ReLU anchor networks for projection initialization)
.

For every chart
i
, there exists a finite set

{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\rm anc}=\{{\bm{a}}_{i,1},\dots,{\bm{a}}_{i,N_{i}}\}\subset K_{i}^{\circ}

depending only on the fixed chart geometry such that

\forall{\bm{u}}\in K_{i}^{\circ},\qquad\min_{{\bm{a}}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\rm anc}}\|{\bm{a}}-{\bm{u}}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}r_{i}^{\rm GN}.

(114)

Moreover, writing
\ell_{i,r}^{\circ}:=b_{i,r}^{\circ}-a_{i,r}^{\circ}
,

N_{i}\leq\prod_{r=1}^{d}\left(1+\left\lceil\frac{\sqrt{d}\,\ell_{i,r}^{\circ}}{r_{i}^{\rm GN}}\right\rceil\right)\leq\prod_{r=1}^{d}\left(2+\frac{\sqrt{d}\,\ell_{i,r}^{\circ}}{r_{i}^{\rm GN}}\right)\leq C_{i}^{\rm anc}(r_{i}^{\rm GN})^{-d},\qquad C_{i}^{\rm anc}:=\prod_{r=1}^{d}\left(2+\sqrt{d}\,\ell_{i,r}^{\circ}\right).

(115)

In particular,
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\rm anc}
is fixed once and for all and is independent of
\varepsilon
,
\varepsilon_{\Pi}
, and
({\bm{x}},t)
.

Proof.

For each coordinate
r
, let
m_{i,r}:=\left\lceil\sqrt{d}\,\ell_{i,r}^{\circ}/r_{i}^{\rm GN}\right\rceil
.
Choose the tensor-product grid

{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\rm anc}:=\prod_{r=1}^{d}\left\{a_{i,r}^{\circ}+\frac{k_{r}}{m_{i,r}}\ell_{i,r}^{\circ}:k_{r}=0,\dots,m_{i,r}\right\}\subset K_{i}^{\circ}.

For any
{\bm{u}}\in K_{i}^{\circ}
, choose the nearest grid point in each coordinate.
The coordinatewise error is at most
\ell_{i,r}^{\circ}/(2m_{i,r})\leq r_{i}^{\rm GN}/(2\sqrt{d})
, and therefore the Euclidean error is at most
r_{i}^{\rm GN}/2
.
This proves
Equation

114
.
The grid cardinality is

|{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\rm anc}|=\prod_{r=1}^{d}(m_{i,r}+1)=\prod_{r=1}^{d}\left(1+\left\lceil\frac{\sqrt{d}\,\ell_{i,r}^{\circ}}{r_{i}^{\rm GN}}\right\rceil\right),

and the final inequality in
Equation

115
follows from
1+\lceil x\rceil\leq 2+x
, together with
r_{i}^{\rm GN}\leq 1
.
∎

Define the aggregate anchor complexity

A_{{\mathcal{M}}}:=\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}|{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\rm anc}|.

(116)

By
Equation

115
,

A_{{\mathcal{M}}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}C_{i}^{\rm anc}(r_{i}^{\rm GN})^{-d}.

(117)

Lemma F.8

(Error propagation for anchored approximate Gauss–Newton iteration)
.

Assume
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
,
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, and
Equation

89
.
Assume
{\bm{a}}\in K_{i}^{\circ}
satisfies

\|{\bm{a}}-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}r_{i}^{\rm GN}.

(118)

Let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}
be a
\Box_{i}
-valued approximate Gauss–Newton step satisfying

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i},\;t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon),\;({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)\end{subarray}}\bigl\|\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{T}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm step}.

(119)

Assume moreover that

\varepsilon_{\rm step}\leq\frac{r_{i}^{\rm GN}}{8\sqrt{d}}.

(120)

Define the approximate iterates by

\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{0}:={\bm{a}},\qquad\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k+1}:=\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}(\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k};{\bm{x}},t).

(121)

Then, every iterate stays in the Gauss–Newton contraction basin:

\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k}\in{\mathcal{B}}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),r_{i}^{\rm GN}\bigr)\cap\Box_{i},\qquad k\geq 0.

(122)

Moreover, for every
K\geq 0
,

\bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{K}-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{2}\leq 2^{-(K+1)}r_{i}^{\rm GN}+2\sqrt{d}\varepsilon_{\rm step}.

(123)

Proof.

Write
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}:={\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),e_{k}:=\|\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}
.
By the anchor assumption
Equation

118
,

e_{0}=\|{\bm{a}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}r_{i}^{\rm GN}.

We first prove that the iterates stay inside the contraction basin.
Assume inductively that

e_{k}\leq\frac{3}{4}r_{i}^{\rm GN}.

Then
\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k}\in{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},r_{i}^{\rm GN})\cap\Box_{i}
, so the exact Gauss–Newton contraction lemma (
Lemma

F.6
) applies:

\|{\mathcal{T}}_{i}(\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k};{\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}e_{k}.

Using the approximate-step error and the norm comparison
\|{\bm{v}}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{d}\,\|{\bm{v}}\|_{\infty}
, we obtain

\displaystyle e_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\|\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}(\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k};{\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}(\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k};{\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{T}}_{i}(\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k};{\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{2}+\|{\mathcal{T}}_{i}(\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k};{\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}\varepsilon_{\rm step}+\frac{1}{2}e_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}\varepsilon_{\rm step}+\frac{3}{8}r_{i}^{\rm GN}.

By
Equation

120
,

\sqrt{d}\,\varepsilon_{\rm step}\leq\frac{1}{8}r_{i}^{\rm GN}.

Therefore

e_{k+1}\leq\frac{1}{8}r_{i}^{\rm GN}+\frac{3}{8}r_{i}^{\rm GN}=\frac{1}{2}r_{i}^{\rm GN}<\frac{3}{4}r_{i}^{\rm GN}.

Since
e_{0}\leq r_{i}^{\rm GN}/2
, induction proves

e_{k}\leq\frac{3}{4}r_{i}^{\rm GN}<r_{i}^{\rm GN}\qquad\text{for all }k\geq 0.

Also, because the approximate step
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i}
is assumed to be
\Box_{i}
-valued, every
\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k}
lies in
\Box_{i}
.
Hence
Equation

122
holds.

Now that the exact contraction applies at every step, we have the recursion

e_{k+1}\leq\frac{1}{2}e_{k}+\sqrt{d}\,\varepsilon_{\rm step}.

(124)

Iterating
Equation

124
gives

\displaystyle e_{K}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-K}e_{0}+\sqrt{d}\,\varepsilon_{\rm step}\sum_{j=0}^{K-1}2^{-j}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-(K+1)}r_{i}^{\rm GN}+2\sqrt{d}\,\varepsilon_{\rm step}.

∎

Lemma F.9

(Neural approximation of one Gauss–Newton step)
.

Fix a localization parameter
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
.
For every
\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}\in(0,1/2]
, there exists a ReLU network
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
with input
({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
and output in
\Box_{i}
such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i},t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon),({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)\end{subarray}}\bigl\|\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{T}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}.

(125)

Moreover, for a finite chart-dependent constant
C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log^{2}(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim dD(d\vee\beta)(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{-1})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}}+D\log^{3}(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{-1}),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\lesssim dD(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+3}(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{-1})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}}\log(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{-1})+dD\log^{4}(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{-1}).

(126)

Proof.

Recall that

{\mathcal{T}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)=\Pi_{i}^{({\rm box})}\Bigl({\bm{u}}+{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\bigl({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\bigr)\Bigr),\qquad{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})={\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{-1}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{\top}.

(127)

The clipping map
\Pi_{i}^{({\rm box})}
is exactly representable by a shallow ReLU network and is
1
-Lipschitz.

Step 1: Uniform bounds.

Since
\Box_{i}
is compact and
{\bm{z}}_{i},{\bm{A}}_{i}
are
C^{\infty}
on
\Box_{i}
, there exists a finite constant
B_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}\geq 1
such that every scalar component of
{\bm{z}}_{i}
and
{\bm{A}}_{i}
belongs to
{\mathcal{H}}^{2(\beta\vee 1)}(\Box_{i},B_{i,{\mathcal{T}}})
, and

\|{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{\infty}+\|{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{\infty}\leq B_{i,{\mathcal{T}}},\qquad{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}.

(128)

Here
\|{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{\infty}
denotes the maximum absolute entry of the
d\times D
matrix
{\bm{A}}_{i}({\bm{u}})
.

Moreover, on the active small-noise region there exists a finite constant
M_{x,i}\geq 1
such that

\|{\bm{x}}\|_{\infty}\leq M_{x,i},\qquad\|{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}=\Bigl\|\frac{{\bm{x}}}{m_{t}}\Bigr\|_{\infty}\leq M_{x,i}\underline{m}^{-1}.

(129)

Indeed, if
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, then
{\bm{x}}
is within
{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\varepsilon^{-1}})
of
m_{t}{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
; on the small-noise branch this is uniformly bounded, and
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
is compact.

Set

C_{i,\times}:=1\vee B_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}\vee M_{x,i}\underline{m}^{-1}.

(130)

Step 2: Approximate the ingredients.

Let
\varepsilon_{\rm aux}\in(0,1/2]
be chosen later.

First, by
Lemma

I.7
with
M_{x}=M_{x,i}
, there exists a vector-valued ReLU network
\widetilde{{\bm{\xi}}}
such that

\bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{\xi}}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm aux}

(131)

uniformly over the active small-noise region.
Its size is bounded by

\displaystyle L_{\xi},\log B_{\xi}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}^{-1}+\log^{2}(C_{i,\times}),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}_{\xi}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim D\bigl(\log^{3}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}^{-1}+\log^{3}(C_{i,\times})\bigr),

\displaystyle S_{\xi}

\displaystyle\lesssim D\bigl(\log^{4}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}^{-1}+\log^{4}(C_{i,\times})\bigr).

(132)

Second, apply
Theorem

I.1
to the scalar components of
{\bm{z}}_{i}
and
{\bm{A}}_{i}
on
\Box_{i}
after a fixed affine rescaling of
\Box_{i}
to
[0,1]^{d}
.
Thus there exist ReLU networks
\widetilde{{\bm{z}}}_{i}
and
\widetilde{{\bm{A}}}_{i}
such that

\|\widetilde{{\bm{z}}}_{i}-{\bm{z}}_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Box_{i})}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm aux},\qquad\|\widetilde{{\bm{A}}}_{i}-{\bm{A}}_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Box_{i})}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm aux}.

(133)

Stacking the
D+dD
scalar approximants gives size

\displaystyle L_{H}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log(C_{i,\times}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}_{H}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim dD(d\vee\beta)(C_{i,\times}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}^{-1})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}},

\displaystyle S_{H}

\displaystyle\lesssim dD(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+3}(C_{i,\times}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}^{-1})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}}\log(C_{i,\times}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}^{-1}),

\displaystyle B_{H}

\displaystyle\leq 1.

(134)

Define

{\bm{v}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t):={\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}),\qquad\widetilde{{\bm{v}}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t):=\widetilde{{\bm{\xi}}}({\bm{x}},t)-\widetilde{{\bm{z}}}_{i}({\bm{u}}).

By
Equations

131
and
133
,

\|\widetilde{{\bm{v}}}-{\bm{v}}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\varepsilon_{\rm aux}.

(135)

Also, by the bounds above,

|A_{i,j\ell}({\bm{u}})|\leq C_{i,\times},\qquad|v_{\ell}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)|\leq 2C_{i,\times}.

Increasing
C_{i,\times}
by an absolute factor if necessary, we may assume
C_{i,\times}\geq 1
and both true inputs to every multiplication below lie in
[-C_{i,\times},C_{i,\times}]
.

Step 3: Approximate the matrix-vector product.

For each
j=1,\dots,d
and
\ell=1,\dots,D
, apply
Lemma

I.3
with two inputs, bound
C_{i,\times}
, intrinsic multiplication error
\varepsilon_{\rm aux}
, and input perturbation tolerance
2\varepsilon_{\rm aux}
.
This gives a multiplication network satisfying

\displaystyle\bigl|\phi_{{\rm mult},j,\ell}\bigl(\widetilde{A}_{i,j,\ell}({\bm{u}}),\widetilde{v}_{\ell}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)\bigr)-A_{i,j,\ell}({\bm{u}})v_{\ell}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)\bigr|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\varepsilon_{\rm aux}+4C_{i,\times}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}\leq 5C_{i,\times}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}.

(136)

The size of each multiplication network is

\displaystyle L_{\rm mult}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(\varepsilon_{\rm aux}^{-1})+\log(C_{i,\times}),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}_{\rm mult}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 96,

\displaystyle S_{\rm mult}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(\varepsilon_{\rm aux}^{-1})+\log(C_{i,\times}),

\displaystyle\log B_{\rm mult}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(C_{i,\times}).

(137)

Define the approximate pre-clipped update by

\widetilde{q}_{j}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t):=u_{j}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{D}\phi_{{\rm mult},j\ell}\bigl(\widetilde{A}_{i,j\ell}({\bm{u}}),\widetilde{v}_{\ell}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)\bigr),\qquad j=1,\dots,d.

(138)

The exact pre-clipped update is

q_{j}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t):=u_{j}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{D}A_{i,j\ell}({\bm{u}})v_{\ell}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t).

Summing
Equation

136
over
\ell
gives

\|\widetilde{{\bm{q}}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{q}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq 5DC_{i,\times}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}.

(139)

Now define the final network by exact clipping:

\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t):=\Pi_{i}^{({\rm box})}\bigl(\widetilde{{\bm{q}}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)\bigr).

(140)

Because
\Pi_{i}^{({\rm box})}
is
1
-Lipschitz,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{T}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)\Bigr\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{{\bm{q}}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{q}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 5DC_{i,\times}\varepsilon_{\rm aux}.

Choose

\varepsilon_{\rm aux}:=\frac{\varepsilon_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{5DC_{i,\times}}.

(141)

Then
Equation

125
follows.

Step 4: Network size.

The network is obtained by composing the following pieces: the
{\bm{\xi}}={\bm{x}}/m_{t}
network from
Lemma

I.7
, the
d
-dimensional Hölder networks for
{\bm{z}}_{i}
and
{\bm{A}}_{i}
from
Theorem

I.1
, the
dD
multiplication networks from
Lemma

I.3
, exact affine summations, and the exact clipping map.

Substituting
Equation

141
into
Equations

132
,
134
and
137
and absorbing the fixed chart constants into a single constant
C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}\geq 1
gives
Equation

126
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma F.10

(Anchored neural approximation of the local projection coordinate)
.

Fix an anchor
{\bm{a}}\in K_{i}^{\circ}
.
Fix a localization parameter
0<\varepsilon_{\rm loc}\leq 1
such that
Equation

89
holds for every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm loc})
.
For every accuracy
\varepsilon_{u}\in(0,1]
, there exists a ReLU network
\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,{\bm{a}},\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon_{\rm loc}}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
with input
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
and output in
\Box_{i}
such that, for every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm loc})
and every
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm loc})
satisfying

\|{\bm{a}}-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}r_{i}^{\rm GN},

\bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,{\bm{a}},\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon_{\rm loc}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{u}.

(142)

Moreover, for a finite chart-dependent constant
C_{i,\Pi}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log^{3}(C_{i,\Pi}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim dD(d\vee\beta)(C_{i,\Pi}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-1})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}}+D\log^{3}(C_{i,\Pi}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-1}),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\lesssim dD(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+3}(C_{i,\Pi}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-1})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}}\log^{2}(C_{i,\Pi}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-1})+dD\log^{5}(C_{i,\Pi}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(C_{i,\Pi}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-1}).

(143)

Proof.

The construction uses a fixed anchor
{\bm{a}}
as the initial condition and then unrolls approximate Gauss–Newton steps.
Set

\varepsilon_{\rm step}:=\min\Bigl\{\frac{r_{i}^{\rm GN}}{8\sqrt{d}},\frac{\varepsilon_{u}}{4\sqrt{d}}\Bigr\}.

(144)

By
Lemma

F.9
, applied with localization parameter
\varepsilon_{\rm loc}
, there exists a ReLU network
\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\rm step}}
with output in
\Box_{i}
such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i},\;t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm loc}),\;({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm loc})\end{subarray}}\Bigl\|\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\rm step}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{T}}_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{x}},t)\Bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm step}.

(145)

The choice
Equation

144
guarantees in particular

\varepsilon_{\rm step}\leq\frac{r_{i}^{\rm GN}}{8\sqrt{d}},

which is exactly the basin condition
Equation

120
required in
Lemma

F.8
.

Let

K_{\varepsilon_{u}}:=\Bigl\lceil\log_{2}\Bigl(\frac{r_{i}^{\rm GN}}{\varepsilon_{u}}\Bigr)\Bigr\rceil_{+},\qquad\lceil a\rceil_{+}:=\max\{\lceil a\rceil,0\}.

(146)

Then

2^{-(K_{\varepsilon_{u}}+1)}r_{i}^{\rm GN}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{u}}{2}.

(147)

Indeed, if
r_{i}^{\rm GN}\leq\varepsilon_{u}
, then
K_{\varepsilon_{u}}=0
and
r_{i}^{\rm GN}/2\leq\varepsilon_{u}/2
.
If
r_{i}^{\rm GN}>\varepsilon_{u}
, then
K_{\varepsilon_{u}}\geq\log_{2}(r_{i}^{\rm GN}/\varepsilon_{u})
, so
2^{-K_{\varepsilon_{u}}}r_{i}^{\rm GN}\leq\varepsilon_{u}
.

Step 1: Define the unrolled network.

Define recursively

\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{0}({\bm{x}},t):={\bm{a}},\qquad\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k+1}({\bm{x}},t):=\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\rm step}}\bigl(\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k}({\bm{x}},t),{\bm{x}},t\bigr),\qquad k=0,\dots,K_{\varepsilon_{u}}-1.

(148)

Finally set

\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,{\bm{a}},\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon_{\rm loc}}({\bm{x}},t):=\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{K_{\varepsilon_{u}}}({\bm{x}},t).

(149)

Because each one-step network is
\Box_{i}
-valued, every iterate
\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{k}({\bm{x}},t)
also lies in
\Box_{i}
.

Step 2: The approximation error.

Assume now that
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm loc})
,
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm loc})
, and

\|{\bm{a}}-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}r_{i}^{\rm GN}.

Then the hypotheses of
Lemma

F.8
are satisfied with
K=K_{\varepsilon_{u}}
.
Hence

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{K_{\varepsilon_{u}}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-(K_{\varepsilon_{u}}+1)}r_{i}^{\rm GN}+2\sqrt{d}\,\varepsilon_{\rm step}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{u}}{2}+2\sqrt{d}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon_{u}}{4\sqrt{d}}=\varepsilon_{u}.

(150)

Since
\|{\bm{v}}\|_{\infty}\leq\|{\bm{v}}\|_{2}
,
Equation

150
implies

\Bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,{\bm{a}},\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon_{\rm loc}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\Bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{u},

which proves
Equation

142
.

Step 3: Network size.

By
Lemma

F.9
, the one-step network with accuracy
\varepsilon_{\rm step}
satisfies, for a chart-dependent constant
C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle L_{\rm step}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log^{2}(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{\rm step}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}_{\rm step}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim dD(d\vee\beta)(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{\rm step}^{-1})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}}+D\log^{3}(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{\rm step}^{-1}),

\displaystyle S_{\rm step}

\displaystyle\lesssim dD(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+3}(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{\rm step}^{-1})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}}\log(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{\rm step}^{-1})+dD\log^{4}(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{\rm step}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\log B_{\rm step}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(C_{i,{\mathcal{T}}}D\varepsilon_{\rm step}^{-1}).

From
Equation

144
, there exists a chart-dependent constant
C_{i,\Pi}\geq 1
such that

D\varepsilon_{\rm step}^{-1}\leq C_{i,\Pi}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-1}.

(151)

Also, by
Equation

146
,

K_{\varepsilon_{u}}\leq C\log(C_{i,\Pi}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-1}).

(152)

The unrolled network is the composition of
K_{\varepsilon_{u}}
copies of the one-step network, with the constant initialization
{\bm{a}}
inserted by an affine layer.
Therefore

L\leq K_{\varepsilon_{u}}L_{\rm step}+{\mathcal{O}}(1),\qquad S\leq K_{\varepsilon_{u}}S_{\rm step}+{\mathcal{O}}(1),

while the width and weight bound are controlled by the maximum of the corresponding quantities of the one-step networks.
Combining these relations with
Equations

151
and
152
gives exactly
Equation

143
.
This completes the proof.
∎

{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}(\varepsilon):=\bigcup_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon).

(153)

For a fixed auxiliary radius multiplier
c_{a}>0
, also write

{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon):=\bigcup_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon).

(154)

Lemma F.11

(Active chart centers lie within the reach tube)
.

Let
\delta_{\Pi}:=\eta_{\Pi}/2
.
Assume
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and
Equation

89
.
If
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\}
, then

\mathrm{dist}\bigl({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t),{\mathcal{M}}\bigr)\leq\delta_{\Pi}.

(155)

Moreover,
\delta_{\Pi}\leq\kappa/2
.

Proof.

Since
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, there exists
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\subset{\mathcal{M}}
such that

\|{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t).

Therefore

\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t),{\mathcal{M}})\leq\|{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t).

By
Equation

89
,

\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t)<\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\Pi}=\delta_{\Pi}.

Hence
Equation

155
holds.
Since
\eta_{\Pi}\leq\kappa
by
Equation

88
, we also have
\delta_{\Pi}\leq\kappa/2
.
∎

Lemma F.12

(Projection objective gap inside the reach tube)
.

Assume
0<\delta\leq\kappa/2
.
Then, for every
{\bm{\xi}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
satisfying
\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}},{\mathcal{M}})\leq\delta
and every
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{M}}
,

\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\Bigl(1+\frac{\delta}{\kappa}\Bigr)\|{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}.

(156)

Proof.

Since
\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}},{\mathcal{M}})\leq\delta\leq\kappa/2
, the reach assumption implies that
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})
is unique,
({\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}))\perp T_{\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})}{\mathcal{M}}
, and
\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}\leq\delta
.
For every
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{M}}
,

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})+\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}),{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\rangle.

(157)

The reach bound gives the tangent-ball inequality

\langle{\bm{v}},{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\rangle\leq\frac{\|{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}}{2\kappa},\qquad{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{M}},\quad{\bm{v}}\in N_{\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}}({\bm{\xi}}){\mathcal{M}},\quad\|{\bm{v}}\|_{2}=1.

(158)

If
{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})=0
, the claim follows immediately from
Equation

157
.
Otherwise, applying
Equation

158
to
{\bm{v}}=({\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}))/\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}
gives

\langle{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}),{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\rangle\leq\frac{\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}}{2\kappa}\|{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{\delta}{2\kappa}\|{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}.

Applying the same inequality to
-({\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}))/\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}
gives

\langle{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}),{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\rangle\geq-\frac{\delta}{2\kappa}\|{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}.

Substituting these two bounds into
Equation

157
yields

\Bigl(1-\frac{\delta}{\kappa}\Bigr)\|{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\Bigl(1+\frac{\delta}{\kappa}\Bigr)\|{\bm{y}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}.

Since
\delta\leq\kappa/2
, the lower coefficient is at least
1/2
.
This proves
Equation

156
.
∎

Theorem

F.13
is the step that avoids black-box high-order approximation of
the ambient map
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}
. The point to check is that the network searches over
finitely many intrinsic anchors, performs local Gauss–Newton updates in chart
coordinates, and uses an objective gap to select near-minimizing projection candidates.
Thus the non-logarithmic covering cost is intrinsic in the active chart coordinates; the
remaining direct ambient factors are displayed explicitly.
The proof has five logically separate checks:

(i)

the active-tube and reach conditions make
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})
unique and place its coordinate in a buffered chart box;

(ii)

a finite intrinsic anchor net contains an anchor inside the Gauss–Newton contraction basin of this coordinate;

(iii)

an approximate Gauss–Newton step is realized by ReLU networks using only
d
-dimensional chart variables and ambient matrix–vector arithmetic;

(iv)

a ReLU minimum and objective gate discard all candidates whose squared-distance objective is not within
{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{2})
of the best candidate;

(v)

the gated average is normalized by a reciprocal network whose denominator is bounded below by the positive gate mass.

Only the third and fourth checks carry non-logarithmic complexity; their covering cost is the intrinsic anchor count times
d
-dimensional chart approximation, with the remaining
D
dependence coming from explicit coordinatewise ambient arithmetic.

Theorem F.13

(Neural approximation of the active projection center)
.

Fix a localization parameter
0<\varepsilon<1
such that
Equation

89
holds for every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
.
For every
\varepsilon_{\Pi}\in(0,1]
, there exists a ReLU network

\widetilde{\Pi}_{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to{\mathbb{R}}^{D}

such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)\\
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}(\varepsilon)\end{subarray}}\Bigl\|\widetilde{\Pi}_{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}\bigl({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr)\Bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\Pi}.

(159)

Let

N_{\rm cand}:=\sum_{i=1}^{C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}|{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\rm anc}|.

Then
N_{\rm cand}\leq A_{{\mathcal{M}}}
, with
A_{{\mathcal{M}}}
bounded in
Equation

117
.
For a finite geometry-dependent constant
C_{\rm proj}\geq 1
,
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
with

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log^{3}(C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-2}),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD(d\vee\beta)(C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-2})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}}+A_{{\mathcal{M}}}D\log^{3}(C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-2}),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+3}(C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-2})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}}\log^{2}(C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-2})+A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD\log^{5}(C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-2}),

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-2}).

(160)

Proof.

It suffices to prove the result for sufficiently small
\varepsilon_{\Pi}
.
For larger
\varepsilon_{\Pi}
, use the construction at a fixed small
\varepsilon_{\Pi}
and absorb constants into
C_{\rm proj}
.

Let

{\mathcal{J}}:=\{(i,{\bm{a}}):1\leq i\leq C_{{\mathcal{M}}},{\bm{a}}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\rm anc}\}.

For each
(i,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}
, we construct a candidate projection center, then use a continuous objective gate to average only near-minimizing candidates.

Step 1: Existence of a good chart-anchor candidate.

Fix
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}(\varepsilon)
.
Write

{\bm{\xi}}:={\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t),\qquad{\mathcal{D}}_{\star}:=\|{\bm{\xi}}-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}.

By
Lemma

F.11
,
\mathrm{dist}({\bm{\xi}},{\mathcal{M}})\leq\delta_{\Pi}
and
\delta_{\Pi}\leq\kappa/2
.
Hence,
Lemma

F.12
applies with
\delta=\delta_{\Pi}
.
For convenience write

C_{\rm glob,\Pi}^{(+)}:=\Bigl(1+\frac{\delta_{\Pi}}{\kappa}\Bigr).

Since
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}(\varepsilon)
, there exists an active chart
i_{\star}
such that
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i_{\star}}(\varepsilon)
.
By
Lemma

F.4
,

\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\in U_{i_{\star}},\qquad{\bm{u}}_{i_{\star}}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t):=\phi_{i_{\star}}(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}))\in K_{i_{\star}}^{\circ}.

By
Lemma

F.7
, there exists
{\bm{a}}_{\star}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i_{\star}}^{\rm anc}
such that

\|{\bm{a}}_{\star}-{\bm{u}}_{i_{\star}}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}r_{i_{\star}}^{\rm GN}.

(161)

Let

L_{z}^{\max}:=\max_{1\leq i\leq C_{{\mathcal{M}}}}L_{i}^{(+)}.

Choose

\varepsilon_{y}^{\rm glob}:=\min\Bigl\{\frac{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}{8},\sqrt{\frac{1}{32C_{\rm glob,\Pi}^{(+)}}}\varepsilon_{\Pi}\Bigr\},\qquad\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm glob}:=\frac{\varepsilon_{y}^{\rm glob}}{\sqrt{d}\,L_{z}^{\max}}.

(162)

For every
(i,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}
, let
\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,{\bm{a}},\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm glob}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}({\bm{x}},t)
be the anchored local-coordinate network from
Lemma

F.10
, with localization parameter
\varepsilon
and accuracy
\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm glob}
.
Define the exact manifold-valued candidate

{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t):={\bm{z}}_{i}\Bigl(\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,{\bm{a}},\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm glob}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}({\bm{x}},t)\Bigr).

(163)

Since the coordinate network is
\Box_{i}
-valued and
\Box_{i}\subset\phi_{i}(U_{i})
, we have
{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{M}}
.

For the good pair
(i_{\star},{\bm{a}}_{\star})
,
Equation

161
and
Lemma

F.10
give

\Bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i_{\star},{\bm{a}}_{\star},\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm glob}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i_{\star}}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\Bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm glob}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\|{\bm{y}}_{i_{\star},{\bm{a}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq L_{i_{\star}}^{(+)}\Bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i_{\star},{\bm{a}}_{\star},\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm glob}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i_{\star}}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq L_{z}^{\max}\sqrt{d}\,\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm glob}=\varepsilon_{y}^{\rm glob}.

(164)

Define

{\mathcal{D}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t):=\|{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}.

(165)

By the upper bound in
Equation

156
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{i_{\star},{\bm{a}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{D}}_{\star}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm glob,\Pi}^{(+)}\|{\bm{y}}_{i_{\star},{\bm{a}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\rm glob,\Pi}^{(+)}(\varepsilon_{y}^{\rm glob})^{2}\leq\frac{1}{32}\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{2}.

(166)

Step 2: Approximate candidate outputs and objectives.

For each
(i,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}
, construct a ReLU network
\widetilde{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}^{\,{\rm out}}
satisfying

\Bigl\|\widetilde{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}^{\,{\rm out}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)\Bigr\|_{2}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}{8}.

(167)

This is obtained by composing
\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,{\bm{a}},\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm glob}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}
with a
{\mathcal{H}}^{2(\beta\vee 1)}
approximation of
{\bm{z}}_{i}
on
\Box_{i}
to coordinate accuracy
\varepsilon_{\Pi}/(8\sqrt{D})
using
Theorem

I.1
.

Next, construct ReLU networks
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}
such that

\bigl|\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{D}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr|\leq\varepsilon_{D},\qquad\varepsilon_{D}:=\frac{1}{32}\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{2}.

(168)

Indeed, approximate
{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)
by
Lemma

I.7
, approximate
{\bm{z}}_{i}
on
\Box_{i}
by
Theorem

I.1
, then use
Lemma

I.3
to square and sum the
D
coordinate differences.
All variables are uniformly bounded on the active small-noise region and on the compact boxes, so coordinate and arithmetic accuracies of order
\varepsilon_{D}/D
make the total error at most
\varepsilon_{D}
.
This is the source of the
D
factor inside the Hölder approximation term in
Equation

160
.

Step 3: Continuous objective gate.

Define the finite minimum

\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\min}({\bm{x}},t):=\min_{(i,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}}\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t).

This is exactly ReLU-realizable using

\min\{a,b\}=a-{\mathsf{ReLU}}(a-b).

Let

\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}:=\frac{1}{32}\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{2}.

For each
(i,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}
, define

\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t):={\mathsf{ReLU}}\Bigl(\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}-\bigl(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)-\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\min}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr)\Bigr),

(169)

and

W_{\rm glob}({\bm{x}},t):=\sum_{(i,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}}\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t).

At least one index attains
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\min}
, and its weight equals
\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}
.
Hence

\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}\leq W_{\rm glob}({\bm{x}},t)\leq N_{\rm cand}\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}.

(170)

We now show that every positive-weight exact candidate is close to
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})
.
Suppose
\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)>0
.
Then

\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)<\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\min}({\bm{x}},t)+\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}.

Since
(i_{\star},{\bm{a}}_{\star})
is an available candidate,

\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\min}({\bm{x}},t)\leq\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{i_{\star},{\bm{a}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}},t)\leq{\mathcal{D}}_{i_{\star},{\bm{a}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}},t)+\varepsilon_{D}.

Therefore, by
Equation

166
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)+\varepsilon_{D}

\displaystyle<\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\min}({\bm{x}},t)+\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}+\varepsilon_{D}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathcal{D}}_{i_{\star},{\bm{a}}_{\star}}({\bm{x}},t)+\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}+2\varepsilon_{D}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathcal{D}}_{\star}+\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}+3\varepsilon_{D}.

(171)

By the definitions of
\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}
and
\varepsilon_{D}
,

\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}+3\varepsilon_{D}=\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{1}{32}+\frac{3}{32}\Bigr)=\frac{1}{2}\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}{2}\Bigr)^{2}.

Thus

{\mathcal{D}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{D}}_{\star}<\frac{1}{2}\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}{2}\Bigr)^{2}.

By the lower bound in
Equation

156
,

\|{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2({\mathcal{D}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{D}}_{\star}).

Therefore

\|{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\|_{2}<\frac{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}{2}\qquad\text{whenever }\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)>0.

(172)

Step 4: Normalize and average.

Define the ideal gated output

\Pi_{\rm gate}({\bm{x}},t):=\frac{\sum_{(i,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}}\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)\widetilde{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}^{\,{\rm out}}({\bm{x}},t)}{W_{\rm glob}({\bm{x}},t)}.

The normalized weights are nonnegative and sum to one.
Combining
Equations

167
and
172
, for every positive-weight candidate,

\bigl\|\widetilde{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}^{\,{\rm out}}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})\bigr\|_{2}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}{8}+\frac{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}{2}=\frac{5}{8}\varepsilon_{\Pi}.

Hence

\|\Pi_{\rm gate}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))\|_{2}\leq\frac{5}{8}\varepsilon_{\Pi}.

(173)

It remains to implement the division by
W_{\rm glob}
.
The scalar weights
\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}}
are computed in parallel for all
(i,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}
, and the sum

W_{\rm glob}({\bm{x}},t)=\sum_{(i,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}}\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)

is represented exactly by one affine layer.
Thus, forming
W_{\rm glob}
introduces no additional approximation error.
By
Equation

170
,
W_{\rm glob}
lies in
[\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate},N_{\rm cand}\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}]
.

Apply
Lemma

I.4
to approximate
1/W_{\rm glob}
on this interval, and then use
Lemma

I.3
to multiply the reciprocal by each numerator coordinate

N_{k}({\bm{x}},t):=\sum_{(i,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}}\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)\widetilde{y}_{i,{\bm{a}},k}^{\,{\rm out}}({\bm{x}},t),\qquad k=1,\dots,D.

Since
N_{\rm cand}
is finite, all candidate outputs are uniformly bounded, and
\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}\asymp\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{2}
, the reciprocal and multiplication accuracies can be chosen with only logarithmic overhead in
\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-1}
so that the total arithmetic error is at most
3\varepsilon_{\Pi}/8
in
\ell_{2}
.
This yields a ReLU network
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}
satisfying

\|\widetilde{\Pi}_{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{\rm gate}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq\frac{3}{8}\varepsilon_{\Pi}.

Combining this with
Equation

173
gives

\|\widetilde{\Pi}_{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\Pi}.

Since
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\leq\|\cdot\|_{2}
, this proves
Equation

159
.

Step 5: Network size.

The dominant cost comes from approximating the chart maps inside the objective networks to coordinate accuracy of order
\varepsilon_{D}/D\asymp\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{2}/D
.
By
Theorem

I.1
, this gives the intrinsic rate
(C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-2})^{\frac{d}{2(\beta\vee 1)}}
up to logarithmic factors.
The anchored local coordinate networks have scale
(CD\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-1})^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}
, which is dominated by this objective-approximation scale because
\varepsilon_{\Pi}\leq 1
.
The finite ReLU minimum, ReLU gates, affine summation layers, reciprocal network, and multiplication networks add only finite atlas factors and logarithmic factors.
Indeed,
W_{\rm glob}\geq\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{\rm gate}\asymp\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{2}
, so approximating
1/W_{\rm glob}
only contributes logarithmic factors of order
\log(C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-2})
.
Stacking over the
N_{\rm cand}
chart-anchor candidates gives the displayed
A_{{\mathcal{M}}}
factors in
Equation

160
.
This completes the proof.

∎

Corollary F.14

(Active chart coordinates from the projection-center network)
.

Fix a chart
i
and a localization parameter
0<\varepsilon<1
such that
Equation

89
holds for every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
.
For every
\varepsilon_{u}\in(0,1]
, there exists a ReLU network

\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to\Box_{i}

such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)\\
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)\end{subarray}}\left\|\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{u}.

(174)

Moreover, for a finite chart- and geometry-dependent constant

C_{i,\Pi}^{\rm coord}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log^{3}\!\bigl(C_{i,\Pi}^{\rm coord}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-2}\bigr),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD(d\vee\beta)\bigl(C_{i,\Pi}^{\rm coord}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-2}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}+A_{{\mathcal{M}}}D\log^{3}\!\bigl(C_{i,\Pi}^{\rm coord}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-2}\bigr),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+3}\bigl(C_{i,\Pi}^{\rm coord}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-2}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}\log^{2}\!\bigl(C_{i,\Pi}^{\rm coord}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD\log^{5}\!\bigl(C_{i,\Pi}^{\rm coord}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-2}\bigr),

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}\!\bigl(C_{i,\Pi}^{\rm coord}D\varepsilon_{u}^{-2}\bigr).

(175)

Proof.

Extend the projection chart affine formula in
Equation

16
to all of

{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
by

\phi_{i}^{\rm amb}({\bm{y}}):=\eta_{i}\bigl({\bm{V}}_{i}^{\top}({\bm{y}}-{\bm{y}}_{i})+{\bm{c}}_{i}\bigr).

Let
L_{\phi,i}^{\rm amb}\geq 1
be a finite Lipschitz constant for this affine map from

({\mathbb{R}}^{D},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
to
({\mathbb{R}}^{d},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
, and set

\varepsilon_{\Pi}:=1\wedge\frac{\varepsilon_{u}}{2L_{\phi,i}^{\rm amb}}.

Apply
Theorem

F.13
with accuracy
\varepsilon_{\Pi}
.
On
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)\subset{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}(\varepsilon)
it gives

\bigl\|\widetilde{\Pi}_{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\Pi}.

Define the raw coordinate network

\widehat{{\bm{u}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}:=\phi_{i}^{\rm amb}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{\varepsilon_{\Pi}}

and then clip its output coordinatewise:

\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}:=\Pi_{i}^{(\rm box)}\bigl(\widehat{{\bm{u}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}\bigr).

The affine map is exact network arithmetic and the clipping map is exactly ReLU-realizable.
By
Lemma

F.4
,
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\in K_{i}^{\circ}\subset\Box_{i}
on

{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, so the clipping map fixes the target coordinate and is

1
-Lipschitz. Therefore

\left\|\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\right\|_{\infty}\leq L_{\phi,i}^{\rm amb}\varepsilon_{\Pi}\leq\varepsilon_{u}.

The size bounds are those of
Equation

160
with

\varepsilon_{\Pi}^{-2}\leq C_{i}\varepsilon_{u}^{-2}
, plus only fixed affine and clipping
layers. Absorbing
C_{i}
and the fixed affine coefficients into

C_{i,\Pi}^{\rm coord}
proves
Equation

175
.
∎

F.3
Projection-centered Laplace expansion

This block derives the exact small-noise asymptotic object that the neural network will later approximate.
The phase is expanded at
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)
, the coordinate of the nearest point on the manifold.
The normal residual is kept in the Gaussian factor, while the remaining smooth terms are expanded into finitely many powers of
\sigma_{t}
and
{\bm{\nu}}_{i}=\sigma_{t}^{-1}{\bm{r}}_{i}
.
The final proposition in this block records the uniform remainder bound and fixes the small-noise constants used later.

On the active region, we center the phase exactly at the projection coordinate
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)
from
Lemma

F.4
.
For fixed
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, write

{\bm{\xi}}:={\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t),\qquad\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}):=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)),\qquad{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}:={\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),

and

{\bm{r}}_{i}:={\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)={\bm{x}}-m_{t}\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}),\qquad{\bm{\nu}}_{i}:=\sigma_{t}^{-1}{\bm{r}}_{i},\qquad{\bm{G}}_{i}^{\Pi}:={\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}).

Lemma F.15

(Projection-centered Gaussian phase expansion)
.

For each
m\geq 1
, define

{\bm{z}}_{i,m}({\bm{u}},{\bm{w}}):=\frac{1}{m!}{\mathrm{D}}^{m}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})[{\bm{w}}^{\otimes m}]\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}.

Fix
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, and assume
Equation

89
.
Let
{\bm{w}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
satisfy
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}}\in\Box_{i}
.
Then

\displaystyle\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|{\bm{r}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}^{\Pi}{\bm{w}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{\ell}\Psi_{i,\ell}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}}_{i},{\bm{w}},t\bigr)+\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(F)}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}}_{i},{\bm{w}},t\bigr),

(176)

where for
\ell\geq 1
,

\Psi_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t):=-m_{t}\,{\bm{\nu}}^{\top}{\bm{z}}_{i,\ell+1}({\bm{u}},{\bm{w}})+\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=\ell+2\\
m_{1},m_{2}\geq 1\end{subarray}}{\bm{z}}_{i,m_{1}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{w}})^{\top}{\bm{z}}_{i,m_{2}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{w}}).

(177)

Moreover, for constants independent of
({\bm{x}},t,{\bm{w}})
,

\displaystyle\bigl|\Psi_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ell}(1+\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2})(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{\ell+2}),

(178)

\displaystyle\bigl|{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(F)}({\bm{u}},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2})(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2\lfloor\beta\rfloor+6}).

Each
\Psi_{i,\ell}
is affine in
{\bm{\nu}}
.

Proof.

Step 1: Taylor expansion of the chart map.

Because
\Box_{i}\Subset\phi_{i}(U_{i})
and
{\bm{z}}_{i}
is smooth on
\phi_{i}(U_{i})
, all derivatives of
{\bm{z}}_{i}
up to order
\lfloor\beta\rfloor+3
are uniformly bounded on
\Box_{i}
.
Since both
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}
and
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}}
lie in
\Box_{i}
, Taylor’s theorem gives

\displaystyle{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})

\displaystyle={\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})+\sum_{m=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2}\sigma_{t}^{m}{\bm{z}}_{i,m}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})+\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+3}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(z)}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}},t)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})+\sum_{m=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2}\sigma_{t}^{m}{\bm{z}}_{i,m}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})+\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+3}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(z)}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}},t).

The remainder satisfies

\|{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(z)}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}},t)\|_{2}\leq C(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+3}).

(179)

Define

\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}}):={\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}).

(180)

Then

\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}})=\sum_{m=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2}\sigma_{t}^{m}{\bm{z}}_{i,m}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})+\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+3}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(z)}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}},t).

(181)

Step 2: Exact algebraic expansion of the phase.

By the definition of
F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}
,

\displaystyle 2F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})-m_{t}\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{r}}_{i}-m_{t}\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{r}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-2m_{t}{\bm{r}}_{i}^{\top}\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}})+m_{t}^{2}\|\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}.

Therefore

\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}=\frac{\|{\bm{r}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}-\frac{m_{t}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\bm{r}}_{i}^{\top}\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}})+\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\|\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}.

(182)

The first Taylor term is

{\bm{z}}_{i,1}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})={\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}.

By
Equation

95
,

{\bm{r}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{z}}_{i,1}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})={\bm{r}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}={\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})^{\top}{\bm{r}}_{i}=0.

(183)

Using
{\bm{r}}_{i}=\sigma_{t}{\bm{\nu}}_{i}
and
Equations

181
and
183
, we get

\displaystyle-\frac{m_{t}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\bm{r}}_{i}^{\top}\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}})

\displaystyle=-m_{t}\sum_{m=2}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2}\sigma_{t}^{m-1}{\bm{\nu}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{z}}_{i,m}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})-m_{t}\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2}{\bm{\nu}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(z)}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}},t)

\displaystyle=-m_{t}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{\ell}{\bm{\nu}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{z}}_{i,\ell+1}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})+\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\rm cr)}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}}_{i},{\bm{w}},t).

(184)

Here the term with
m=\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2
has order
\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}
and is included in the remainder, together with the Taylor remainder.
Hence, using
Equation

179
and
\sigma_{t}\leq 1
,

|{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\rm cr)}({\bm{u}},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)|\leq C(1+\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2})(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+3}).

(185)

Step 3: Expansion of the quadratic term.

Using
Equation

181
,

\displaystyle\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\|\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2}\sigma_{t}^{m_{1}+m_{2}-2}{\bm{z}}_{i,m_{1}}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})^{\top}{\bm{z}}_{i,m_{2}}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})+\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\rm quad)}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}},t).

The pair
(m_{1},m_{2})=(1,1)
gives

\displaystyle\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{z}}_{i,1}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})^{\top}{\bm{z}}_{i,1}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi})^{\top}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}=\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}^{\Pi}{\bm{w}}.

All other principal pairs satisfy
m_{1}+m_{2}-2\geq 1
.
Reindexing by
\ell=m_{1}+m_{2}-2
and retaining powers
\ell=1,\dots,\lfloor\beta\rfloor
gives

\displaystyle\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\|\Delta_{i}({\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}^{\Pi}{\bm{w}}+\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}+m_{2}=\ell+2\\
m_{1},m_{2}\geq 1\end{subarray}}{\bm{z}}_{i,m_{1}}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})^{\top}{\bm{z}}_{i,m_{2}}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\rm quad)}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}},t).

(186)

The omitted Taylor pairs have total power at least
\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}
after division by
\sigma_{t}^{2}
, and the terms involving
{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(z)}
have even higher powers.
Therefore

|{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\rm quad)}({\bm{u}},{\bm{w}},t)|\leq C(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2\lfloor\beta\rfloor+6}).

(187)

Step 4: Collect the coefficients.

Substituting
Equations

184
and
186
into
Equation

182
gives
Equation

176
, with
\Psi_{i,\ell}
defined by
Equation

177
and

{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(F)}:={\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\rm cr)}+{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\rm quad)}.

The bounds in
Equation

178
follow from the multilinearity of
{\mathrm{D}}^{m}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})[{\bm{w}}^{\otimes m}]
, the uniform derivative bounds on
\Box_{i}
, and
Equations

185
and
187
.
Finally,
\Psi_{i,\ell}
is affine in
{\bm{\nu}}
because its first term is linear in
{\bm{\nu}}
and its second term is independent of
{\bm{\nu}}
.
∎

By the convention in
Definition

2.2
, every coefficient retained in the neural approximation has strictly positive regularity
\beta-q>0
.

Proposition F.16

(Local Laplace expansion for de-Gaussianized chart integrals)
.

Assume the small-noise branch is chosen so that, for every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
, we have
\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t)<\eta_{\Pi}/2
.
Fix
i\in\{1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\}
and
R_{i}\in\{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i},{\mathcal{P}}_{i,1},\dots,{\mathcal{P}}_{i,D}\}
.
Then, there exist coefficient functions

A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}:K_{i}^{\circ}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to{\mathbb{R}},\qquad 0\leq q\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor,\qquad{\bm{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{D},\quad\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q,

such that, for every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and every
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
,

\displaystyle R_{i}({\bm{x}},t)

\displaystyle=\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{q}\sum_{\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q}A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),t\bigr){\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)^{{\bm{\lambda}}}+\overline{{\mathrm{Rem}}}_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{x}},t),

(188)

where

\bigl|\overline{{\mathrm{Rem}}}_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr|\leq C\sigma_{t}^{\beta}\exp\Bigl(-c\frac{\|{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr).

(189)

Moreover,

A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}(\cdot,t)\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta-q}(K_{i}^{\circ},C_{\rm coef}),\qquad t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}],

where
C_{\rm coef}
depends only on
B_{a}
,
\beta
,
\underline{m},\overline{m}
, and
\mathfrak{C}_{{\mathcal{M}}}
.

Proof.

We prove the result for a generic
R_{i}\in\{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i},{\mathcal{P}}_{i,1},\dots,{\mathcal{P}}_{i,D}\}
, and write
a_{i}^{(R)}
for the corresponding scalar amplitude.
Fix
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, and set

{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}:={\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),\qquad{\bm{r}}:={\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t),\qquad{\bm{\nu}}:={\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=\sigma_{t}^{-1}{\bm{r}},\qquad{\bm{G}}^{\Pi}:={\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}).

All constants below may depend on
\underline{m},\overline{m},\beta
, and
\mathfrak{C}_{{\mathcal{M}}}
, but not on
({\bm{x}},t)
and
\varepsilon
.

By definition,

R_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{\phi_{i}(U_{i})}\exp\Bigl(-F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})/\sigma_{t}^{2}\Bigr)a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}.

With
{\bm{u}}={\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}}
and

\Omega_{i,{\bm{x}},t}:=\sigma_{t}^{-1}\bigl(\phi_{i}(U_{i})-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\bigr),

the factor
\sigma_{t}^{-d}
cancels and

R_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=\int_{\Omega_{i,{\bm{x}},t}}\exp\Bigl(-F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})/\sigma_{t}^{2}\Bigr)a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{w}}.

(190)

Step 1: Uniform quadratic lower bound for the phase.

Define

{\bm{\xi}}:={\bm{x}}/m_{t},\qquad\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{\xi}}):=\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}.

Since
F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})=m_{t}^{2}\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{\xi}})
and
{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}})
,

F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})=\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}.

By
Lemma

F.4
,
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\in K_{i}^{\circ}\Subset\Box_{i}
and

\nabla_{\bm{u}}\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi};{\bm{\xi}})={\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})^{\top}\bigl({\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})-{\bm{\xi}}\bigr)=0.

Moreover, by
Lemma

F.5
,

\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi};{\bm{\xi}})\succeq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{i}^{(-)}{\bm{I}}_{d}.

The active condition and
\delta_{\rm act}(\varepsilon,t)<\eta_{\Pi}/2
imply that
{\bm{\xi}}\in\mathcal{X}_{i}
.
Recall the constants
H_{i}
and
r_{i}
from
Equations

91
and
92
.
Then, whenever
\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}\leq r_{i}
,

\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{\xi}})\succeq\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{i}^{(-)}{\bm{I}}_{d}.

Taylor’s theorem gives

\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{\xi}})-\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi};{\bm{\xi}})\geq\frac{\lambda_{i}^{(-)}}{8}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}^{2}.

Substituting
{\bm{u}}={\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}}
yields, for
\sigma_{t}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq r_{i}
,

\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\geq\frac{\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}+c_{0}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}

(191)

for some
c_{0}>0
.

Next let
S_{i}^{(R)}:=\operatorname{supp}a_{i}^{(R)}\Subset\phi_{i}(U_{i})
and consider

\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ}:=\{{\bm{\zeta}}\in\mathcal{X}_{i}:\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\zeta}})\in\phi_{i}^{-1}(K_{i}^{\circ})\}.

This set is compact because
\mathcal{X}_{i}
is compact, the reach projection is continuous on the reach tube, and
\phi_{i}^{-1}(K_{i}^{\circ})
is compact in
U_{i}
.
On
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ}
, write

{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{\zeta}}):=\phi_{i}(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\zeta}})).

Define

{\mathcal{C}}_{i}^{(R)}:=\Bigl\{({\bm{\zeta}},{\bm{u}})\in\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\circ}\times S_{i}^{(R)}:\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{\zeta}})\|_{2}\geq r_{i}\Bigr\}.

If this compact set is nonempty, the continuous function

({\bm{\zeta}},{\bm{u}})\mapsto\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{\zeta}})-\Phi_{i}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{\zeta}});{\bm{\zeta}}\bigr)

is strictly positive on it.
Otherwise
{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})
would be another nearest point on
{\mathcal{M}}
to
{\bm{\zeta}}
, contradicting uniqueness of the metric projection in the reach tube.
Hence there is
c_{1}>0
such that, for active
{\bm{\xi}}
,

\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{\xi}})-\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi};{\bm{\xi}})\geq c_{1}

whenever
{\bm{u}}\in S_{i}^{(R)}
and
\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}\geq r_{i}
.
Therefore, on this part of the support,

\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\geq\frac{\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\frac{\underline{m}^{2}c_{1}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

(192)

Step 2: Split the integral into a local window and a tail.

Let

R_{\sigma}:=A\sqrt{\log(e/\sigma_{t})},

where
A>0
will be chosen large enough.
By decreasing the small-noise threshold if necessary, assume

\sigma_{t}R_{\sigma}\leq r_{i}\leq d_{i}^{\Box}/2.

Since
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\in K_{i}^{\circ}
, this implies

{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}}\in\Box_{i}\subset\phi_{i}(U_{i}),\qquad\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{\sigma}.

Thus the local part of
Equation

190
is over the full ball
\{\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{\sigma}\}
.

Write
R_{i}=I_{\rm loc}+I_{\rm tail}
, where
I_{\rm loc}
is the integral over
\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{\sigma}
.
On
R_{\sigma}<\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq r_{i}/\sigma_{t}
, use
Equation

191
; on
\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}>r_{i}/\sigma_{t}
, use
Equation

192
on the support of
a_{i}^{(R)}
.
Since
a_{i}^{(R)}
is bounded and
S_{i}^{(R)}
has finite Lebesgue measure,

\displaystyle|I_{\rm tail}|
