\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})}\int_{\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}>R_{\sigma}}e^{-c_{0}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}{\bm{w}}+C\sigma_{t}^{-d}e^{-\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})}e^{-c/\sigma_{t}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})}e^{-c_{2}R_{\sigma}^{2}}+C\sigma_{t}^{-d}e^{-\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})}e^{-c/\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

(193)

Choose
A
so large that
e^{-c_{2}R_{\sigma}^{2}}\leq C\sigma_{t}^{\beta}
.
Also, after decreasing the small-noise threshold if necessary,
\sigma_{t}^{-d}e^{-c/\sigma_{t}^{2}}\leq C\sigma_{t}^{\beta}
.
Hence

|I_{\rm tail}|\leq C\sigma_{t}^{\beta}\exp\Bigl(-c\frac{\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr).

(194)

Step 3: Expand the phase on the local window.

On
\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{\sigma}
,
Lemma

F.15
and the
\lfloor\beta\rfloor
-convention give the truncated form

\displaystyle\frac{F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}^{\Pi}{\bm{w}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{\ell}\Psi_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)+\sigma_{t}^{\beta}\widehat{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(F)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t).

(195)

Indeed, the remainder from
Lemma

F.15
has order
\sigma_{t}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}
.
Since
\lfloor\beta\rfloor<\beta\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1
and
\sigma_{t}\leq 1
, this is bounded by
\sigma_{t}^{\beta}
times the same polynomial; when
\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, the two powers coincide.
Thus

|\widehat{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(F)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)|\leq C(1+\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2})(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2\lfloor\beta\rfloor+6})

on the local window.
Set

Z_{\sigma}:=-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{\ell}\Psi_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)-\sigma_{t}^{\beta}\widehat{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(F)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t).

Then

\exp\Bigl(-F_{i,{\bm{x}},t}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})/\sigma_{t}^{2}\Bigr)=e^{-\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})}e^{-\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}^{\Pi}{\bm{w}}}e^{Z_{\sigma}}.

Since
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
,
\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}\leq c_{\star}\sqrt{\log\varepsilon^{-1}}
.
On
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
,
\log\varepsilon^{-1}\lesssim\log(e/\sigma_{t})
.
Combining this with
\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{\sigma}
and
Equation

178
, we obtain
|Z_{\sigma}|\leq 1/2
after decreasing the small-noise threshold if necessary.

Step 4: Expand the exponential correction.

Taylor expansion of
e^{Z_{\sigma}}
, collecting powers of
\sigma_{t}
up to order
\lfloor\beta\rfloor
, gives

e^{Z_{\sigma}}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{\ell}{\mathcal{E}}_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)+\sigma_{t}^{\beta}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\exp)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t),

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{i,0}=1
, and for
1\leq\ell\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor
,

{\mathcal{E}}_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t):=\sum_{r=1}^{\ell}\frac{(-1)^{r}}{r!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1}+\cdots+\ell_{r}=\ell\\
\ell_{j}\geq 1\end{subarray}}\Psi_{i,\ell_{1}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)\cdots\Psi_{i,\ell_{r}}({\bm{u}},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t).

Moreover, on the local window,

|{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\exp)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)|\leq C(1+\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{P_{E}})(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{M_{E}})

for finite constants
P_{E},M_{E}
.

Step 5: Expand the amplitude and multiply expansions.

Since
a_{i}^{(R)}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}(\phi_{i}(U_{i}),B_{a})
, Taylor’s theorem with the
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}
remainder gives

a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})=\sum_{m=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{m}{\mathcal{A}}_{i,m}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})+\sigma_{t}^{\beta}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\rm amp)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}}),

where

{\mathcal{A}}_{i,m}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},{\bm{w}}):=\frac{1}{m!}{\mathrm{D}}^{m}a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}})[{\bm{w}}^{\otimes m}],

and

|{\mathcal{A}}_{i,m}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},{\bm{w}})|\leq C(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{m}),\qquad|{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(\rm amp)}({\bm{u}},{\bm{w}})|\leq C(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{\beta}).

This is Taylor’s theorem through order
\lfloor\beta\rfloor
with the
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}
remainder.
When
\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, the convention
\lfloor\beta\rfloor=\beta-1
makes this the standard Lipschitz top-derivative remainder of order
\beta
.
Multiplying gives

e^{Z_{\sigma}}a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})=\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{q}{\mathcal{U}}_{i,q}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)+\sigma_{t}^{\beta}{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t),

where

{\mathcal{U}}_{i,q}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m+\ell=q\\
0\leq m,\ell\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor\end{subarray}}{\mathcal{A}}_{i,m}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},{\bm{w}}){\mathcal{E}}_{i,\ell}({\bm{u}},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t).

All omitted products contain either an explicit amplitude or exponential remainder, or have total algebraic order at least
\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1
.
Since
\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1\geq\beta
, every omitted product is bounded by
\sigma_{t}^{\beta}
times a fixed polynomial in
({\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}})
on
0<\sigma_{t}\leq 1
.
Furthermore,

|{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)|\leq C(1+\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{P_{R}})(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{M_{R}})

on
\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{\sigma}
.

Step 6: Identify the principal coefficients.

Substituting the expansions into
I_{\rm loc}
yields

I_{\rm loc}=e^{-\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})}\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{q}\int_{\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{\sigma}}e^{-\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}^{\Pi}{\bm{w}}}{\mathcal{U}}_{i,q}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{w}}+E_{\rm loc}.

By the polynomial bound on
{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i}^{(R)}
and uniform ellipticity of
{\bm{G}}^{\Pi}
,

|E_{\rm loc}|\leq C\sigma_{t}^{\beta}e^{-\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{2}/2}(1+\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{P_{R}})\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}e^{-c_{g}\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{M_{R}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{w}}\leq C\sigma_{t}^{\beta}e^{-c\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{2}}.

For
0\leq q\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor
, define the coefficient functions by collecting powers of
{\bm{\nu}}
in

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}e^{-\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}){\bm{w}}}{\mathcal{U}}_{i,q}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{w}}=\sum_{\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q}A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},t){\bm{\nu}}^{{\bm{\lambda}}}.

This is valid because
{\mathcal{U}}_{i,q}^{(R)}
is a polynomial in
{\bm{\nu}}
of degree at most
q
.

The replacement of the local integral by the full integral costs only a tail error.
Indeed,

|{\mathcal{U}}_{i,q}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)|\leq C(1+\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{q})(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{M_{U}}),

so, after increasing
A
if necessary,

\displaystyle e^{-\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})}\sigma_{t}^{q}\left|\int_{\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}>R_{\sigma}}e^{-\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}^{\Pi}{\bm{w}}}{\mathcal{U}}_{i,q}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{w}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-c\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{2}}e^{-c_{3}R_{\sigma}^{2}/2}\leq C\sigma_{t}^{\beta}e^{-c\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{2}}.

Combining this extension error with the bounds for
E_{\rm loc}
and
I_{\rm tail}
proves
Equations

188
and
189
, because
\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{2}=\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}/\sigma_{t}^{2}
.

Step 7: Hölder regularity of the coefficients.

Each
A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},t)
is a finite linear combination of integrals of terms of the form

e^{-\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}){\bm{w}}}\times\text{polynomial in }{\bm{w}}\times\text{smooth geometric factors in }{\bm{u}}\times\partial^{\alpha}a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}),\qquad|\alpha|\leq q.

The tensor
{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})
is smooth and uniformly positive definite on
K_{i}^{\circ}
, so derivatives falling on the Gaussian factor are dominated by
e^{-c\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}
times a polynomial in
\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}
, uniformly in
{\bm{u}}\in K_{i}^{\circ}
and
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
.
Hence differentiation under the integral is justified up to the order allowed by the amplitude.
Since
a_{i}^{(R)}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}(\phi_{i}(U_{i}),B_{a})
, the worst regularity occurs when
|\alpha|=q
, giving
\partial^{\alpha}a_{i}^{(R)}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta-q}
.
Multiplication by smooth geometric factors and integration against the uniformly dominated Gaussian preserve this regularity.
Therefore

A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}(\cdot,t)\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta-q}(K_{i}^{\circ},C_{\rm coef}),\qquad t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}].

This completes the proof.
∎

Choice of noise-regime thresholds.

All phrases above of the form “after decreasing the small-noise threshold if necessary” are collected in the single threshold
\bar{\sigma}_{\rm Lap}
below.
The branch constants
C_{\rm sm}
and
c_{\rm lg}
are chosen only after this collection is complete, so the small/large-noise cover and its overlap are not affected by the intermediate reductions.
Since the atlas is finite, choose
C_{\rm Lap}^{\max}\geq 1
to dominate the constants in the Laplace remainder bound
Equation

189
, uniformly over all charts
i
and all
R\in\mathcal{R}
.
Also choose
\bar{\sigma}_{\rm Lap}\in(0,1]
small enough that every small-noise requirement invoked in the proof of
Proposition

F.16
holds whenever
0<\sigma_{t}\leq\bar{\sigma}_{\rm Lap}
: in particular
\sigma_{t}R_{\sigma}\leq r_{i}
, the compact-away tail satisfies
\sigma_{t}^{-d}e^{-c/\sigma_{t}^{2}}\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{\beta}
, and the exponential correction obeys
|Z_{\sigma}|\leq 1/2
, uniformly over all charts and integrands.
Such a number exists because the atlas and the retained coefficients are finite and because
\sigma^{-\ell}e^{-c/\sigma^{2}}\to 0
and
\sigma^{\gamma}(\log(e/\sigma))^{M}\to 0
as
\sigma\downarrow 0
for every fixed
\ell,\gamma,M>0
.
From now on, the constants
C_{\rm sm}
and
c_{\rm lg}
in
Definition

D.1
are fixed so that

C_{\rm sm}\leq\bar{\sigma}_{\rm Lap},\qquad C_{\rm Lap}^{\max}C_{\rm sm}^{\beta}\leq\frac{1}{6},\qquad 4c_{\rm lg}\leq C_{\rm sm}.

(196)

Thus, on
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
, the analytic Laplace remainder is bounded by the target order
{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon)
, while
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm lg}(\varepsilon)
still have the overlap needed for branch interpolation.

F.4
Network assembly and final small-noise theorem

After the projection-coordinate networks are available, the remaining neural operations
are algebraic or standard approximation tasks: residuals and normalized normals,
Gaussian factors, Laplace coefficients, certified active-chart gates, and the final
stable ratio. This section assembles those pieces into the small-noise score network.

F.4.1
Residual, normal-coordinate, and Gaussian-factor networks

After the projection network is available, the remaining geometric quantities are algebraic.
The residual is
{\bm{r}}_{i}={\bm{x}}-m_{t}\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t})
, the normalized residual is
{\bm{\nu}}_{i}=\sigma_{t}^{-1}{\bm{r}}_{i}
, and the Gaussian factor is
\exp(-\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}/2)
.
The following lemmas propagate the projection error through these operations and record the resulting network sizes.

Lemma F.17

(ReLU approximation of projection residuals and normalized normals)
.

Fix
i\in\{1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\}
.
Fix a localization parameter
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
such that
Equation

89
holds for every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
.
For
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, define

{\bm{\pi}}({\bm{x}},t):=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}\bigl({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr),\qquad{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t):={\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{\pi}}({\bm{x}},t),\qquad{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t):=\sigma_{t}^{-1}{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t).

Let

C_{\nu,\varepsilon}:=2\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-1}+c_{\star}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}.

For every
\varepsilon_{\rm res}\in(0,1]
, define

C_{\rm res}:=e\vee\bigl(4096\,C_{\rm proj}C_{\nu,\varepsilon}^{2}\bigr),

where
C_{\rm proj}
is the geometry-dependent constant from
Theorem

F.13
.
Then there exist ReLU networks

\widetilde{{\bm{r}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\rm res}},\widetilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\rm res}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to{\mathbb{R}}^{D}

such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)\\
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)\end{subarray}}\bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{r}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\rm res}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm res},

and

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)\\
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)\end{subarray}}\bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\rm res}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm res}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log^{3}\bigl(C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2}\bigr),

(197)

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD(d\vee\beta)\bigl(C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}+A_{{\mathcal{M}}}D\log^{3}\bigl(C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2}\bigr),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+3}\bigl(C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}\log^{2}\bigl(C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2}\bigr)+A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD\log^{5}\bigl(C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2}\bigr),

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}\bigl(C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2}\bigr).

Proof.

Step 1: Active-region bounds.

We work uniformly over
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
.
Since
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
, there exists
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\subset{\mathcal{M}}
such that

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq c_{\star}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}.

Since
{\bm{\pi}}({\bm{x}},t)
is the closest point on
{\mathcal{M}}
to
{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)={\bm{x}}/m_{t}
,

\displaystyle\|{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}

\displaystyle=m_{t}\mathrm{dist}\bigl({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t),{\mathcal{M}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq m_{t}\|{\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{y}}\|_{2}=\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\star}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}.

(198)

Thus

\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}=\sigma_{t}^{-1}\|{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq c_{\star}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}\leq C_{\nu,\varepsilon}.

Also, because
\sigma_{t}\leq 1
,

|r_{i,j}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq c_{\star}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}\leq C_{\nu,\varepsilon}.

Step 2: Approximate the projection center and residual.

Set

\varepsilon_{r}:=\frac{\varepsilon_{\rm res}}{8C_{\nu,\varepsilon}},\qquad\varepsilon_{\rm cen}:=\varepsilon_{r}/8.

By
Theorem

F.13
, applied with localization parameter
\varepsilon
and accuracy
\varepsilon_{\rm cen}
, there is a ReLU network
\widetilde{{\bm{\pi}}}_{\varepsilon_{\rm cen}}
satisfying

\bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{\pi}}}_{\varepsilon_{\rm cen}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{\pi}}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm cen}.

In
Theorem

F.13
, we use the smoothness
2(\beta\vee 1)
of the geometric chart maps, hence its non-logarithmic factor is controlled by

\bigl(C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\rm cen}^{-2}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}.

Since

\varepsilon_{\rm cen}^{-2}=64\varepsilon_{r}^{-2}=4096C_{\nu,\varepsilon}^{2}\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2},

we have

C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\rm cen}^{-2}\leq C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2}.

By
Lemma

I.2
, let
\widetilde{m}(t)
approximate
m_{t}
with

|\widetilde{m}(t)-m_{t}|\leq\varepsilon_{m},\qquad\varepsilon_{m}:=\varepsilon_{r}/8.

For each
j=1,\dots,D
, use
Lemma

I.3
to multiply
\widetilde{m}(t)
and
\widetilde{\pi}_{\varepsilon_{\rm cen},j}({\bm{x}},t)
with multiplication accuracy
\varepsilon_{M,r}:=\varepsilon_{r}/2
.
Since
m_{t}\in[0,1]
and
{\bm{\pi}}({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{M}}\subset[0,1]^{D}
, the true inputs lie in
[-1,1]^{2}
, and the two input errors are at most
\varepsilon_{r}/8
.
Therefore

|\widetilde{h}_{j}({\bm{x}},t)-m_{t}\pi_{j}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq\varepsilon_{M,r}+2\cdot 1\cdot\frac{\varepsilon_{r}}{8}=\frac{3}{4}\varepsilon_{r}\leq\varepsilon_{r}.

Define

\widetilde{r}_{i,j}({\bm{x}},t):=x_{j}-\widetilde{h}_{j}({\bm{x}},t).

Then

|\widetilde{r}_{i,j}({\bm{x}},t)-r_{i,j}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq\varepsilon_{r},

and therefore

\bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{r}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\rm res}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{r}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm res}.

Step 3: Approximate the normalized residual.

Let
\phi_{1/\sigma}(t)
approximate
\sigma_{t}^{-1}
with

|\phi_{1/\sigma}(t)-\sigma_{t}^{-1}|\leq\varepsilon_{\sigma},\qquad\varepsilon_{\sigma}:=\frac{\varepsilon_{\rm res}}{8C_{\nu,\varepsilon}}.

For each
j
, use
Lemma

I.3
to multiply
\phi_{1/\sigma}(t)
and
\widetilde{r}_{i,j}({\bm{x}},t)
with multiplication accuracy
\varepsilon_{M,\nu}:=\varepsilon_{\rm res}/2
.
The true inputs lie in
[-C_{\nu,\varepsilon},C_{\nu,\varepsilon}]^{2}
, and the two input errors are at most
\varepsilon_{\rm res}/(8C_{\nu,\varepsilon})
.
Hence

|\widetilde{\nu}_{i,j}({\bm{x}},t)-\sigma_{t}^{-1}r_{i,j}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq\varepsilon_{M,\nu}+2C_{\nu,\varepsilon}\frac{\varepsilon_{\rm res}}{8C_{\nu,\varepsilon}}=\frac{3}{4}\varepsilon_{\rm res}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm res}.

Thus

\bigl\|\widetilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\rm res}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm res}.

Step 4: Size.

The construction consists of the projection-center network, the schedule networks for
m_{t}
and
\sigma_{t}^{-1}
,
D
product subnetworks for
m_{t}{\bm{\pi}}
,
D
product subnetworks for
\sigma_{t}^{-1}{\bm{r}}_{i}
, and affine subtraction layers.
The center network contributes the bound from
Equation

160
with
C_{\rm proj}D\varepsilon_{\rm cen}^{-2}\leq C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2}
.
The remaining components contribute only logarithmic powers in
C_{\nu,\varepsilon}
,
\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-1}
, and
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-1}
, all bounded by powers of
\log(C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2})
.
Summing the components gives
Equation

197
.
∎

Lemma F.18

(ReLU approximation of the projection-center Gaussian factor)
.

Fix
i\in\{1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\}
.
Fix a localization parameter
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
such that
Equation

89
holds for every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
.
Define

{\mathcal{G}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t):=\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)=\exp\Bigl(-\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr).

Let
C_{\nu,\varepsilon}
and
C_{\rm res}
be as in
Lemma

F.17
.
For every
\varepsilon_{G}\in(0,1]
, define

\varepsilon_{\nu}:=\frac{\varepsilon_{G}}{12D^{1/2}C_{\nu,\varepsilon}},\qquad C_{\rm gau}:=e\vee\Bigl(144D^{2}C_{\rm res}C_{\nu,\varepsilon}^{2}\Bigr).

Then there exists a ReLU network
\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{G}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)\\
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)\end{subarray}}\bigl|\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{G}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{G}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr|\leq\varepsilon_{G}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log^{3}(C_{\rm gau}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2}),

(199)

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD(d\vee\beta)\bigl(C_{\rm gau}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}+A_{{\mathcal{M}}}D\log^{3}(C_{\rm gau}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2}),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+3}\bigl(C_{\rm gau}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}\log^{2}(C_{\rm gau}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2})+A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD\log^{5}(C_{\rm gau}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2}),

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(C_{\rm gau}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2}).

Proof.

Step 1: Approximate
{\bm{\nu}}_{i}
.

Set

s_{i}({\bm{x}},t):=\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2},\qquad{\mathcal{G}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=e^{-s_{i}({\bm{x}},t)}.

By
Lemma

F.17
, applied with accuracy
\varepsilon_{\nu}
, there exists
\widetilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\nu}}
such that

\|\widetilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\nu}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\nu}.

By
Equation

198
,

\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq c_{\star}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}\leq C_{\nu,\varepsilon}.

Step 2: Approximate the exponent.

Let

\varepsilon_{\rm sq}:=\frac{\varepsilon_{G}}{3D}.

For each
\ell=1,\dots,D
, apply
Lemma

I.3
to square
\widetilde{\nu}_{i,\varepsilon_{\nu},\ell}
.
Thus

|\widetilde{q}_{\ell}-\widetilde{\nu}_{i,\varepsilon_{\nu},\ell}^{2}|\leq\varepsilon_{\rm sq}.

Define

\widetilde{s}_{i}({\bm{x}},t):=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{D}\widetilde{q}_{\ell}({\bm{x}},t).

The active residual bound is in
\ell_{2}
, not merely coordinatewise. Hence

\|\widetilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\nu}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq D^{1/2}\varepsilon_{\nu}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{G}}{12C_{\nu,\varepsilon}}.

Using

\left|\|\widetilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\nu}}\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right|\leq\|\widetilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\nu}}-{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}\left(2\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}+\|\widetilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{\nu}}-{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}\right),

and
C_{\nu,\varepsilon}\geq 1
, the perturbation error in the exact squared norm is
at most
\varepsilon_{G}/4
.
Then

|\widetilde{s}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)-s_{i}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq\frac{D}{2}\varepsilon_{\rm sq}+\frac{\varepsilon_{G}}{4}\leq\frac{5\varepsilon_{G}}{12}.

Step 3: Approximate the exponential.

Set

\widetilde{s}_{i}^{+}({\bm{x}},t):={\mathsf{ReLU}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)).

Since
s_{i}\geq 0
and
{\mathsf{ReLU}}
is
1
-Lipschitz,

|\widetilde{s}_{i}^{+}({\bm{x}},t)-s_{i}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq\frac{5\varepsilon_{G}}{12}.

Apply
Lemma

I.5
with accuracy
\varepsilon_{\exp}:=\varepsilon_{G}/3
and define

\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{G}}({\bm{x}},t):=\phi_{\exp}(\widetilde{s}_{i}^{+}({\bm{x}},t)).

Then

|\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{G}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\mathcal{G}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq\varepsilon_{\exp}+|\widetilde{s}_{i}^{+}({\bm{x}},t)-s_{i}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq\varepsilon_{G}.

Step 4: Network size.

Since

C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\nu}^{-2}=144D^{2}C_{\rm res}C_{\nu,\varepsilon}^{2}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2}\leq C_{\rm gau}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2},

the normal-residual network has the size from
Equation

197
with
C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\rm res}^{-2}
replaced by
C_{\rm gau}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2}
.
The square subnetworks and the exponential network add only logarithmic powers in
C_{\rm gau}\varepsilon_{G}^{-2}
, and the summation and ReLU gate are exact.
Therefore
Equation

199
follows.
∎

F.4.2
Laplace coefficient networks on the active branch

The Laplace coefficients are the only factors in the small-noise expansion that depend on the density regularity.
We first factor each coefficient into schedule powers and Hölder functions of the projection coordinate, extending the latter to a buffered box while preserving the chart-support property.
Then we approximate these coefficient functions and compose them with the coordinate projection network.
The weighted corollary includes the powers of
\sigma_{t}
and
{\bm{\nu}}_{i}
that multiply each coefficient in the Laplace sum.

Lemma F.19

(Factorization and extension of the Laplace coefficient functions)
.

Fix
i\in\{1,\dots,C_{{\mathcal{M}}}\}
,
R\in\mathcal{R}
,
0\leq q\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor
, and
{\bm{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{D}
with
\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q
.
Assume
\beta-q>0
.
Let
K_{i}^{\circ}
and
\Box_{i}
be the buffered coordinate boxes fixed in
Equations

20
and
21
.
For every intermediate box
\Box_{i}^{A}
with
K_{i}^{\circ}\Subset\Box_{i}^{A}\Subset\Box_{i}
, there exist a nonempty finite set
\mathcal{E}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}
, integers
r_{e}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and functions

B_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},e}^{(R)}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta-q}(\Box_{i}^{A},C_{A,q}),\qquad e\in\mathcal{E}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)},

such that the function

\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},t):=\sum_{e\in\mathcal{E}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}}m_{t}^{r_{e}}B_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},e}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}),\qquad({\bm{u}},t)\in\Box_{i}^{A}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}],

(200)

is an extension of
A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}
:

\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},t)=A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},t),\qquad({\bm{u}},t)\in K_{i}^{\circ}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}].

(201)

With
S_{i}^{(R)}:=\mathsf{supp}a_{i}^{(R)}
, the extension can be chosen so that

\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},t)=0,\qquad({\bm{u}},t)\in\bigl(\Box_{i}^{A}\setminus S_{i}^{(R)}\bigr)\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}].

(202)

Moreover,

\sup_{t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}\left\|\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}(\cdot,t)\right\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta-q}(\Box_{i}^{A})}\leq C_{A,q}.

(203)

The constants
|\mathcal{E}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}|
,
\max_{e}|r_{e}|
, and
C_{A,q}
are finite and depend only on
i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},R,\beta,B_{a},\underline{m},\overline{m}
, and the geometric constants.

Proof.

By
Equation

177
, every
\Psi_{i,\ell}
is the sum of a term linear in
{\bm{\nu}}
with coefficient
m_{t}
and a term independent of
{\bm{\nu}}
with coefficient
m_{t}^{2}
.
Hence each coefficient of
{\bm{\nu}}^{{\bm{\lambda}}}
in
{\mathcal{E}}_{i,\ell}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{i,q}^{(R)}
is a finite sum of terms of the form

m_{t}^{a}P({\bm{w}})H({\bm{u}})\partial^{\alpha}a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}),\qquad|\alpha|\leq q,

where
a
is an integer,
P
is a polynomial in
{\bm{w}}
, and
H
is a smooth geometric factor on
\Box_{i}
.
Therefore
A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}
is a finite sum of integrals

m_{t}^{a}H({\bm{u}})\partial^{\alpha}a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}})\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}e^{-\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}){\bm{w}}}{\bm{w}}^{\rho}{\mathrm{d}}{\bm{w}},

(204)

with
|\alpha|\leq q
and finite multi-indices
\rho
.

Since
{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})
is smooth and uniformly positive definite on
\Box_{i}
, the Gaussian moment formula gives

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}e^{-\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}){\bm{w}}}{\bm{w}}^{\rho}{\mathrm{d}}{\bm{w}}=m_{t}^{-d-|\rho|}(2\pi)^{d/2}\det({\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}))^{-1/2}P_{\rho}({\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}})^{-1}),

with the usual convention that odd moments vanish.
Substituting this identity into
Equation

204
gives an explicit finite formula for the coefficient on
K_{i}^{\circ}
.
Fix the stated intermediate box
\Box_{i}^{A}
, and use the same explicit Gaussian-moment formula to define the extension on all of
\Box_{i}^{A}
.
Equivalently, after collecting the finitely many powers of
m_{t}
, define
B_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},e}^{(R)}
directly on
\Box_{i}^{A}
as the corresponding finite sums of products of smooth geometric factors and derivatives
\partial^{\alpha}a_{i}^{(R)}
,
|\alpha|\leq q
.
If a coefficient is identically zero, take
\mathcal{E}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}
to be a singleton with
r_{e}=0
and
B_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},e}^{(R)}\equiv 0
.

Since
a_{i}^{(R)}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}
on the coordinate domain and the geometric factors are smooth on
\Box_{i}
, each directly defined
B_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},e}^{(R)}
belongs to
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta-q}(\Box_{i}^{A})
.
The formula agrees with the original coefficient on
K_{i}^{\circ}
, giving
Equation

201
.
Moreover, if
{\bm{u}}\notin S_{i}^{(R)}=\mathsf{supp}a_{i}^{(R)}
, then
a_{i}^{(R)}
vanishes in a neighborhood of
{\bm{u}}
, and all derivatives
\partial^{\alpha}a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}})
with
|\alpha|\leq q
vanish.
The directly defined Gaussian-moment formula is therefore zero at
{\bm{u}}
, which proves
Equation

202
.
Taking
C_{A,q}
to dominate the finitely many
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta-q}
-norms of the directly defined factors proves
Equation

203
and the stated dependence of the constants.
∎

Lemma F.20

(ReLU approximation of the Laplace coefficient functions)
.

Fix
i,R,q,{\bm{\lambda}}
as in
Lemma

F.19
, and assume
\beta-q>0
.
Let
\Box_{i}^{A}
,
\mathcal{E}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}
,
r_{e}
,
B_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},e}^{(R)}
, and
\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}
be the objects supplied by
Lemma

F.19
.
For every
0<\varepsilon_{A}\leq 1/2
, there is a ReLU network

\widetilde{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},\varepsilon_{A}}^{(R)}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to{\mathbb{R}}

such that

\sup_{({\bm{u}},t)\in\Box_{i}^{A}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}\left|\widetilde{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},\varepsilon_{A}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},t)-\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}},t)\right|\leq\varepsilon_{A}.

(205)

Moreover, for a finite constant
C_{A,{\rm nn}}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle L,\log B

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee(\beta-q)))\log^{2}(C_{A,{\rm nn}}\varepsilon_{A}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim(d\vee(\beta-q))(C_{A,{\rm nn}}\varepsilon_{A}^{-1})^{d/(\beta-q)}+\log^{3}(C_{A,{\rm nn}}\varepsilon_{A}^{-1}),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\lesssim(d+\beta-q+2)^{d+4}(C_{A,{\rm nn}}\varepsilon_{A}^{-1})^{d/(\beta-q)}\log(C_{A,{\rm nn}}\varepsilon_{A}^{-1})+\log^{4}(C_{A,{\rm nn}}\varepsilon_{A}^{-1}).

(206)

Thus the non-logarithmic coefficient cost is
(\varepsilon_{A}^{-1})^{d/(\beta-q)}
.

Proof.

Let

N_{A}:=|\mathcal{E}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}|,\qquad r_{A}:=\max_{e\in\mathcal{E}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}}|r_{e}|,\qquad M_{A}:=1\vee\underline{m}^{-r_{A}}\vee\overline{m}^{r_{A}}.

For each
e
, apply
Theorem

I.1
, after affine rescaling of
\Box_{i}^{A}
to
[0,1]^{d}
, to approximate
B_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},e}^{(R)}
to accuracy

\varepsilon_{B}:=\frac{\varepsilon_{A}}{8N_{A}M_{A}}.

This gives the intrinsic cost
(C\varepsilon_{A}^{-1})^{d/(\beta-q)}
, up to logarithmic factors.

For the one-dimensional schedule factor
m_{t}^{r_{e}}
, use the following three cases.
If
r_{e}=0
, represent
m_{t}^{r_{e}}
by the constant-one network.
If
r_{e}>0
, approximate
m_{t}
by
Lemma

I.2
and form the fixed positive power by
Lemma

I.3
.
If
r_{e}<0
, approximate
1/m_{t}
by
Lemma

I.6
and form the fixed positive power
(1/m_{t})^{-r_{e}}
by
Lemma

I.3
.
Because
r_{A}
is fixed and
m_{t}\in[\underline{m},\overline{m}]
, this schedule subnetwork has only logarithmic dependence on
\varepsilon_{A}^{-1}
, and can be made accurate enough that the error in each product

m_{t}^{r_{e}}B_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},e}^{(R)}({\bm{u}})

is at most
\varepsilon_{A}/(2N_{A})
uniformly on
\Box_{i}^{A}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
.
Summing the
N_{A}
products in an affine output layer yields
Equation

205
.
Adding the intrinsic Hölder-approximation cost, the logarithmic schedule cost, and the fixed product/summation overhead gives
Equation

206
.
∎

Corollary F.21

(Weighted coefficient approximation on the active branch)
.

Fix
i,R,q,{\bm{\lambda}}
as in
Lemma

F.19
, and assume
\beta-q>0
.
Let
0<\varepsilon\leq 1/2
be a localization parameter for which
Equation

89
holds on
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
.
Assume the small-noise branch satisfies

\sigma_{t}\leq\sigma_{\star}(\varepsilon)\qquad\text{for every }t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon),

For every
0<\varepsilon_{\rm term}\leq 1/2
, define

\varepsilon_{A,q}:=\frac{1}{2}\wedge c_{A,q}\,\varepsilon_{\rm term}\,\sigma_{\star}(\varepsilon)^{-q}\,\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{-q/2},

(207)

with
c_{A,q}>0
sufficiently small.
Then there is a ReLU network

\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},\varepsilon_{A,q}}^{(R)}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to{\mathbb{R}}

with

\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},\varepsilon_{A,q}}^{(R)}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L_{\mathcal{A}},{\bm{W}}_{\mathcal{A}},S_{\mathcal{A}},B_{\mathcal{A}})

such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)\\
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)\end{subarray}}\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},\varepsilon_{A,q}}^{(R)}({\bm{x}},t)-A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),t\bigr)\right|\leq\varepsilon_{A,q}.

(208)

Consequently,

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)\\
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)\end{subarray}}\sigma_{t}^{q}|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)^{{\bm{\lambda}}}|\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},\varepsilon_{A,q}}^{(R)}({\bm{x}},t)-A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),t\bigr)\right|\leq\varepsilon_{\rm term}.

(209)

Let
\alpha_{q}:=(\beta-q)\wedge 1
.
There is a finite constant
C_{\mathcal{A},q}\geq 1
, depending only on the fixed chart, geometric, schedule, and coefficient constants, such that

\displaystyle L_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log^{3}\!\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}D\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-2/\alpha_{q}}\bigr)+\log(e(d\vee(\beta-q)))\log^{2}\!\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-1}\bigr),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}_{\mathcal{A}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD(d\vee\beta)\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}D\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-2/\alpha_{q}}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}+A_{{\mathcal{M}}}D\log^{3}\!\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}D\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-2/\alpha_{q}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+(d\vee(\beta-q))\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-1}\bigr)^{d/(\beta-q)}+\log^{3}\!\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-1}\bigr),

\displaystyle S_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+3}\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}D\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-2/\alpha_{q}}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}\log^{2}\!\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}D\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-2/\alpha_{q}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+A_{{\mathcal{M}}}dD\log^{5}\!\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}D\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-2/\alpha_{q}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+(d+\beta-q+2)^{d+4}\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-1}\bigr)^{d/(\beta-q)}\log\!\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-1}\bigr)+\log^{4}\!\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-1}\bigr),

\displaystyle\log B_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}\!\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}D\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-2/\alpha_{q}}\bigr)+\log^{2}\!\bigl(C_{\mathcal{A},q}\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-1}\bigr).

(210)

In particular, the total non-logarithmic size is bounded, up to logarithmic factors and fixed constants, by

\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-d/(\beta-q)}+D^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-d/(\alpha_{q}(\beta\vee 1))}.

(211)

If
\sigma_{\star}(\varepsilon)\asymp\varepsilon^{1/\beta}
and
\varepsilon_{\rm term}\asymp\varepsilon
, then the two
\varepsilon
-dependent powers in
Equation

211
are bounded by

\lesssim\varepsilon^{-d/\beta}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{C_{\beta,q}},

for a finite exponent
C_{\beta,q}
.
Hence the composed coefficient approximation does not worsen the intrinsic
\varepsilon^{-d/\beta}
rate, up to logarithmic factors and the displayed polynomial ambient factor.

Proof.

Use the fixed buffered box
K_{i}^{\circ}
and the intermediate box
\Box_{i}^{A}
from
Lemma

F.19
.
Let

\alpha_{q}:=(\beta-q)\wedge 1.

Apply
Corollary

F.14
with localization parameter
\varepsilon
and coordinate accuracy

\varepsilon_{u}:=c\varepsilon_{A,q}^{1/\alpha_{q}},

where the constant
c>0
is chosen small enough that the coordinate output remains in
\Box_{i}^{A}
whenever the true coordinate
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)
lies in
K_{i}^{\circ}
.
This uses the fixed positive margin
\mathrm{dist}(K_{i}^{\circ},\partial\Box_{i}^{A})
.
Denote the resulting coordinate network by
\widetilde{{\bm{u}}}_{i,\varepsilon_{u}}^{\Pi,\varepsilon}
.
Apply
Lemma

F.20
with accuracy
\varepsilon_{A,q}/2
, and compose the resulting coefficient network with this coordinate network.
Since

\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}(\cdot,t)\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta-q}(\Box_{i}^{A},C_{A,q})

uniformly in
t
, it is
\alpha_{q}
-Hölder uniformly in
t
.
Thus the coordinate error contributes at most
C_{A,q}\varepsilon_{u}^{\alpha_{q}}
, which is at most
\varepsilon_{A,q}/2
by the choice of
c
.
The intrinsic coefficient-network error contributes the other
\varepsilon_{A,q}/2
.
Since
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\in K_{i}^{\circ}
, the extension identity
Equation

201
gives

\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),t\bigr)=A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),t\bigr),

which proves
Equation

208
.

On
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)
,
Equation

198
gives

\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}.

Since
\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q
, this implies

|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)^{{\bm{\lambda}}}|\leq C_{q}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{q/2}.

Together with
\sigma_{t}\leq\sigma_{\star}(\varepsilon)
and
\varepsilon_{A,q}\leq c_{A,q}\varepsilon_{\rm term}\sigma_{\star}(\varepsilon)^{-q}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{-q/2}
, this yields

\sigma_{t}^{q}|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}^{{\bm{\lambda}}}|\varepsilon_{A,q}\leq C_{q}\sigma_{\star}(\varepsilon)^{q}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{q/2}c_{A,q}\varepsilon_{\rm term}\sigma_{\star}(\varepsilon)^{-q}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{-q/2}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm term}

after choosing
c_{A,q}
small enough.
This proves
Equation

209
.

The coefficient network contributes the terms in
Equation

206
with accuracy
\varepsilon_{A,q}/2
.
The coordinate network is obtained from
Corollary

F.14
with accuracy
\varepsilon_{u}=c\varepsilon_{A,q}^{1/\alpha_{q}}
; substituting this value into
Equation

175
gives the terms with scale
D\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-2/\alpha_{q}}
.
The final composition and the finite summations change the displayed quantities only by fixed constants.
After increasing
C_{\mathcal{A},q}
, this proves
Equation

210
.
In particular, the coordinate network’s non-logarithmic contribution is

(D\varepsilon_{u}^{-2})^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}\lesssim D^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-d/(\alpha_{q}(\beta\vee 1))}.

Together with the coefficient contribution
\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-d/(\beta-q)}
, this proves
Equation

211
.

Finally, since
\sigma_{\star}(\varepsilon)\asymp\varepsilon^{1/\beta}
and
\varepsilon_{\rm term}\asymp\varepsilon
, for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
, the minimum in
Equation

207
is inactive and

\varepsilon_{A,q}\asymp\varepsilon^{1-q/\beta}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{-q/2}=\varepsilon^{(\beta-q)/\beta}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{-q/2}.

Substituting this relation into the first term of
Equation

211
gives

\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-d/(\beta-q)}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-d/\beta}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{qd/(2(\beta-q))}.

For the second term, if
\beta-q\geq 1
, then
\alpha_{q}=1
and

\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-d/(\alpha_{q}(\beta\vee 1))}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-d(\beta-q)/(\beta(\beta\vee 1))}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{qd/(2(\beta\vee 1))}\leq\varepsilon^{-d/\beta}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{qd/(2(\beta\vee 1))},

because
\beta-q\leq\beta\vee 1
.
If
\beta-q<1
, then
\alpha_{q}=\beta-q
, and

\varepsilon_{A,q}^{-d/(\alpha_{q}(\beta\vee 1))}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-d/(\beta(\beta\vee 1))}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{qd/(2(\beta-q)(\beta\vee 1))}\leq\varepsilon^{-d/\beta}\bigl(\log(e/\varepsilon)\bigr)^{qd/(2(\beta-q)(\beta\vee 1))},

because
\beta\vee 1\geq 1
.
The displayed polylogarithmic rate follows.
∎

Corollary F.22

(Active-branch component approximation on an enlarged chart radius)
.

Fix
c_{a}>0
.
Assume the
c_{a}
-active localization condition

\delta_{\rm act}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon,t)<\eta_{\Pi}/2,\qquad t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon),

holds.
Then, for every chart
i
, on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
, the residual bound

\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq c_{a}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}

holds, and the following fixed-
c_{a}
approximation statements are valid.
Their error bounds and network-size orders are the same as in the cited default statements, with constants allowed to depend on the fixed number
c_{a}
, and with the replacement

{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon)\mapsto{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon),\qquad{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}(\varepsilon)\mapsto{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon),\qquad C_{\nu,\varepsilon}\mapsto 2\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-1}+c_{a}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}.

(i)

The buffered-chart and reach-tube conclusions of
Lemmas

F.4
and
F.11
hold on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
.
In particular
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\in K_{i}^{\circ}
there.

(ii)

For every coordinate accuracy
\varepsilon_{u}
, the chart-coordinate network in
Corollary

F.14
can be chosen so that its error bound holds uniformly on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
.

(iii)

Theorem

F.13
has the same statement on the enlarged active union
{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
.

(iv)

The residual, normal, Gaussian-factor, and weighted-coefficient approximation statements in
Lemmas

F.17
,
F.18
and
F.21
hold uniformly on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
.

For fixed
c_{a}
, the polynomial and logarithmic orders in all displayed network sizes are unchanged.

Proof.

For the buffered-chart and reach-tube statements, the original proofs use the active-set
definition only through the distance estimate

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq c_{a}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)},\qquad{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i},

and the resulting reach/local-coordinate condition

\delta_{\rm act}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon,t)<\eta_{\Pi}/2
.
The assumed
c_{a}
-active localization condition therefore gives the same projected
coordinate, buffered-domain inclusion, and uniqueness conclusions on

{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
.
The residual estimate becomes

\|{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq c_{a}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)},\qquad\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq c_{a}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}.

Thus every place where
C_{\nu,\varepsilon}
appears may use
2\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-1}+c_{a}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}
.
For the global projection theorem, the original argument uses only the following two facts: an active pair
({\bm{x}},t)
supplies a chart
i_{\star}
for which the exact projected coordinate lies in
K_{i_{\star}}^{\circ}
, and all other candidates are separated by the same objective gap outside the accepted near-minimizer set.
Both facts hold with
{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
in place of
{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}(\varepsilon)
, because
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i_{\star}}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
and the enlarged buffered-chart conclusion gives the good coordinate in
K_{i_{\star}}^{\circ}
.
Thus the minimizer-selection and error estimates of
Theorem

F.13
hold on
{\mathcal{K}}_{\rm act}^{(c_{a})}(\varepsilon)
with only fixed-
c_{a}
changes in constants.
This proves the fixed-
c_{a}
version of
Theorem

F.13
.
Composing that fixed-
c_{a}
projection-center network with the affine extension of the chart map
\phi_{i}
and the exact box clipping layer gives the fixed-
c_{a}
version of
Corollary

F.14
.

The proofs of the residual and Gaussian-factor lemmas then use this fixed-
c_{a}
global projection theorem in place of the default one, together with the enlarged residual bound displayed above.
The weighted coefficient corollary uses the fixed-
c_{a}
chart-coordinate network and the same coefficient extension.
For fixed
c_{a}
, the replacement only changes constants and powers of the same logarithmic factor.
The constructions, error allocations, and network sizes are otherwise identical.
∎

Corollary F.23

(
H
-active component approximation)
.

Fix
c_{a}>0
,
H\geq 1
, and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1/2
.
Assume
[t_{0},t_{1}]\subset{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and

c_{a}\frac{\sigma_{t}}{m_{t}}\sqrt{H}\leq\eta_{\Pi}/2,\qquad t\in[t_{0},t_{1}].

Set
\sigma_{H}^{\star}:=\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\sigma_{t}
, so
\sigma_{H}^{\star}\leq C_{\rm sm}\varepsilon^{1/\beta}
.
For

A_{t}(H):=\{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\leq C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}\},

define

{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}:=\left\{({\bm{x}},t):{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}(H),\ \exists{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\text{ with }\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq c_{a}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}\right\}.

Then, for every chart
i
, on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}
the projection
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t})
is unique, the projected coordinate satisfies
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\in K_{i}^{\circ}
, and

\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq c_{a}\sqrt{H}.

Moreover, the component approximation statements in
Corollaries

F.14
,
F.13
,
F.17
,
F.18
and
F.21
have
H
-active versions on the sets
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}
and
\bigcup_{i}{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}
.
The corresponding size bounds are obtained from the cited bounds by replacing
C_{\nu,\varepsilon}
with
2\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-1}+c_{a}\sqrt{H}
and by replacing each logarithmic scale
\log(C\varepsilon^{-1})
with
H+\log(C\varepsilon^{-1})
.
More explicitly, for the weighted-coefficient statement, fix the same indices
i,R,q,{\bm{\lambda}}
and the same term accuracy
0<\varepsilon_{\rm term}\leq 1/2
as in
Corollary

F.21
.
In the
H
-active version of that corollary, the coefficient tolerance is

\varepsilon_{A,q}^{H}:=\frac{1}{2}\wedge c_{A,q}\varepsilon_{\rm term}(\sigma_{H}^{\star})^{-q}H^{-q/2}.

With this replacement, the weighted error bound in
Equation

209
holds on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}
with
\varepsilon_{A,q}
replaced by
\varepsilon_{A,q}^{H}
.
For fixed
c_{a}
, these replacements preserve the same non-logarithmic intrinsic costs and introduce only additional fixed polynomial powers of
H
.

Proof.

The proof of
Corollary

F.22
depends on the active set only through the reach condition and the residual bound.
The assumed
H
-active reach condition gives uniqueness of the metric projection, buffered-coordinate inclusion, and the Gauss–Newton contraction exactly as before, with
\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon)}
replaced by
\sqrt{H}
.
The residual bound follows from the active-distance inequality in the same way as in
Equation

198
.
The chart-coordinate network, global projection network, residual network, and Gaussian-factor network use this radius only through the scalar ranges for products, reciprocals, ramps, and squared norms.
Thus their proofs carry over with
C_{\nu,\varepsilon}
replaced by
2\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-1}+c_{a}\sqrt{H}
and with logarithmic factors enlarged to
H+\log(C\varepsilon^{-1})
.
For the coefficient composition, the only changed analytic estimate is
|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}^{{\bm{\lambda}}}|\leq C_{q}H^{q/2}
when
\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q
.
The definition of
\varepsilon_{A,q}^{H}
and the bound
\sigma_{t}\leq\sigma_{H}^{\star}
give the same weighted error proof as
Corollary

F.21
.
The intrinsic Hölder approximation exponents are unchanged, and the new powers of
H
are absorbed into the stated logarithmic-scale replacement.
∎

Lemma F.24

(Certified
H
-active chart gate from objective weights)
.

Fix a chart index
i
, a radius multiplier
c_{a}\geq c_{\star}
,
H\geq 1
,
0<\varepsilon\leq 1/2
, and a coordinate tolerance
0<\eta\leq 1
.
Let
[t_{0},t_{1}]\subset{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon)
and assume

c_{a}\frac{\sigma_{t}}{m_{t}}\sqrt{H}\leq\eta_{\Pi}/2,\qquad t\in[t_{0},t_{1}].

Set
A_{t}(H):=\{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\leq C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}\}
.
Define
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}
from this
A_{t}(H)
as in
Corollary

F.23
.
Let
\Box_{i}^{A}
be any fixed coordinate box with
K_{i}^{\circ}\Subset\Box_{i}^{A}\Subset\Box_{i}
.
Then there exist ReLU networks

\Gamma_{i}^{H}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to[0,1],\qquad\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to\Box_{i},\qquad\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}^{A}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to\Box_{i}^{A},

with the following properties, uniformly for
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
and
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}(H)
.
If
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}
, then

\Gamma_{i}^{H}({\bm{x}},t)=1,\qquad\|\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq\eta,\qquad\|\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}^{A}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq\eta.

(212)

If
\Gamma_{i}^{H}({\bm{x}},t)>0
, then
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t})\in U_{i}
and, with
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t):=\phi_{i}(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t}))
,

\|\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq\eta.

(213)

The network
\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}^{A}
is the exact coordinatewise clipping of
\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}
to
\Box_{i}^{A}
.
The sizes are bounded by the
H
-active global projection-objective construction with projection accuracy
c\eta
and with every logarithmic scale enlarged to
H+\log(C\varepsilon^{-1})
; in particular, this gate adds no non-logarithmic factor beyond the intrinsic projection-objective cost
\eta^{-d/(\beta\vee 1)}
and fixed-atlas arithmetic.

Proof.

We repeat the objective-weight part of the proof of
Theorem

F.13
, using the
H
-active extension from
Corollary

F.23
, but we keep the chart identity of each candidate.
Let

{\mathcal{J}}:=\{(j,{\bm{a}}):1\leq j\leq C_{{\mathcal{M}}},\ {\bm{a}}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{j}^{\rm anc}\},\qquad N_{\rm cand}:=|{\mathcal{J}}|.

Choose an ambient candidate accuracy
\varepsilon_{\rm cert}=c_{\rm cert}\eta
, where the fixed geometric constant
c_{\rm cert}>0
will be decreased several times below.
For each
(j,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}
, form the anchored coordinate candidate
\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{j,{\bm{a}}}
, the manifold candidate
{\bm{y}}_{j,{\bm{a}}}:={\bm{z}}_{j}(\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{j,{\bm{a}}})
, and an objective network
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{j,{\bm{a}}}
approximating
\|{\bm{x}}/m_{t}-{\bm{y}}_{j,{\bm{a}}}\|_{2}^{2}
.
The coordinate candidates are box-clipped by construction, so all
{\bm{y}}_{j,{\bm{a}}}
and all objective networks are well-defined on the whole set
A_{t}(H)
, even when chart
j
is not the active chart.
The objective and arithmetic tolerances are chosen so that

|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{j,{\bm{a}}}-\|{\bm{x}}/m_{t}-{\bm{y}}_{j,{\bm{a}}}\|_{2}^{2}|\leq\gamma/64,\qquad\gamma:=\varepsilon_{\rm cert}^{2},

(214)

uniformly on
A_{t}(H)
.
Because
c_{a}\geq c_{\star}\geq C_{\varrho}
and the chart supports cover
{\mathcal{M}}
, every
({\bm{x}},t)\in A_{t}(H)
belongs to at least one
H
-active chart set
{\mathcal{K}}_{j}^{(c_{a},H)}
.
For such a chart,
Corollary

F.23
gives
{\bm{u}}_{j}^{\Pi}\in K_{j}^{\circ}
, and
Lemma

F.7
supplies an anchor whose local Gauss–Newton candidate is within
{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon_{\rm cert})
of the nearest projection.
After decreasing
c_{\rm cert}
, the corresponding exact objective is at most
\gamma/64
above the true minimum
\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}}/m_{t},{\mathcal{M}})^{2}
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\min}:=\min_{(j,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{j,{\bm{a}}}
and define

\omega_{j,{\bm{a}}}:={\mathsf{ReLU}}\!\left(\gamma-\bigl(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{j,{\bm{a}}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\min}\bigr)\right),\qquad W_{\rm glob}:=\sum_{(j,{\bm{a}})\in{\mathcal{J}}}\omega_{j,{\bm{a}}},\qquad\Omega_{i}:=\sum_{{\bm{a}}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\rm anc}}\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}}.

At least one candidate attains
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\min}
, hence

\gamma\leq W_{\rm glob}\leq N_{\rm cand}\gamma.

(215)

If
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}
, the good chart-
i
candidate described above satisfies

\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{i,{\bm{a}}_{\star}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\min}\leq\frac{3\gamma}{64},

because
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{\min}
is not below the true minimum by more than
\gamma/64
.
Therefore
\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}_{\star}}\geq 61\gamma/64
, and
Equation

215
gives

\frac{\Omega_{i}}{W_{\rm glob}}\geq\tau_{0},\qquad\tau_{0}:=\frac{1}{2N_{\rm cand}}.

(216)

Conversely, suppose
\Omega_{i}>0
.
Then some chart-
i
candidate has positive weight.
The same calculation as
Equations

171
and
172
, with the tolerances in
Equation

214
, implies

\|{\bm{y}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)-\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t})\|_{2}\leq C\varepsilon_{\rm cert}

(217)

for every positive-weight chart-
i
candidate.
Let

\delta_{i}^{U}:=\mathrm{dist}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\bigl({\bm{z}}_{i}(\Box_{i}),{\mathcal{M}}\setminus U_{i}\bigr)>0,

with the convention
\mathrm{dist}(A,\emptyset)=+\infty
.
After decreasing
c_{\rm cert}
so that
C\varepsilon_{\rm cert}<\delta_{i}^{U}/2
,
Equation

217
forces
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t})\in U_{i}
whenever
\Omega_{i}>0
.
The inverse chart is Lipschitz on the compact set
\phi_{i}^{-1}(\Box_{i})
, so every positive-weight chart-
i
coordinate candidate satisfies

\|\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq C_{i}\varepsilon_{\rm cert}.

(218)

Define the ideal chart coordinate average

{\bm{u}}_{i,{\rm id}}({\bm{x}},t):=\frac{\sum_{{\bm{a}}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{i}^{\rm anc}}\omega_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{i,{\bm{a}}}({\bm{x}},t)}{\Omega_{i}({\bm{x}},t)}

whenever
\Omega_{i}>0
.
If
\Omega_{i}/W_{\rm glob}\geq\tau_{0}/32
, then
Equation

215
gives
\Omega_{i}\geq\tau_{0}\gamma/32
, and
Equation

218
gives
\|{\bm{u}}_{i,{\rm id}}-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{i}\varepsilon_{\rm cert}
.
Implement the denominator as
\Omega_{i}\vee(\tau_{0}\gamma/64)
, approximate the reciprocal and products to error at most
c\eta
, and apply the exact clipping map
\Pi_{i}^{(\rm box)}
as the final layer.
After decreasing
c_{\rm cert}
, the implemented coordinate
\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}
maps into
\Box_{i}
and satisfies
Equation

213
whenever the gate below is nonzero, because the clipping is
1
-Lipschitz and fixes
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}
.

It remains only to construct the binary-safe chart gate.
Approximate
\Omega_{i}/W_{\rm glob}
with error at most
\tau_{0}/32
on the interval in
Equation

215
.
Let
\theta_{\rm cert}:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
be the ReLU ramp that is zero on
(-\infty,\tau_{0}/16]
and one on
[\tau_{0}/2,\infty)
, and define
\Gamma_{i}^{H}
by applying
\theta_{\rm cert}
to this implemented ratio.
If
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}
, then
Equation

216
and the ratio error imply that the ramp input is at least
\tau_{0}/2
, so
\Gamma_{i}^{H}=1
.
If
\Gamma_{i}^{H}>0
, then the exact ratio is positive and in fact
\Omega_{i}/W_{\rm glob}\geq\tau_{0}/32
, so the clipped denominator in the coordinate average is inactive and the coordinate certification above applies.
Finally, define
\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}^{A}
by exact coordinatewise clipping of
\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}
to
\Box_{i}^{A}
.
On
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}
, the true coordinate lies in
K_{i}^{\circ}\Subset\Box_{i}^{A}
, and the coordinatewise clipping is
1
-Lipschitz and fixes this true coordinate.
All added operations are finite sums, minima, ReLU ramps, clipped reciprocals, products, and coordinatewise clipping, so their costs are logarithmic or fixed-atlas costs on top of the
H
-active projection-objective networks.
This proves the lemma.
∎

F.4.3
Projection–Laplace chart approximation for small noise

We first state the chart-level projection–Laplace approximation used by the density-lower-bound small-noise construction.
The proposition below is deliberately local: it certifies the neural Laplace expansion only on the enlarged chart-active set where the chart coordinate and residual subnetworks have proved error bounds.
Outside the default chart-active set, the true chart integral is already small by the inactive-tail lemma; the final de-Gaussianized construction below adds a separate certified chart gate before using inactive-chart neural outputs.
This separation keeps the proof auditable because no local chart-coordinate network is used outside the region where its approximation guarantee is available.

Proposition F.25

(Projection–Laplace chart approximation for small noise)
.

Assume
1
and
2
.
Let
0<\varepsilon_{\rm sm}\leq 1/2
, and assume the localization condition
Equation

89
, as well as the same condition with
3c_{\star}
in place of
c_{\star}
, holds for every
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
.
For every chart
i
and every
R\in\mathcal{R}
, there exists a ReLU network
\widetilde{R}_{i}^{\rm sm}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})\\
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(3c_{\star})}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})\end{subarray}}\left|R_{i}({\bm{x}},t)-\widetilde{R}_{i}^{\rm sm}({\bm{x}},t)\right|\leq\varepsilon_{\rm sm}.

(219)

In addition, the true inactive tail satisfies

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})\\
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t},\;({\bm{x}},t)\notin{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})\end{subarray}}|R_{i}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq\varepsilon_{\rm sm}/6.

(220)

Moreover, let
C_{\rm size,sm}\geq e
be a fixed constant large enough to absorb the chart, schedule, coefficient, and arithmetic constants, and define

\Lambda_{\rm sm}:=e\vee C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}\vee{\underline{t}}^{-1}.

(221)

For finite exponents
\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}
depending only on
(\beta,d)
, the same construction can be chosen so that, up to fixed geometry-dependent constants,

L+\log B\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log^{3}\Lambda_{\rm sm},\qquad\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}+S\lesssim D^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}}A_{{\mathcal{M}}}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-d/\beta}\log^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}\Lambda_{\rm sm}.

(222)

The expanded depth, width, sparsity, and log-weight bookkeeping is recorded in
Lemma

F.26
.

Lemma F.26

(Small-noise chart size bookkeeping)
.

Work in the setting of
Proposition

F.25
.
For
0\leq q\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor
, write
\alpha_{q}:=(\beta-q)\wedge 1
.
There is a finite exponent
\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}
, depending only on
(\beta,d)
, such that the network in
Proposition

F.25
may be chosen so that the following bounds hold.
More explicitly, one may take

\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}=\left\lceil\frac{\lfloor\beta\rfloor\,d}{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}+\frac{d}{\beta\vee 1}+3\right\rceil.

In the display below, every occurrence of
\log(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1})
may equivalently be replaced by
\log\Lambda_{\rm sm}
.
This only enlarges the stated bounds and makes the lower-time dependence of the schedule networks explicit.

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(e(d\vee\beta))\log^{3}\!\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}\bigr),

(223)

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}}\frac{(D+d+\beta)^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\Biggl[\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-2}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\Bigl\{\Bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-2(1-q/\beta)/\alpha_{q}}\log^{q/\alpha_{q}}\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}\bigr)\Bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}

\displaystyle+\Bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-(1-q/\beta)}\log^{q/2}\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}\bigr)\Bigr)^{d/(\beta-q)}\Bigr\}\Biggr]\log^{3+d/2}\!\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}\bigr),

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}}A_{{\mathcal{M}}}\frac{dD(D+d+\beta)^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+4}}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\Biggl[\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-2}\bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\Bigl\{\Bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-2(1-q/\beta)/\alpha_{q}}\log^{q/\alpha_{q}}\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}\bigr)\Bigr)^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}

\displaystyle+\Bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-(1-q/\beta)}\log^{q/2}\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}\bigr)\Bigr)^{d/(\beta-q)}\Bigr\}\Biggr]\log^{5+d/2}\!\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}\bigr),

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}\!\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}\bigr).

The factors
\log^{3+d/2}(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1})
and
\log^{5+d/2}(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1})
include the logarithmic overhead of the projection-coordinate gate, the residual ramp, and the final products.
No mesh of
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
at scale
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}
is used; therefore the small-noise branch has no polynomial
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-1}
factor in its width or sparsity bound.
The fixed localization constant
c_{\star}
is chosen from the annular Gaussian-tail bound in
Lemma

F.1
and is independent of
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}
.
For fixed
d,\beta
and fixed geometry, the bracketed non-logarithmic factor in
Equation

223
is

\lesssim\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-d/\beta}\log^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}\!\bigl(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}\bigr)

after the finite order
q_{\rm geom}
in
Equation

1
is fixed, and one may take

\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}=C+\max\left\{8(q_{\rm geom}+2),\left\lceil\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1+\frac{\lfloor\beta\rfloor\,d}{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}+\frac{d}{\beta\vee 1}+\frac{d}{2}+6\right\rceil\right\}.

Here the
C
in the exponent is universal and independent of

D,n,\sigma_{t},p_{\min}
, and the numerical geometry constants; the resulting polynomial
ambient exponent may depend on the fixed
q_{\rm geom}
. In the main
d>2

regime we take the canonical choice
q_{\rm geom}=q_{\rm opt}(\beta)
from
Equation

2
;
then
q_{\rm geom}={\mathcal{O}}_{\beta}(1)
and the displayed formula gives

\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}={\mathcal{O}}_{\beta}(d)
.
Thus the small-noise branch has intrinsic approximation rate
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-d/\beta}
, up to logarithmic factors, while the ambient-dimension dependence is polynomial in
D
.
The factor
D^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}}
records the additional powers of the retained Laplace-term count that enter through the per-term coefficient accuracies, together with the squared-normal residual-gate cost; all lower-noise dependence is confined to logarithmic schedule and reciprocal accuracies.

Proof of
Proposition

F.25
and
Lemma

F.26
.

Fix
i
and
R\in\mathcal{R}
.
The number of retained
(q,{\bm{\lambda}})
-terms is

\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\#\{{\bm{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{D}:\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q\}=\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\binom{D+q}{q}=\binom{D+\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}.

Also, since
d<D
and
\lfloor\beta\rfloor<\beta
,

\binom{D+\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}(D+d+\beta)\leq\frac{(D+d+\beta)^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!},

which is the simpler ambient-dimension factor used in the width bound.
Similarly,

\binom{D+\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\,dD(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+4}\leq\frac{dD(D+d+\beta)^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}(d+2(\beta\vee 1))^{d+4}}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!},

which is the corresponding factor used in the sparsity bound.
The same retained-term count also appears in the allocated accuracies
\varepsilon_{\rm term}^{\rm sm}
and
\varepsilon_{A,q}^{\rm sm}
below.
Since
q\in\{0,\dots,\lfloor\beta\rfloor\}
is a finite set and the coefficient network sizes contain only fixed powers of
(\varepsilon_{A,q}^{\rm sm})^{-1}
, all additional powers of
\binom{D+\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}
are bounded by
D^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}}
after increasing
\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}(\beta,d)
.
Indeed,

\binom{D+\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\leq C_{\beta}D^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor},\qquad\max_{0\leq q\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left\{\frac{d}{\beta-q},\frac{d}{((\beta-q)\wedge 1)(\beta\vee 1)}\right\}\leq\frac{d}{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}.

Thus the retained-term allocation contributes at most

D^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor d/(\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor)}
,
up to fixed
(\beta,d)
-dependent constants, before the other displayed ambient
factors are included.
The residual and coefficient-coordinate constructions contribute at most

D^{d/(2(\beta\vee 1))}
beyond the explicit leading
D
-factor. The largest
projection-normal auxiliary cost comes from operations depending on

\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}
. For the original chart expansion this is the Gaussian factor in

Lemma

F.18
; for the de-Gaussianized construction below it is the
residual-norm gate. In both cases the normal approximation must be controlled in

\ell_{2}
, so a coordinatewise normal accuracy of order
D^{-1/2}
is required up
to logarithmic factors. Equivalently the internal scale in

Equation

197
is at most
D^{2}
, and therefore this part contributes
at most
D^{d/(\beta\vee 1)}
.
The remaining residual ramps, products, and exact affine sums contribute only fixed
ambient powers, absorbed by the
+3
in
\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}
.
The constant summands in the bracketed size factor are dominated by the
q=0
terms of the displayed sum because
C_{\rm size,sm}\geq e
,
0<\varepsilon_{\rm sm}\leq 1/2
, and
d\geq 1
.
Set

\varepsilon_{\rm term}^{\rm sm}:=c_{\rm term}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}\binom{D+\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}^{-1},\qquad\sigma_{\rm sm}:=C_{\rm sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{1/\beta}.

For
0\leq q\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor
, define

\alpha_{q}:=(\beta-q)\wedge 1,\qquad\varepsilon_{A,q}^{\rm sm}:=\frac{1}{2}\wedge c_{A,q}^{\rm sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{1-q/\beta}\bigl(\log(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1})\bigr)^{-q/2},

where
c_{A,q}^{\rm sm}>0
is chosen no larger than the fixed quantity

c_{A,q}c_{\rm term}C_{\rm sm}^{-q}\binom{D+\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}^{-1},

and
c_{A,q}
is the constant from
Corollary

F.21
.
By the fixed choice of
c_{\star}
in the active-set definition, and after decreasing the fixed numerical budgets below if necessary, the inactive tail in
Lemma

F.1
is at most
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}/6
.

Step 1: Analytic small-noise target.

On the active region
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
, use
Proposition

F.16
.
On the inactive region inside
A_{t}
, use zero.
Thus define

R_{i,{\rm app}}^{\rm sm}({\bm{x}},t):=\begin{cases}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{q}\sum_{\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q}A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),t\bigr){\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)^{{\bm{\lambda}}},&({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm}),\\[4.0pt]
0,&({\bm{x}},t)\notin{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm}).\end{cases}

By
Propositions

F.16
and
F.1
, uniformly over
t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
and
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
,

|R_{i}({\bm{x}},t)-R_{i,{\rm app}}^{\rm sm}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq\varepsilon_{\rm sm}/3.

(224)

The active part follows because the remainder in
Equation

189
is bounded by
C_{\rm Lap}^{\max}\sigma_{t}^{\beta}
, and the threshold
C_{\rm sm}
in
Definition

D.1
was chosen so that
C_{\rm Lap}^{\max}C_{\rm sm}^{\beta}\leq 1/6
.
Hence
C_{\rm Lap}^{\max}\sigma_{t}^{\beta}\leq\varepsilon_{\rm sm}/6
on
{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
.
The inactive part is bounded by
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}/6
by the fixed choice of
c_{\star}
.
This proves the true-tail statement
Equation

220
.

Step 2: Projection-coordinate gate.

Let
\Box_{i}^{A}
be the coefficient-extension box from
Lemma

F.19
, so that
K_{i}^{\circ}\Subset\Box_{i}^{A}\Subset\Box_{i}
.
Define the enlarged active set

{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{+}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm}):={\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(3c_{\star})}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm}).

By the
3c_{\star}
-variant of the localization condition, the proof of
Lemma

F.4
applies on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{+}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
.
In particular,
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\in K_{i}^{\circ}
there and the fixed-radius component approximations in
Corollary

F.22
, with
c_{a}=3c_{\star}
, are available.

Choose two piecewise-affine ReLU ramps.
The first is a coordinate bump
\theta_{i}^{A}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to[0,1]
, equal to one on
K_{i}^{\circ}
and equal to zero outside
\Box_{i}^{A}
.
Since the chart atlas and boxes are fixed, this bump is implemented with a fixed number of one-dimensional ramps and ReLU max/min operations.
The second is a residual bump
\theta_{\rm res}:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\to[0,1]
, equal to one on

\left[0,\left(\frac{3c_{\star}}{2}\right)^{2}\log(e/\varepsilon_{\rm sm})\right]

and equal to zero on

\left[(2c_{\star})^{2}\log(e/\varepsilon_{\rm sm}),\infty\right].

Let
\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}
be the chart-coordinate network obtained from the fixed-radius version of
Corollary

F.14
, and let
\widetilde{\bm{\nu}}_{i}
be the normalized-residual network from the fixed-radius version of
Lemma

F.17
.
Write

\Lambda_{\rm gate}:=1+\log(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}),\qquad\varepsilon_{\nu,{\rm gate}}:=c_{\rm gate}\bigl(1\wedge D^{-1/2}\Lambda_{\rm gate}^{1/2}\bigr).

Choose the projection-coordinate accuracy and normal-residual accuracy so that, on

{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{+}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
,

\|\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}\|_{\infty}\leq c_{\rm gate}\Lambda_{\rm gate}^{-1},\qquad\|\widetilde{\bm{\nu}}_{i}-{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\nu,{\rm gate}},

where
c_{\rm gate}>0
is a sufficiently small geometric constant.
Form
\|\widetilde{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}
using the multiplication lemma and define

\Theta_{i}^{\rm sm}({\bm{x}},t):=\theta_{i}^{A}\!\left(\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)\right)\theta_{\rm res}\!\left(\|\widetilde{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}\right),

(225)

with the final product again implemented by
Lemma

I.3
.
The normal-residual accuracy gives

\|\widetilde{\bm{\nu}}_{i}-{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}\leq c_{\rm gate}\Lambda_{\rm gate}^{1/2},

and, on the enlarged active region,

\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}\leq C\Lambda_{\rm gate}^{1/2}
. Hence

\bigl|\|\widetilde{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr|\leq Cc_{\rm gate}\Lambda_{\rm gate}.

After choosing
c_{\rm gate}
and the square-subnetwork accuracy small enough, the
implemented squared norm stays inside the fixed separation margin between the two
residual-ramp thresholds. The corresponding residual-network size has internal scale

C_{\rm res}D\varepsilon_{\nu,{\rm gate}}^{-2}\leq CC_{\rm res}D^{2}
, up to
logarithmic factors, which is the
D^{d/(\beta\vee 1)}
contribution recorded in

\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}
.
The gate satisfies

\Theta_{i}^{\rm sm}=1\quad\text{on }{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm}),

with the allocated gate error absorbed into the final
{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
approximation budget.
Indeed, on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
, the buffered-chart lemma gives
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}\in K_{i}^{\circ}
and
\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}\|_{2}\leq c_{\star}\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon_{\rm sm})}
, so both ramps are equal to one after the above perturbation margin.
The proposition makes no neural-output claim outside
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{+}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
.
This is essential: the chart coordinate and residual subnetworks above are certified on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{+}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
, and the analytic tail bound
Equation

220
handles the true chart integral away from the default active set.
Thus the implemented localization is obtained from projection coordinates, normalized residuals, and support-preserving coefficient extensions only on the certified enlarged active region, not from a
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}
-mesh of
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
.
Its size is absorbed into the same polylogarithmic factor as the component networks and has no polynomial
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-1}
contribution.

On the transition region
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{+}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})\setminus{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
, no point of
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
satisfies the active distance condition with radius
c_{\star}
.
Therefore the true chart integral is
{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
by
Lemma

F.1
and the fixed choice of
c_{\star}
.
The neural output on this transition region is controlled after the component construction by the support dichotomy in Step 4 below.

Step 3: Neural approximation of the active expansion.

For every
q\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor
and
\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q
, approximate

A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}\bigl({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t),t\bigr)

by the fixed-radius version of
Corollary

F.21
, supplied by
Corollary

F.22
, with term accuracy

\varepsilon_{\rm term}^{\rm sm}.

Here and below,
Corollary

F.21
and the residual and Gaussian lemmas are used through
Corollary

F.22
with
c_{a}=3c_{\star}
.
Approximate
{\bm{\nu}}_{i}
by
Lemma

F.17
, form the monomial
{\bm{\nu}}_{i}^{{\bm{\lambda}}}
by
Lemma

I.3
, approximate
\sigma_{t}^{q}
by the constant-one network when
q=0
and by
Lemma

I.8
when
q\geq 1
, and approximate the Gaussian factor by
Lemma

F.18
.
In the case
q\geq 1
, the accuracy parameter passed to
Lemma

I.8
is chosen as the minimum of the required schedule accuracy,
{\underline{t}}/2
, and
1/2
.
This makes the admissibility condition in
Lemma

I.8
automatic.
The resulting factor
\log{\underline{t}}^{-1}
is bounded by
\log\Lambda_{\rm sm}
, hence is included in the logarithmic factors displayed in
Equation

223
.
Internal accuracies are chosen so that each retained term contributes at most
\varepsilon_{\rm term}^{\rm sm}
to the final error after multiplication by the bounded remaining factors.
The bounds

\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{\log(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1})},\qquad\sigma_{t}\leq C_{\rm sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{1/\beta},

are exactly the bounds used in
Corollary

F.21
, so the coefficient-composition cost remains
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-d/\beta}
up to logarithms for every
\beta>0
.

Multiplying by the projection-coordinate gate
\Theta_{i}^{\rm sm}
, summing the finitely many
(q,{\bm{\lambda}})
-terms in an affine output layer, and allocating one more
{\mathcal{O}}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
budget to the final products gives a network
\widetilde{R}_{i}^{\rm sm}
.

Step 4: Transition-region control.

We first bound this network on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{+}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})\setminus{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
, where the gate can take values between
0
and
1
.
Let
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}={\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}({\bm{x}},t)
.
If
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\in S_{i}^{(R)}:=\mathsf{supp}a_{i}^{(R)}
, then
{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}({\bm{x}},t))\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
.
Since
({\bm{x}},t)\notin{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
, this particular support point does not satisfy the active distance condition.
Hence

\|{\bm{r}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}=\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})\|_{2}>c_{\star}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}}.

The exact normal Gaussian factor is therefore at most
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{c_{\star}^{2}/2}
, and the approximated Gaussian factor is small up to its allocated additive error.
If
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\notin S_{i}^{(R)}
, then
Equation

202
gives
\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},t)=0
for every retained coefficient.
The implemented coefficient network is evaluated at the approximate coordinate
\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}
used in
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},\varepsilon_{A,q}^{\rm sm}}^{(R)}
, not exactly at
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}
.
By the fixed-radius coordinate approximation in
Corollary

F.22
, the choice
\varepsilon_{u}\asymp(\varepsilon_{A,q}^{\rm sm})^{1/((\beta-q)\wedge 1)}
, the
(\beta-q)
-Hölder bound in
Equation

203
, and the intrinsic coefficient-network error,

\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}},\varepsilon_{A,q}^{\rm sm}}^{(R)}({\bm{x}},t)\right|\leq C\|\widetilde{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{\infty}^{(\beta-q)\wedge 1}+\varepsilon_{A,q}^{\rm sm}\leq C\varepsilon_{A,q}^{\rm sm}.

In both cases, the bounds

\|{\bm{\nu}}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{\log(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1})},\qquad\sigma_{t}^{q}\leq\sigma_{\rm sm}^{q},

and the definition of
\varepsilon_{A,q}^{\rm sm}
imply that each retained term is bounded by its term budget.
After summing the finitely many retained terms and decreasing
c_{\rm term}
, if necessary, the neural output on the transition region is at most
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}/3
.

On
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
, the gate equals
1
, and the component approximations give the same bound against the active expansion.
Consequently

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})\\
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{+}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})\end{subarray}}\left|\widetilde{R}_{i}^{\rm sm}({\bm{x}},t)-R_{i,{\rm app}}^{\rm sm}({\bm{x}},t)\right|\leq 2\varepsilon_{\rm sm}/3.

(226)

Combining
Equations

224
and
226
proves
Equation

219
.

Step 5: Network size.

The projection, residual, Gaussian, coefficient, schedule-power, monomial, gate, and final product subnetworks are all finite in number for fixed
d,\beta
.
Adding their displayed size bounds, substituting the definition of
\varepsilon_{A,q}^{\rm sm}
, and enlarging
C_{\rm size,sm}
to absorb the fixed coefficient constants gives
Equation

223
; the additional powers of the retained-term count are absorbed by
D^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm sm}}
.
The projection-coordinate gate in
Equation

225
consists of a fixed chart coordinate bump, one residual-norm computation, and one scalar residual ramp.
Consequently its cost is polynomial in
D
and polylogarithmic in
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1}
, and it introduces no
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}
-mesh or polynomial lower-noise covering factor.
The only remaining non-logarithmic terms are the projection/coefficient Hölder approximation terms recorded in
Equations

197
,
199
and
211
; under
\sigma_{t}\lesssim\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{1/\beta}
, these are bounded by
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-d/\beta}
up to powers of
\log(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1})
.
The geometric projection terms use the smoothness
2(\beta\vee 1)
, while the density coefficient terms use their true regularities
\beta-q
; this is the point that keeps the displayed rate valid also for
0<\beta<1
.
∎

F.4.4
Score approximation on the small-noise regime

Lemma

F.27
is the key lower-bound step in the
small-noise branch. Without de-Gaussianization, the denominator contains the normal
factor
\exp\{-\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}/(2\sigma_{t}^{2})\}
and a reciprocal network would have to
resolve an exponentially small quantity on the tube. The lemma removes this common
factor and proves a polynomial floor, which is what makes the later ratio network stable.

Lemma F.27

(Small-noise de-Gaussianized denominator lower bound)
.

Assume
1
and
3
.
There are constants
c_{Q}>0
and
C_{Q}>0
, depending only on the displayed finite
geometry controls and the uniform schedule bounds in
Equation

11
,
such that

c_{Q}^{-1}\vee C_{Q}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}.

The following holds.
Fix
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
, put
H:=D\vee\log n
, and let

A_{t}:=\{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\leq C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}\}.

For every
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
, choose any nearest point

{\bm{y}}_{\star}\in\argmin_{{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{M}}}\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2},\qquad{\bm{r}}_{\star}:={\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}_{\star}.

Then

\exp\!\left(\frac{\|{\bm{r}}_{\star}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right){\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)\geq c_{Q}p_{\min}\bigl(1+C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}\bigr)^{-d/2}\geq C_{Q}^{-1}p_{\min}H^{-d/4}.

(227)

In particular, if
{\bm{x}}/m_{t}
lies in the reach tube and

{\bm{y}}_{\star}=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t})
, the same bound applies to the de-Gaussianized
denominator defined with the projection residual

{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)={\bm{x}}-m_{t}\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t})
.

Proof.

Step 1: choose a chart with uniformly positive amplitude.

Because
{\mathcal{M}}
is compact, the nearest point
{\bm{y}}_{\star}
exists.
Since the partition of unity is nonnegative and sums to one, there is an index
i_{\star}

such that
\rho_{i_{\star}}({\bm{y}}_{\star})\geq C_{{\mathcal{M}}}^{-1}
.
Let
{\bm{u}}_{\star}:=\phi_{i_{\star}}({\bm{y}}_{\star})
.
The support of
\rho_{i_{\star}}
is contained in
U_{i_{\star}}
, and

\phi_{i_{\star}}({\mathcal{S}}_{i_{\star}})\Subset K_{i_{\star}}^{\circ}
.
Using the finite atlas, the
C^{1}
-bounds on
\bar{\rho}_{i}
, the positive Jacobian lower
