bound on each compact coordinate box, and the lower bound

\rho_{i_{\star}}({\bm{y}}_{\star})\geq C_{{\mathcal{M}}}^{-1}
, there are constants

r_{Q}>0
and
c_{a}>0
, independent of
({\bm{x}},t,D,n)
, such that

B_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}({\bm{u}}_{\star},r_{Q})\subset K_{i_{\star}}^{\circ},\qquad a_{i_{\star}}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}})=\bar{\rho}_{i_{\star}}({\bm{u}})p_{0}({\bm{z}}_{i_{\star}}({\bm{u}}))\sqrt{\det{\bm{G}}_{i_{\star}}({\bm{u}})}\geq c_{a}p_{\min}

for every
{\bm{u}}\in B_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}({\bm{u}}_{\star},r_{Q})
.
By
Definitions

B.5
and
F.3
,
these constants may be chosen with

r_{Q}^{-1}\vee c_{a}^{-1}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}
.

Step 2: compare the phase to the nearest-point phase.

Set
i=i_{\star}
and
{\bm{z}}={\bm{z}}_{i}
.
Since
{\bm{y}}_{\star}
minimizes
{\bm{y}}\mapsto\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}
over the smooth manifold,
the first variation vanishes:

{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{\star})^{\top}{\bm{r}}_{\star}=0.

For
{\bm{u}}={\bm{u}}_{\star}+{\bm{\delta}}\in B_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}({\bm{u}}_{\star},r_{Q})
, Taylor’s theorem gives

{\bm{z}}({\bm{u}})-{\bm{z}}({\bm{u}}_{\star})={\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{\star}){\bm{\delta}}+{\bm{E}}({\bm{\delta}}),\qquad\|{\bm{E}}({\bm{\delta}})\|_{2}\leq C\|{\bm{\delta}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Therefore, using
0<m_{t}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{r}}_{\star}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=2m_{t}\langle{\bm{r}}_{\star},{\bm{y}}_{\star}-{\bm{z}}({\bm{u}})\rangle+m_{t}^{2}\|{\bm{z}}({\bm{u}})-{\bm{y}}_{\star}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|{\bm{r}}_{\star}\|_{2})\|{\bm{\delta}}\|_{2}^{2}.

The first inequality uses only the nearest-point property of
{\bm{y}}_{\star}
.
The last inequality uses the vanished linear term

\langle{\bm{r}}_{\star},{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}_{\star}){\bm{\delta}}\rangle=0
and the uniform
C^{2}
-bound on the
chart map.

Step 3: integrate over a fixed rescaled coordinate ball.

Let
R_{Q}\in(0,r_{Q}]
be fixed and write

a_{\star}:=1+\|{\bm{r}}_{\star}\|_{2},\qquad{\bm{u}}={\bm{u}}_{\star}+\sigma_{t}a_{\star}^{-1/2}{\bm{w}},\qquad\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{Q}.

Since
\sigma_{t}\leq 1
, this coordinate ball is contained in

B_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}({\bm{u}}_{\star},r_{Q})
.
By Steps 1 and 2,

\displaystyle\exp\!\left(\frac{\|{\bm{r}}_{\star}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right){\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{B({\bm{u}}_{\star},r_{Q})}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{r}}_{\star}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}

\displaystyle\geq c_{a}p_{\min}a_{\star}^{-d/2}\int_{\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{Q}}e^{-C\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}{\bm{w}}

\displaystyle\geq c_{Q}p_{\min}(1+\|{\bm{r}}_{\star}\|_{2})^{-d/2}.

Step 4: specialize to
A_{t}
.

If
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
, then

\|{\bm{r}}_{\star}\|_{2}=\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\leq C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}
, which gives the first
bound in
Equation

227
.
Because
H\geq 1
and
\sigma_{t}\leq 1
,

(1+C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H})^{d/2}\leq C_{Q}H^{d/4}
after increasing
C_{Q}
.
This proves the second bound.
∎

Lemma F.28

(
H
-localized de-Gaussianized small-noise chart approximation)
.

Assume
1
and
2
.
Let
H\geq 1
,
0<\varepsilon_{\rm sm}\leq 1/2
, and suppose

H\geq\log(e/\varepsilon_{\rm sm}).

By the global choice above,
c_{\star}\geq 2C_{\varrho}
. Assume that

[t_{0},t_{1}]\subset{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
and that, for every

t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
,

3c_{\star}\frac{\sigma_{t}}{m_{t}}\sqrt{H}\leq\eta_{\Pi}/2.

(228)

For

A_{t}(H):=\{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\leq C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}\},

define the projection residual

{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t):={\bm{x}}-m_{t}\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t}),\qquad({\bm{x}},t)\in A_{t}(H),\quad t\in[t_{0},t_{1}],

which is well-defined by
Equation

228
. For

R\in\{{\mathcal{Q}},{\mathcal{P}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{P}}_{D}\}
, set

\bar{R}_{i}({\bm{x}},t):=\exp\!\left(\frac{\|{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)R_{i}({\bm{x}},t).

In particular, write
\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
and
\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{i,k}
for the choices

R={\mathcal{Q}}
and
R={\mathcal{P}}_{k}
, and define

\bar{\mathcal{Q}}:=\sum_{i}\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{i},\qquad\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{k}:=\sum_{i}\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{i,k},\qquad\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{i,k}:=\frac{m_{t}\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{i,k}-x_{k}\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}}{\sigma_{t}},\qquad\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{k}:=\sum_{i}\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{i,k}.

Then, for every chart
i
and every such
R
, there is a ReLU network

\widehat{\bar{R}}_{i,H}^{\rm sm}
such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]\\
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}(H)\end{subarray}}|\widehat{\bar{R}}_{i,H}^{\rm sm}({\bm{x}},t)-\bar{R}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq C\varepsilon_{\rm sm}H^{C}.

(229)

Moreover, after summing the fixed atlas, there are networks

\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{Q}}}_{H}^{\rm sm}
and

\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{U}}}_{H}^{\rm sm}
satisfying

|\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{Q}}}_{H}^{\rm sm}({\bm{x}},t)-\bar{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)|\vee\|\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{U}}}_{H}^{\rm sm}({\bm{x}},t)-\bar{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq C\varepsilon_{\rm sm}H^{C}

(230)

uniformly over
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
and
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}(H)
.
All networks in this lemma may be chosen so that their depth, width, sparsity, and
log-weight obey the bounds in

Equation

223
, with the logarithmic factors

\log(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1})
replaced by

H+\log(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1})
, and with the fixed-atlas sum and
the
D
-coordinate parallelization absorbed by the displayed ambient polynomial powers.
Hence, when

H=D\vee\log n
, the extra
H^{C}
factors are absorbed by the displayed

(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}
powers below.

Proof.

Step 1:
H
-active localization and reach control.

For
c_{a}>0
, define

{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{a},H)}:=\left\{({\bm{x}},t):{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}(H),\ \exists{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\text{ with }\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq c_{a}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}\right\}.

Because
c_{\star}\geq 2C_{\varrho}
and the sets
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}
cover
{\mathcal{M}}
, every
({\bm{x}},t)\in A_{t}(H)
belongs to
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{\star},H)}
for at least one chart.
If
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(3c_{\star},H)}
, then

\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}}/m_{t},{\mathcal{M}})\leq 3c_{\star}\frac{\sigma_{t}}{m_{t}}\sqrt{H}\leq\eta_{\Pi}/2.

Thus the nearest projection
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t})
is unique, its chart coordinate in chart
i
lies in
K_{i}^{\circ}
, and the Gauss–Newton and buffered-coordinate estimates used earlier hold with
\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon_{\rm sm})}
replaced by
\sqrt{H}
.
Throughout the active part of the proof write

{\bm{\xi}}:={\bm{x}}/m_{t},\qquad{\bm{\pi}}:=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{\xi}}),\qquad{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}:=\phi_{i}({\bm{\pi}}),\qquad{\bm{r}}:={\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{\pi}},\qquad{\bm{\nu}}:=\sigma_{t}^{-1}{\bm{r}}.

The active condition gives
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\in K_{i}^{\circ}
and
\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}\leq 3c_{\star}\sqrt{H}
.

Step 2: de-Gaussianized coercivity.

Let
\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{\xi}})=\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{\xi}}-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}
, and write
S_{i}^{(R)}:=\mathsf{supp}a_{i}^{(R)}
.
The projection orthogonality gives
\nabla_{\bm{u}}\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi};{\bm{\xi}})=0
.
The proof of
Lemma

F.5
, with the
H
-reach condition above, gives

\nabla_{\bm{u}}^{2}\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi};{\bm{\xi}})\succeq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{i}^{(-)}{\bm{I}}_{d}.

Hence there are constants
r_{i}^{0}>0
and
c_{0}>0
such that

\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}};{\bm{\xi}})-\Phi_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi};{\bm{\xi}})\geq c_{0}\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}^{2}

whenever
{\bm{u}}\in\Box_{i}
and
\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}\leq r_{i}^{0}
.
On the compact set of pairs with
{\bm{u}}\in S_{i}^{(R)}
and
\|{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}\|_{2}\geq r_{i}^{0}
, uniqueness of the reach projection gives a strictly positive phase gap.
Combining the local quadratic gap with the compact-away gap and using the bounded diameter of
\Box_{i}
, we obtain the uniform de-Gaussianized lower bound

\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\geq c\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}

for all
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}}\in S_{i}^{(R)}
, after decreasing the fixed small-noise threshold in
Equation

196
if necessary.
Consequently, for
R_{H}:=A\sqrt{H}
with
A
fixed sufficiently large,

\int_{\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}>R_{H}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{r}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2})^{C}{\mathrm{d}}{\bm{w}}\leq C\varepsilon_{\rm sm}H^{-C},

where we used
H\geq\log(e/\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
and increased
A
to dominate the finitely many polynomial powers used below.

Step 3: scalar Taylor expansion after removing the normal Gaussian.

For
s\in[0,\sigma_{t}]
, define the smooth difference quotient

{\bm{Z}}_{s}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}}):=\int_{0}^{1}{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\theta s{\bm{w}}){\bm{w}}{\mathrm{d}}\theta,

so that
{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+s{\bm{w}})-{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})=s{\bm{Z}}_{s}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})
and
{\bm{Z}}_{0}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})={\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}
.
Define the de-Gaussianized phase along this scalar path by

\Theta_{s}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t):=\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{\nu}}-m_{t}{\bm{Z}}_{s}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{2}.

At
s=\sigma_{t}
, this is exactly the exponent in the de-Gaussianized chart integral, and at
s=0
it equals
\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}
because
{\bm{J}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})^{\top}{\bm{\nu}}=0
.
We now verify that the same coercivity holds uniformly along this interpolation.
For
s=0
, ellipticity of
{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})
gives

\Theta_{0}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)\geq c\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}.

For
0<s\leq\sigma_{t}
, set
{\bm{x}}_{s}:=m_{t}{\bm{\pi}}+s{\bm{\nu}}
. Since

\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}\leq 3c_{\star}\sqrt{H}
and

Equation

228
holds,

\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}}_{s}/m_{t},{\mathcal{M}})\leq s\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}/m_{t}\leq 3c_{\star}\sigma_{t}m_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{H}\leq\eta_{\Pi}/2.

Moreover
{\bm{\pi}}=\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}_{s}/m_{t})
, because
{\bm{x}}_{s}/m_{t}
lies on the normal segment
issued from
{\bm{\pi}}
and remains inside the reach tube. Applying the reach-tube objective
gap
Lemma

F.12
to the pair

{\bm{x}}_{s}/m_{t}
and
{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+s{\bm{w}})
gives

\|{\bm{x}}_{s}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+s{\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{x}}_{s}-m_{t}{\bm{\pi}}\|_{2}^{2}\geq c\,m_{t}^{2}\|{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+s{\bm{w}})-{\bm{\pi}}\|_{2}^{2}.

On the compact coordinate box, the inverse chart is uniformly bi-Lipschitz, hence

\|{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+s{\bm{w}})-{\bm{\pi}}\|_{2}\geq c\,s\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\qquad\text{whenever }{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+s{\bm{w}}\in\Box_{i}.

Dividing the previous objective gap by
2s^{2}
gives

\Theta_{s}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)=\frac{\|{\bm{x}}_{s}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+s{\bm{w}})\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{x}}_{s}-m_{t}{\bm{\pi}}\|_{2}^{2}}{2s^{2}}\geq c\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Thus
\Theta_{s}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)\geq c\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}
on the chart support for every

s\in[0,\sigma_{t}]
.
For
R\in\{{\mathcal{Q}},{\mathcal{P}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{P}}_{D}\}
, set

G_{R}(s,{\bm{w}}):=\exp\{-\Theta_{s}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)\}a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+s{\bm{w}}).

The coercivity from Step 2 and the smoothness of
{\bm{z}}_{i}
imply that all derivatives in
s
falling on the geometric and exponential factors are bounded by

CH^{C}(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2})^{C}e^{-c\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}

for
0\leq s\leq\sigma_{t}
and
\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{H}
.
The density-dependent amplitude is expanded only to order
\lfloor\beta\rfloor
, and the remainder is controlled by the
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}
modulus of
a_{i}^{(R)}
along the line
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+s{\bm{w}}
.
Taylor’s theorem in the scalar variable
s
therefore gives

G_{R}(\sigma_{t},{\bm{w}})=\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{q}G_{R,q}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)+\sigma_{t}^{\beta}\mathcal{E}_{R}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t),

with

|\mathcal{E}_{R}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}},t)|\leq CH^{C}(1+\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2})^{C}e^{-c\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}^{2}}.

Each coefficient
G_{R,q}
is a finite sum of a polynomial in
({\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}})
, a smooth geometric factor in
{\bm{u}}^{\Pi}
, a bounded schedule power of
m_{t}
, and a derivative
\partial^{\alpha}a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})
with
|\alpha|\leq q
.
After integration in
{\bm{w}}
, the coefficients agree with the
A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}
defined in
Proposition

F.16
, because both constructions differentiate the same recentered integrand after the common normal Gaussian factor has been removed.
Integrating the Taylor expansion on
\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}\leq R_{H}
and adding the tail from Step 2 yields

\bar{R}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{q}\sum_{\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q}A_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},t){\bm{\nu}}^{{\bm{\lambda}}}+{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i,H}^{(R)}({\bm{x}},t),\qquad|{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i,H}^{(R)}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq C\sigma_{t}^{\beta}H^{C}.

Since
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]\subset{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
, this remainder is bounded by
C\varepsilon_{\rm sm}H^{C}
.

Step 4: centered numerator expansion without a
\sigma_{t}^{-1}
loss.

For each coordinate
k
, define

N_{k}(s,{\bm{w}}):=-\nu_{k}+m_{t}({\bm{Z}}_{s}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},{\bm{w}}))_{k}.

At
s=\sigma_{t}
, this satisfies

N_{k}(\sigma_{t},{\bm{w}})=\frac{m_{t}z_{i,k}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{w}})-x_{k}}{\sigma_{t}}.

Thus
\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{i,k}
is the integral of
N_{k}(\sigma_{t},{\bm{w}})G_{{\mathcal{Q}}}(\sigma_{t},{\bm{w}})
.
Applying the same scalar Taylor argument to the product
N_{k}(s,{\bm{w}})G_{{\mathcal{Q}}}(s,{\bm{w}})
gives

\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{i,k}({\bm{x}},t)=\sum_{q=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sigma_{t}^{q}\sum_{\|{\bm{\lambda}}\|_{1}\leq q+1}A_{i,k,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(\bar{U})}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi},t){\bm{\nu}}^{{\bm{\lambda}}}+{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i,k,H}^{(\bar{U})}({\bm{x}},t),

where
|{\mathrm{Rem}}_{i,k,H}^{(\bar{U})}({\bm{x}},t)|\leq C\varepsilon_{\rm sm}H^{C}
.
For clarity, we verify the centered-numerator coefficient analogue used by the network
construction. Differentiating
N_{k}(s,{\bm{w}})G_{{\mathcal{Q}}}(s,{\bm{w}})
at
s=0
at most
q
times
produces finite sums of terms of the form

m_{t}^{a}P({\bm{\nu}},{\bm{w}})H({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})\partial^{\alpha}a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi})\exp\!\left(-\frac{m_{t}^{2}}{2}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{i}({\bm{u}}^{\Pi}){\bm{w}}\right),\qquad|\alpha|\leq q,

where
P
is a polynomial whose
{\bm{\nu}}
-degree is at most
q+1
; the extra degree is
exactly the leading centered factor
-\nu_{k}
in
N_{k}
. After integrating in
{\bm{w}}
, the
Gaussian moment formula used in
Lemma

F.19
expresses each coefficient

A_{i,k,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(\bar{U})}
as a finite sum of powers of
m_{t}
times products of
smooth geometric factors and derivatives
\partial^{\alpha}a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}
with

|\alpha|\leq q
. Therefore, on the same intermediate box

K_{i}^{\circ}\Subset\Box_{i}^{A}\Subset\Box_{i}
, define the extension by this identical finite
formula. If
{\bm{u}}\notin S_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}=\mathsf{supp}a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}
, then
a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}
vanishes in a
neighborhood of
{\bm{u}}
, so all derivatives
\partial^{\alpha}a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}({\bm{u}})
with

|\alpha|\leq q
vanish; the centered extension is therefore support-preserving. Since

a_{i}^{({\mathcal{Q}})}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}
and only derivatives up to order
q
are used, the extended
coefficient belongs to
{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta-q}(\Box_{i}^{A})
with norm bounded by the same finite
geometry and density factors as in
Lemma

F.19
. Applying the coefficient
network construction of
Lemma

F.20
to this finite family gives the same
depth, width, sparsity, and log-weight bounds, up to one additional fixed power of
D

for the coordinate index
k
and the extra normal monomial. This power is absorbed into

\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}
.
This direct centered expansion is the reason the proof never forms
(m_{t}\bar{\mathcal{P}}-x\bar{\mathcal{Q}})/\sigma_{t}
from two separately approximated networks.

Step 5: certified chart gate and network realization.

Fix one intermediate coefficient box
\Box_{i}^{A}
with
K_{i}^{\circ}\Subset\Box_{i}^{A}\Subset\Box_{i}
, and use the coefficient-extension construction on this common box for the finite family of retained terms.
Choose the coordinate tolerance
\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm cert}
as a fixed constant multiple of the smallest coordinate tolerance required by these retained coefficient networks.
Apply
Lemma

F.24
with
c_{a}=3c_{\star}
,
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{\rm sm}
,
\eta=\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm cert}
, and this box
\Box_{i}^{A}
.
It gives networks
\Gamma_{i}^{H}
,
\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}
, and
\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}^{A}
such that
\Gamma_{i}^{H}=1
and
\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}^{A}
is an accurate coefficient coordinate on
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(3c_{\star},H)}
, while
\Gamma_{i}^{H}>0
anywhere in
A_{t}(H)
implies that
\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}
is a certified chart-
i
coordinate for
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t})
.
For the coefficient functions in Steps 3 and 4, use the extensions from

Lemma

F.19
and the centered-numerator analogues verified in Step 4,
evaluate them at the clipped certified coordinate
\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}^{A}
, and multiply the
retained expansion by
\Gamma_{i}^{H}
.
Use the global normal-residual network supplied by
Corollary

F.23
on the active union
\bigcup_{j}{\mathcal{K}}_{j}^{(3c_{\star},H)}
, which contains
A_{t}(H)
by Step 1.
Allocate accuracy
c\varepsilon_{\rm sm}H^{-C}
to each coefficient, monomial, schedule power, product, normal-residual operation, certified chart gate, and final affine summation.
On
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(3c_{\star},H)}
the gate equals one, the coordinate and normal-residual errors are certified, and the Taylor expansions from Steps 3 and 4 are approximated with total error
C\varepsilon_{\rm sm}H^{C}
.
The resulting size bounds are the same as those in
Equation

223
, with every logarithmic scale enlarged to
H+\log(C_{\rm size,sm}\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-1})
, because the certified objective gate reuses the global projection objective networks and adds only fixed-atlas reciprocals, ramps, products, and sums.

Step 6: inactive and transition regions after de-Gaussianization.

If
({\bm{x}},t)\notin{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(3c_{\star},H)}
and
{\bm{u}}\in S_{i}^{(R)}
, then

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}>3c_{\star}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}.

Since
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}(H)
gives
\|{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}
and
c_{\star}\geq 2C_{\varrho}
, we have

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}\geq cc_{\star}^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}H.

The de-Gaussianized inactive integrand is therefore bounded by
e^{-cc_{\star}^{2}H}
times a fixed polynomial in
H
.
The global choice of
c_{\star}
makes the true inactive contribution bounded by
C\varepsilon_{\rm sm}H^{C}
.
It remains to control the implemented chart output on the same inactive region.
If
\Gamma_{i}^{H}=0
, the implemented chart output is zero.
If
\Gamma_{i}^{H}>0
, the certification in Step 5 gives a valid chart coordinate
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}=\phi_{i}(\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t}))
and
\|\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}-{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm cert}
.
Because
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}(H)
and
c_{\star}\geq 2C_{\varrho}
, the condition
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}\in S_{i}^{(R)}
would imply
({\bm{x}},t)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(c_{\star},H)}\subset{\mathcal{K}}_{i}^{(3c_{\star},H)}
, contradicting the inactive assumption.
Hence
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}\notin S_{i}^{(R)}
whenever the inactive-region gate is nonzero.
If
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}\in\Box_{i}^{A}
, the support-preserving extension gives
\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi},t)=0
, and the Hölder estimate plus the
1
-Lipschitz clipping to
\Box_{i}^{A}
gives
|\overline{A}_{i,q,{\bm{\lambda}}}^{(R)}(\breve{\bm{u}}_{i}^{A},t)|\leq C(\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm cert})^{(\beta-q)\wedge 1}
.
If
{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\Pi}\notin\Box_{i}^{A}
, the fixed positive separation of
S_{i}^{(R)}\subset K_{i}^{\circ}\Subset\Box_{i}^{A}
from
\partial\Box_{i}^{A}
and the choice of
\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm cert}
ensure that the clipped coefficient input remains in
\Box_{i}^{A}\setminus S_{i}^{(R)}
, so the same bound holds with the intrinsic coefficient-network accuracy alone.
Choosing
\varepsilon_{u}^{\rm cert}
according to the coefficient tolerances in Step 5 makes every retained inactive term at most its allocated
c\varepsilon_{\rm sm}H^{-C}
budget after multiplication by
\sigma_{t}^{q}
and the
H^{q/2}
normal-monomial bound.
Combining the active approximation, the true inactive tail, and the certified off-support network control proves
Equation

229
.
Summing over the fixed atlas and using the direct centered-numerator networks from Step 4 proves
Equation

230
.
The displayed network-size statement follows from the size bounds in Step 5, the fixed atlas summation, and the coordinatewise parallelization for
\bar{\mathcal{U}}
.
∎

Corollary F.29

(Score approximation on small noise regime
[t_{0},t_{1}]
)
.

Assume
1
,
2
and
3
, and suppose
D\vee p_{\min}^{-1}\leq n^{a_{0}}
.
Put
H:=D\vee\log n
, let
q_{\rm geom}
be as in
Equation

1
, and, after
this finite order is fixed, take

\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}=C+\max\left\{8(q_{\rm geom}+2),\left\lceil\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1+\frac{\lfloor\beta\rfloor\,d}{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}+\frac{d}{\beta\vee 1}+\frac{d}{2}+6\right\rceil\right\},

where the
C
in the exponent is universal and independent of

D,n,\sigma_{t},p_{\min}
, and the numerical geometry constants. Thus

\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}
may depend on the fixed finite order
q_{\rm geom}
; in
the main
d>2
regime, choosing
q_{\rm geom}=q_{\rm opt}(\beta)
yields

q_{\rm geom}={\mathcal{O}}_{\beta}(1)
and hence
\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}={\mathcal{O}}_{\beta}(d)
.
Let
C_{\rm den,sm}\geq 1
be a fixed constant large enough to
dominate the reciprocal and product constants in the small-noise ratio step. Assume the
small-noise reach condition

3c_{\star}\underline{m}^{-1}\sigma_{t_{1}}\sqrt{H}\leq\eta_{\Pi}/2

(231)

and the small-noise denominator-resolution condition

C_{\rm den,sm}p_{\min}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}n^{-\beta/(d+2\beta)}H^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}/2-1/2}\leq 1.

(232)

For the VP/OU schedules used here,
\sigma_{t_{1}}\asymp n^{-1/(d+2\beta)}
, so

Equation

231
is implied by

D\vee\log n\leq c\,\underline{m}^{2}\eta_{\Pi}^{2}\,n^{2/(d+2\beta)}.

There exists a switched ReLU score network
s_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}
on
[t_{0},t_{1}]
such that for

A_{t}:=\{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\leq C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}\},

\|\sigma_{t}s_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}({\bm{x}},t)-\sigma_{t}s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-\beta/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}/2},\quad{\bm{x}}\in A_{t},\;\;t\in[t_{0},t_{1}],

(233)

or equivalently,

\|s_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}({\bm{x}},t)-s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-2\beta/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}\sigma_{t}^{-2},\quad{\bm{x}}\in A_{t},\quad t\in[t_{0},t_{1}].

(234)

\sup_{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\|s_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq C\sigma_{t}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{1/2},\qquad t\in[t_{0},t_{1}],

(235)

and

L,\;\log B\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

(236)

and

\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty},\;S\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{d/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

(237)

Proof.

Step 1: de-Gaussianize the chart integrals.

On the tube

A_{t}:=\{\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}},m_{t}{\mathcal{M}})\leq C_{\varrho}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{H}\},

the reach condition
Equation

231
gives

\mathrm{dist}({\bm{x}}/m_{t},{\mathcal{M}})\leq C_{\varrho}\frac{\sigma_{t}}{m_{t}}\sqrt{H}\leq\eta_{\Pi}/2.

Thus
\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t})
is the unique metric projection, and we may define

{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t):={\bm{x}}-m_{t}\Pi_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\bm{x}}/m_{t}).

Define the de-Gaussianized denominator and centered numerator by

\bar{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t):=\exp\!\left(\frac{\|{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right){\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t),\qquad\bar{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t):=\exp\!\left(\frac{\|{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right){\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t).

(238)

Equivalently, for
R\in\{{\mathcal{Q}},{\mathcal{P}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{P}}_{D}\}
, define

\bar{R}_{i}({\bm{x}},t):=\exp\!\left(\frac{\|{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)R_{i}({\bm{x}},t).

(239)

Using
Equation

48
, this removes exactly the common normal Gaussian factor:

\bar{R}_{i}({\bm{x}},t)=\sigma_{t}^{-d}\int_{K_{i}^{\rm cell}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)a_{i}^{(R)}({\bm{u}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{u}}.

(240)

Thus all exponential factors in the lower-bound small-noise approximation are displayed explicitly: the original chart Gaussian

\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right),

the common de-Gaussianizing multiplier

\exp\!\left(\frac{\|{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right),

and their product

\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right).

Consequently,

\bar{\mathcal{Q}}=\sum_{i}\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{i},\qquad\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{k}=\sum_{i}\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{i,k},\qquad\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{k}=\frac{m_{t}\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{k}-x_{k}\bar{\mathcal{Q}}}{\sigma_{t}}.

(241)

The common exponential factor cancels, so

\frac{\bar{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)}{\bar{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)}=\frac{{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)}{{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)}=\sigma_{t}s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\qquad({\bm{x}}\in A_{t}).

Step 2: lower-bound the de-Gaussianized denominator.

By
Lemma

F.27
, the density lower bound gives the
following denominator floor after de-Gaussianization:

\bar{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)\geq C^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{-C}p_{\min}(D\vee\log n)^{-d/4},\qquad({\bm{x}},t)\in A_{t},\quad t\in[t_{0},t_{1}],

(242)

The factor
(D\vee\log n)^{d/4}
in the reciprocal is absorbed into the

(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}
part of the explicit ambient polynomial below,
and the displayed
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}
factor is carried with the other geometry
terms.
The centered-ratio bound from
Equation

64
, used with

{\underline{t}}=t_{0}
and the assumption
p_{\min}^{-1}\leq n^{a_{0}}
, gives

\|\bar{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq CH^{1/2}\bar{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)\qquad(({\bm{x}},t)\in A_{t}).

(243)

Step 3: approximate the de-Gaussianized numerator and denominator.

Set
a_{\beta}:=\beta/(d+2\beta)
and
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}:=\frac{1}{2}\wedge e\,n^{-a_{\beta}}
.
If
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}=e\,n^{-a_{\beta}}
, then
\log(e/\varepsilon_{\rm sm})=a_{\beta}\log n\leq H
.
If
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}=1/2
, then
\log(e/\varepsilon_{\rm sm})=\log(2e)\leq H
because
d\geq 1
,
d<D
, and hence
D\geq 2
.
Use
Lemma

F.28
with
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}
and
H=D\vee\log n
.
The inclusion
[t_{0},t_{1}]\subset{\mathcal{I}}_{\rm sm}(\varepsilon_{\rm sm})
follows from the choice of the small-noise endpoint
t_{1}
.
The reach condition needed by that lemma is exactly
Equation

231
.
This is the point where the de-Gaussianized construction uses
H
-active charts rather than the smaller
\sqrt{\log(e/\varepsilon_{\rm sm})}
-active charts: for every
{\bm{x}}\in A_{t}
, a nearest chart is active, while charts outside the
3c_{\star}\sqrt{H}
-enlargement have

\|{\bm{x}}-m_{t}{\bm{z}}_{i}({\bm{u}})\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\bm{r}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}\geq c\sigma_{t}^{2}H

on their supports and are exponentially negligible after de-Gaussianization.
After summing over charts, this gives networks
\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{Q}}}
and

\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{U}}}
satisfying

|\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{Q}}}({\bm{x}},t)-\bar{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)|\vee\|\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{U}}}({\bm{x}},t)-\bar{\mathcal{U}}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}n^{-\beta/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}/2-d/4-1/2}

(244)

on
A_{t}
, uniformly in
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
.
Here the universal constant in the definition of
\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}
is chosen large enough that the
H^{C}
loss in
Equation

230
is dominated by
(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}/2-d/4-1/2}
.
The size is bounded by the right-hand sides of
Equations

236
and
237
: in
Equation

223
we use
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}=\frac{1}{2}\wedge e\,n^{-\beta/(d+2\beta)}
, so
\varepsilon_{\rm sm}^{-d/\beta}\lesssim n^{d/(d+2\beta)}
.
The extra
\log{\underline{t}}^{-1}
from
Equation

221
is absorbed into
(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}
because
{\underline{t}}=t_{0}\asymp n^{-2(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}
.

Step 4: form the stable ratio.

By
Equation

242
,

\bar{\mathcal{Q}}({\bm{x}},t)^{-1}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}p_{\min}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{d/4}\qquad(({\bm{x}},t)\in A_{t}).

The denominator-resolution condition
Equation

232

and
Equation

244
imply

C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}n^{-\beta/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}/2-d/4-1/2}\leq c\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{-C}p_{\min}(D\vee\log n)^{-d/4}/4,

by the fixed choice of
C_{\rm den,sm}
. Hence

\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{Q}}}\vee c\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{-C}p_{\min}(D\vee\log n)^{-d/4}/2\geq c\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{-C}p_{\min}(D\vee\log n)^{-d/4}/2
, and the standard reciprocal and
product networks applied to

\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{U}}}/(\widehat{\bar{\mathcal{Q}}}\vee c\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{-C}p_{\min}(D\vee\log n)^{-d/4}/2)
, together with

Equations

243
and
244
, give
a network
\widehat{v}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}
such that

\|\widehat{v}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}({\bm{x}},t)-\sigma_{t}s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-\beta/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}/2},\qquad({\bm{x}},t)\in A_{t}.

By
Equation

64
, with
{\underline{t}}=t_{0}
, the polynomial
assumption
D\vee p_{\min}^{-1}\leq n^{a_{0}}
, and

\sigma_{t_{0}}^{-1}\leq n^{C}
, the true score obeys

\sigma_{t}\|s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq\sigma_{t}\|s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{1/2},\qquad({\bm{x}},t)\in A_{t}.

Set
s_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}:=\sigma_{t}^{-1}\widehat{v}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}
, with coordinatewise clipping at
C\sigma_{t}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{1/2}
.
On
A_{t}
,
Equations

233
and
234
follow.
Coordinatewise clipping is
1
-Lipschitz and fixes the target coordinatewise on
A_{t}
, so it
does not increase the tube approximation error. The clipping construction gives

Equation

235
.

Step 5: record size.

The reciprocal, products, clipping, and time switch add only polynomial factors in
D
,
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}
,
B_{0}
,
p_{\min}^{-1}
, and polylogarithmic factors in
n
.
The scalar reciprocal and clipping layers only affect
L+\log B
by polylogarithmic factors and do not change the leading intrinsic width/sparsity term.
Hence
Equations

236
and
237
follow.
∎

Appendix G
Score Estimation on Compact Smooth Manifolds

This appendix proves the learned-score bounds used by the distribution estimation results in
Appendix

H
.
The clipped neural network class
\widetilde{{\mathcal{NN}}}
, the clipped score-estimation oracle inequality, and the covering-number evaluation are stated in the main text in
Section

4
.
Here we apply those tools to the density-lower-bound small-noise and large-noise branches.
On a large-noise slab
I_{k}=[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
, the tangent-cell class uses cells of radius

r_{k}=c_{0}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}\wedge r_{\star,n}
. The cap
r_{\star,n}
, defined below, is the
accuracy-limited radius needed to make the fixed-order tangent-cell expansion uniform
also on order-one-noise slabs.
The dyadic sum of the
\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}
terms is dominated by the first large-noise
slab; the additional
r_{\star,n}^{-d}
contribution is tracked explicitly in the
oracle and
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
bounds.
The small-noise branch uses the projection–Laplace class under
3
.
In all size bounds in this section, the factor
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}
may be
read as

C(1\vee S_{{\mathcal{M}}})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}(1\vee\kappa^{-1})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}\mathcal{K}_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}

using the reach-explicit finite-geometry convention.

The section has one generic tool and two applications. The oracle inequality

Theorem

G.1
is the only statistical argument; the large-noise and
small-noise results then insert the deterministic comparison classes from

Theorems

E.5
and
F.29
and evaluate
their entropy sizes. Readers interested only in the final rates can read

Propositions

G.2
and
G.3

first and return to the oracle proof for concentration details.

G.1
Oracle Inequality for Score Estimation

Theorem G.1

(Oracle inequality for clipped score estimation)
.

Let
n^{-{\mathcal{O}}(1)}\leq{\underline{t}}<{\overline{t}}\leq n^{{\mathcal{O}}(1)}
,
D\leq n^{{\mathcal{O}}(1)}
, and fix an arbitrary
A>0
.
Let
\widehat{s}
be an empirical risk minimizer over the neural network class

{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}\equiv\widetilde{{\mathcal{NN}}}(L_{{\underline{t}}},{\bm{W}}_{{\underline{t}}},S_{{\underline{t}}},B_{{\underline{t}}})

for the interval-restricted denoising loss
\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}
, namely

\widehat{s}\in\argmin_{s\in{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s,{\bm{x}}^{(i)}).

The same conclusion holds for any measurable approximate empirical minimizer with
empirical excess at most
n^{-A}
, after increasing the negligible remainder.
Then the expected excess risk satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\{{\bm{x}}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\Bigl[\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}[\|\widehat{s}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}]{\mathrm{d}t}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\lesssim\inf_{s\in{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}}\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\bigl[\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}+\frac{(D\vee\log n)^{2}\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\log\bigl({\mathcal{N}}(n^{-{\mathcal{O}}(1)},{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\vee n\bigr)}{n}+n^{-A}.

Proof.

Step 1: reduce the denoising risk to the marginal score risk.

Write

\mathbb{P}_{n}f:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f({\bm{x}}^{(i)})

for the empirical average over the training sample. For
s\in{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}
, set

{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s):=\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}.

The denoising-score identity, applied on the interval
[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
, gives

{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s)={\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{0}}\{\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s,{\bm{X}}_{0})-\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s^{*},{\bm{X}}_{0})\}.

Since the last term is independent of
s
, the empirical minimizer of

\mathbb{P}_{n}\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s)
is also an empirical minimizer of

\mathbb{P}_{n}\{\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s)-\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s^{*})\}
.

Step 2: record the envelope and variance bounds.

The clipped definition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{NN}}
and
Lemma

I.12
imply

\sup_{s,s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}}\sup_{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}|\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s,{\bm{x}})-\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s^{\prime},{\bm{x}})|\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{2}\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}.

Moreover, applying
Lemma

I.11
to
s,s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}
, using the
same clipped envelope, and then averaging over
{\bm{X}}_{0}
, gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{0}}\bigl[\{\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s,{\bm{X}}_{0})-\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s^{\prime},{\bm{X}}_{0})\}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{2}\left(\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{2}\left(\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)\{{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s)+{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s^{\prime})\}.

(245)

Here the last inequality is the elementary bound

\|s-s^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|s-s^{*}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|s^{\prime}-s^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
.

Step 3: apply Bernstein on a finite cover.

Let
\eta=n^{-A_{0}}
, with
A_{0}
a sufficiently large fixed constant, and let

\mathcal{N}_{\eta}
be a minimal
\eta
-net of
{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}
in the supremum norm
after the input truncation and affine rescaling specified in
Section

4
.
Because all functions in
{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}
are realized after this input truncation, this
is also a global
\eta
-net for the clipped functions on

{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
.
For fixed
u,v\in\mathcal{N}_{\eta}
, Bernstein’s inequality and

Equation

245
give, with probability at least
1-e^{-r}
,

\displaystyle|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{0}})\{\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(u)-\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(v)\}|

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\sqrt{\frac{(D\vee\log n)^{2}\left(\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)\{{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(u)+{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(v)\}r}{n}}+\frac{(D\vee\log n)^{2}\left(\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)r}{n}\right].

Taking a union bound over
\mathcal{N}_{\eta}^{2}
and choosing

r=C\log\bigl({\mathcal{N}}(\eta,{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\vee n\bigr)

shows that the same display holds simultaneously for all
u,v\in\mathcal{N}_{\eta}
with
probability at least
1-n^{-A_{1}}
, after increasing
C
.

Step 4: transfer from the cover to the clipped class.

If
u
is an
\eta
-net approximation of
s
, then the pointwise bound in

Lemma

I.11
, together with the clipped envelope, yields

\sup_{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}|\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s,{\bm{x}})-\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(u,{\bm{x}})|\leq C\sqrt{D({\overline{t}}-{\underline{t}})(D\vee\log n)^{2}\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}}\,\eta.

Thus

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{0}}|\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s)-\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(u)|+\mathbb{P}_{n}|\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s)-\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(u)|\leq n^{-A_{2}}

deterministically after taking
A_{0}
large enough. This uses

D\leq n^{{\mathcal{O}}(1)}
and
n^{-{\mathcal{O}}(1)}\leq{\underline{t}}<{\overline{t}}\leq n^{{\mathcal{O}}(1)}
, so that

(D\vee\log n)^{2}\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq n^{{\mathcal{O}}(1)}
.
Since

{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s)-{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(u)={\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{0}}\{\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s,{\bm{X}}_{0})-\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(u,{\bm{X}}_{0})\},

the same estimate also gives

|{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s)-{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(u)|\leq n^{-A_{2}}
.
Hence the finite-cover Bernstein display applies to arbitrary
s,s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}
,
with
{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(u)+{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(v)
replaced by

{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s)+{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s^{\prime})+n^{-A_{2}}
, up to another
additive
n^{-A_{2}}
. Enlarging
A_{2}
, this is the same display with the covering
number evaluated at
n^{-{\mathcal{O}}(1)}
.

Step 5: use the ERM basic inequality.

Fix any
s_{0}\in{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}
. On the high-probability event from Steps 3–4,

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{0}}\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(\widehat{s},{\bm{X}}_{0})-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{0}}\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s_{0},{\bm{X}}_{0})\leq({\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{0}}-\mathbb{P}_{n})\{\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(\widehat{s})-\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s_{0})\}+n^{-A_{2}},

because

\mathbb{P}_{n}\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(\widehat{s})\leq\mathbb{P}_{n}\ell_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s_{0})
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(\widehat{s})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s_{0})+C\Biggl[\sqrt{\frac{(D\vee\log n)^{2}\left(\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)\{{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(\widehat{s})+{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s_{0})\}r}{n}}+\frac{(D\vee\log n)^{2}\left(\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)r}{n}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\qquad+n^{-A_{2}}.

Using
\sqrt{ab}\leq a/(4C)+Cb
first with

a={\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(\widehat{s})
and then with

a={\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s_{0})
, we absorb the square-root term and obtain

{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(\widehat{s})\leq C{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s_{0})+C\frac{(D\vee\log n)^{2}\left(\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)r}{n}+n^{-A_{2}}.

Taking the infimum over
s_{0}\in{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}
gives the high-probability oracle bound.

Step 6: pass to expectation.

On the complement of the high-probability event, the clipped envelope and

Lemma

I.10
give

{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(\widehat{s})\leq 2\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|\widehat{s}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}+2\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{2}\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}.

Choosing
A_{1}
large makes this exceptional contribution
{\mathcal{O}}(n^{-A})
. We obtain

{\mathbb{E}}_{\{{\bm{x}}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}}{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(\widehat{s})\leq C\inf_{s\in{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}(s)+C\frac{(D\vee\log n)^{2}\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\log\bigl({\mathcal{N}}(n^{-{\mathcal{O}}(1)},{\mathcal{NN}}_{{\underline{t}}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\vee n\bigr)}{n}+n^{-A},

which is the claimed oracle inequality.
∎

G.2
Time-Grid and Integral Conventions

For the VP/OU schedules used in the rate theorem,
\sigma_{t}^{2}\asymp t
for

0<t\leq 1
and
\sigma_{t}\asymp 1
for
t\geq 1
on the truncated interval. The large-noise
grid is chosen so that

[t_{1},T]=\bigcup_{k=2}^{K}I_{k},\qquad I_{k}=[t_{k-1},t_{k}],\qquad t_{k}=2t_{k-1}\ \text{while }t_{k}\leq 1,\qquad K\leq C\log n,

(246)

where the dyadic portion is followed by
{\mathcal{O}}(\log n)
order-one slabs of uniformly bounded
length from time
1
to
T\asymp\log n
. On every
large-noise slab,

c\,\sigma_{t_{k-1}}\leq\sigma_{t}\leq C\,\sigma_{t_{k-1}},\qquad\int_{I_{k}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq C.

For the small-noise interval and

t_{0}\asymp n^{-2(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}
,
t_{1}\asymp n^{-2/(d+2\beta)}
,

\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq C\log(t_{1}/t_{0})\leq C\log n.

(247)

G.3
Score Estimation on Large-Noise Regime

We construct the deterministic comparison network inside the oracle proof below, so the
approximation loss, slab-local entropy scale, and stochastic term are checked for the same
large-noise class.

Proposition G.2

(Score estimation on large-noise regime)
.

Let
I_{k}=[t_{k-1},t_{k}]\subset[t_{1},T]
be a large-noise slab satisfying

Equation

246
.
Assume
1
,
2
and
3

and
D\vee p_{\min}^{-1}\leq n^{a_{0}}
. On the global grid used in the main theorem,

\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-1}\leq C\sigma_{t_{1}}^{-1}\lesssim n^{1/(d+2\beta)}
, so every
logarithmic range depending on
\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-1}
contributes only polylogarithmic
factors in
n
, which are hidden in
\tilde{{\mathcal{O}}}
after enlarging constants depending
only on
(\beta,d,a_{0})
.
Let
\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\rm lg}
be the clipped large-noise network class with the parameter
bounds displayed in
Equation

249
below and coordinatewise
clipping at
C\sigma_{t}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{1/2}
.
Recall
\mathfrak{r}_{n}
and
q_{\star}
from
Equation

3
, and define the
accuracy-limited tangent-cell radius

r_{\star,n}:=c_{\star}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{-C_{\star}}B_{0}^{-C_{\star}}p_{\min}^{C_{\star}}(D\vee\log n)^{-C_{\star}}\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{1/q_{\star}},

where
C_{\star}
is sufficiently large and
c_{\star}
sufficiently small, depending only
on
(\beta,d)
and the fixed numerical constants in

Theorem

E.5
.
In particular,

r_{\star,n}^{-d}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}(D\vee\log n)^{C}\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{-d/q_{\star}}.

Let
\widehat{s}_{k}^{\rm lg}
be an empirical risk minimizer over this class on
I_{k}
.
Then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\{{\bm{x}}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\int_{I_{k}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|\widehat{s}_{k}^{\rm lg}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}\left[n^{-2(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}+\frac{\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+r_{\star,n}^{-d}}{n}\right](D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}+n^{-A}.

(248)

Writing
L_{k},{\bm{W}}_{k},S_{k},B_{k}
for the depth, width vector, sparsity, and weight bound of
the learned large-noise class, its parameters satisfy

\displaystyle L_{k}

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

(249)

\displaystyle\log B_{k}

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}_{k}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}\left(\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+r_{\star,n}^{-d}\right)(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

\displaystyle S_{k}

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}\left(\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+r_{\star,n}^{-d}\right)(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

Proof.

Step 1: construct a comparison network in the slab-local class.

Apply
Theorem

E.5
on
I_{k}
, with the theorem’s local
time-window notation set to
{\underline{t}}=t_{k-1}
and
{\overline{t}}=t_{k}
.
Set

r_{k}:=c_{0}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}\wedge r_{\star,n},

where
c_{0}>0
is a sufficiently small numerical constant.
Use theorem parameters

\varepsilon_{\rm cell}=r_{k}^{\beta}
and

\varepsilon_{\rm sc}=n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}/2}/\sqrt{D}
.
The constant
c_{0}
is chosen so that

\sigma_{t}\geq c_{\rm lg}r_{k}
throughout
I_{k}
, using the slab comparability in

Equation

246
.
The polynomial bounds on
D
,
p_{\min}^{-1}
, and the automatic grid bound on

\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-1}
imply

1+R_{\rm lb}\leq(D\vee\log n)^{2(q_{\rm geom}+2)+C},\qquad H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb}\leq\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}(D\vee\log n)^{2(q_{\rm geom}+2)+C},\qquad\sqrt{D}\leq D\vee\log n.

By the displayed bound on
H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb}
and the definition of
r_{\star,n}
,
the phase-smallness part of
Equation

65
holds:

\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}r_{k}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb})^{1/2}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}r_{\star,n}(D\vee\log n)^{C}\leq c_{\rm ph,cell}.

The same choice of
C_{\star}
and
c_{\star}
gives the high-order geometry remainder bound

\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}r_{k}^{q_{\rm geom}-2}(H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb})^{C}\leq c\,\frac{n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}/2}}{\sqrt{D}(1+R_{\rm lb})}.

Here we used the polynomial bound on
R_{\rm lb}
and the fact that

r_{k}\leq r_{\star,n}
; this is the step that prevents the order-one-noise slabs from
violating the fixed-order Taylor remainder condition.
Bounded
n
are absorbed by increasing constants.
Thus the geometry condition in
Equation

65
is
satisfied, and
Equation

66
gives a network

s_{k,0}^{\rm lg}
such that, on the tube
A_{t}
defined in
Equation

7
,

\|s_{k,0}^{\rm lg}({\bm{x}},t)-s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\sigma_{t}^{-2}n^{-2(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

After adding coordinatewise clipping at

C\sigma_{t}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{1/2}
, and enlarging constants if needed,
we obtain
s_{k}^{\rm lg}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\rm lg}
with the same tube error and with

\sup_{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\|s_{k}^{\rm lg}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq C\sigma_{t}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{1/2}.

Indeed, on
A_{t}
,
Equation

64
and the preceding
polynomial assumptions on
D,p_{\min}^{-1}
, and
\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-1}
imply

\sigma_{t}\|s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq\sigma_{t}\|s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{1/2}.

Coordinatewise clipping at this radius is
1
-Lipschitz and fixes the target value
coordinatewise, so it cannot increase the tube approximation error.
Its size is bounded by
Equation

67
. The identity

(r_{k}^{\beta})^{-d/\beta}=r_{k}^{-d}\leq C\left(\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+r_{\star,n}^{-d}\right),

and the absorption of
H_{\rm lg}^{\rm lb}
and
R_{\rm lb}
into

(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}
shows that the resulting class may be chosen with
the parameter bounds displayed in

Equation

249
.

Step 2: integrate the comparison loss.

On
A_{t}^{c}
, apply
Lemma

C.3
with

\ell_{\rm off}=D\vee\log n
; the clipping bound for
s_{k}^{\rm lg}
is then within
the required coordinatewise envelope. Thus, for any prescribed fixed
A^{\prime}>0
, after
increasing
C_{\varrho}
,

\int_{A_{t}^{c}}\|s_{k}^{\rm lg}({\bm{x}},t)-s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bm{x}}){\mathrm{d}}{\bm{x}}\leq C\sigma_{t}^{-2}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}n^{-A^{\prime}}.

Taking
A^{\prime}
large enough and using the polynomial lower bound on
\sigma_{t}
, the
off-tube contribution is absorbed into the tube rate. Therefore

\int_{I_{k}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|s_{k}^{\rm lg}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-2(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

where we used
\int_{I_{k}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq C_{\rm grid}
. Hence

\inf_{s\in\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\rm lg}}\int_{I_{k}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-2(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

Step 3: evaluate the slab-local entropy.

Apply the clipped score-estimation oracle
Theorem

G.1
on the slab

I_{k}
.
By
Equation

249
, the large-noise class satisfies the entropy
evaluation
Equation

8
with

M_{k}:=\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+r_{\star,n}^{-d}.

Indeed,
L_{k}+\log B_{k}
is polylogarithmic up to the displayed polynomial prefactors, while

S_{k}+\|{\bm{W}}_{k}\|_{\infty}
is bounded by
M_{k}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}
times those
same fixed polynomial prefactors.
Therefore

\log\bigl({\mathcal{N}}(n^{-A},\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\rm lg},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\vee n\bigr)\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}\left(\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+r_{\star,n}^{-d}\right)(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

Substituting this covering bound into the oracle inequality, using

\int_{I_{k}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq C_{\rm grid}
, and absorbing the oracle envelope factor

(D\vee\log n)^{2}
into
\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}
, gives the stochastic contribution

C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}\frac{\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+r_{\star,n}^{-d}}{n}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

with all manifold and smoothness prefactors displayed.
Combining this with the comparison loss from Step 2 proves

Equation

248
.
∎

G.4
Score Estimation on Small-Noise Regime

We also fold the deterministic small-noise comparison loss into the oracle proof, so the
comparison function and the learned class are checked in the same place.

Theorem G.3

(Score estimation on small-noise regime)
.

Assume
1
,
2
and
3
and
D\vee p_{\min}^{-1}\leq n^{a_{0}}
.
Assume also the small-noise reach and denominator-resolution conditions

Equations

231
and
232
.
Let
\mathcal{F}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}
be the clipped small-noise network class with clipping
radius
C\sigma_{t}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{1/2}
and with the size bounds in

Equations

236
and
237
, and let

\widehat{s}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}
be an empirical risk minimizer over

\mathcal{F}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}
on
[t_{0},t_{1}]
.
Then

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}_{\{{\bm{x}}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|\widehat{s}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}

(250)

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-2\beta/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}

\displaystyle+C\frac{(D\vee\log n)^{C}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\log\bigl({\mathcal{N}}(n^{-A},\mathcal{F}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\vee n\bigr)}{n}+n^{-A}.

In particular, using
Equation

8
with
M_{n}=n^{d/(d+2\beta)}
,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}_{\{{\bm{x}}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|\widehat{s}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}

(251)

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-2\beta/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}+n^{-A}.

Proof.

The comparison network
s_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}
belongs to
\mathcal{F}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}
, after
the same coordinatewise clipping at radius
C\sigma_{t}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{1/2}
.
Let
A_{t}
be the tube defined in
Equation

7
.
On
A_{t}
,
Equation

234
gives the following bound after
inserting the harmless factors
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}\geq 1
:

\|s_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}({\bm{x}},t)-s^{*}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-2\beta/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}.

The single-slab integral bound
Equation

247
gives

\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq C\log n\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{C}
, so the
tube contribution is bounded by

C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-2\beta/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

On
A_{t}^{c}
, apply
Lemma

C.3
with

\ell_{\rm off}=D\vee\log n
; the clipping bound

Equation

235
satisfies the required envelope. After increasing

C_{\varrho}
, this gives
n^{-A}
.
Thus

\inf_{s\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-2\beta/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}+n^{-A}.

Applying the clipped oracle inequality
Theorem

G.1
to the class
\mathcal{F}_{\rm sm}^{\rm lb}
gives
Equation

250
.
The entropy bound follows from

Equations

236
,
237
and
8
;
it is bounded by

C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{d/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

with all manifold and smoothness prefactors displayed.
Since

\frac{n^{d/(d+2\beta)}}{n}=n^{-1+d/(d+2\beta)}=n^{-2\beta/(d+2\beta)}

and
\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{C}
, the stochastic term is absorbed into the right side of
Equation

251
.
∎

Appendix H
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
Distribution Estimation

This appendix section contains the formal
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
distribution-estimation results.
The main-paper summaries are stated in
Section

5
; the generic score-estimation tools are stated in
Section

4
, and the formal learned-score inputs are proved in
Appendix

G
.
All displayed
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
bounds below use the branchwise density-lower-bound learned-score
inputs and the off-tube localization bounds.

Whenever an appendix estimate invokes the regular VP schedule, it refers to the
standard VP/OU schedule facts that, for universal schedule constants independent
of
(n,D)
,

c_{\sigma}t\leq\sigma_{t}^{2}\leq C_{\sigma}t,\quad 1-m_{t}\leq C_{m}t\qquad(0\leq t\leq 1),\qquad m_{t}\leq C_{\rm mix}e^{-c_{\rm mix}t}\quad(t\geq 0).

(252)

In particular, on every early interval
[0,t_{1}]
used below,
m_{t}
is bounded
below by a positive schedule constant
\underline{m}
once
t_{1}\leq 1
.

The main statement is
Corollary

H.1
. Its proof is a bookkeeping
argument with four terms: early stopping, terminal Gaussian initialization, the
small-noise score error, and the dyadic sum of large-noise score errors. The only
distribution-level input is the slabwise perturbation lemma

Lemma

I.17
; all score-estimation inputs come from

Propositions

G.2
and
G.3
.

H.1
Controlled-regime checklist

This subsection expands the non-asymptotic regime conditions invoked in

Theorem

5.1
.
Let
H_{n}:=D\vee\log n
and let
q_{\rm geom}
be as in
Equation

1
. The conditions split into
the following visually distinct groups.

Condition group

Meaning

Schedule

The regular VP schedule convention equation
252
, so that

\sigma_{t}^{2}\asymp t
on
[0,1]
,
m_{t}\asymp 1
on the early-stopping regime, and

m_{T}
decays exponentially on the terminal regime

Geometry

P_{0}
is supported on a compact smooth
d
-manifold
{\mathcal{M}}\subset[0,1]^{D}
with reach

\kappa>0
, volume
S_{{\mathcal{M}}}
, and finite envelope
\mathcal{K}_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}

Density

p_{0}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}({\mathcal{M}},B_{0})
and
p_{0}\geq p_{\min}>0

Polynomial control

D\vee p_{\min}^{-1}\leq n^{a_{0}}
and the displayed geometry/density factors are bounded
uniformly in
n
, or by fixed powers of

in growing-ambient families

Time grid

t_{0}\asymp n^{-2(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}

Small-noise reach

3c_{\star}\underline{m}^{-1}\sigma_{t_{1}}\sqrt{H_{n}}\leq\eta_{\Pi}/2
, so the forward tube is
inside the region where nearest projection is unique

Denominator resolution

C_{\rm den,sm}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-1}n^{-\beta/(d+2\beta)}H_{n}^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}/2-1/2}\leq 1
, so the de-Gaussianized denominator can be

Estimator

Clipped ERM over the slab-dependent ReLU classes from

and

The small-noise reach and denominator-resolution conditions are sample-size conditions,
not additional smoothness assumptions. For fixed
D
, fixed positive reach, fixed

p_{\min}
, and fixed finite-order geometry, both hold for all sufficiently large
n
.
For growing
D
or deteriorating geometry, they state explicitly how much ambient growth,
small reach, or small density lower bound the proof can tolerate.

H.2
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
distribution estimation

We use the same slabwise partition throughout this subsection: after choosing

t_{0},t_{1},T
, write

[t_{0},T]=[t_{0},t_{1}]\cup\bigcup_{k=2}^{K}I_{k},\qquad I_{k}=[t_{k-1},t_{k}],

where the large-noise slabs satisfy
Equation

246
; in particular, this is
the dyadic grid
t_{k}=2t_{k-1}
while
t_{k}\leq 1
, followed by order-one slabs up to

T
.

Audit checkpoint: rate assembly.

Corollary

H.1
is where the two score-estimation regimes become one
distribution rate. The small-noise score risk is larger, but the interval length
contributes the factor
\sqrt{t_{1}}\asymp n^{-1/(d+2\beta)}
. The large-noise stochastic
terms are summed over the dyadic grid, where

\sum_{k}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}
is dominated by the first large-noise slab, while the
accuracy-cap contribution
\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{-d/q_{\star}}
, with
\mathfrak{r}_{n}
and

q_{\star}
from
Equation

3
, is carried explicitly through the
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}

perturbation sum. These checks are the only rate-assembly points after the branchwise learned-score bounds are available.

summarizes the early stopping, score-estimation, and terminal terms.

\mathfrak{r}_{n}
and

from

.

Corollary H.1

(
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}

.

1
,
2
and
3
,

D\vee p_{\min}^{-1}\leq n^{a_{0}}
, and the two small-noise conditions

Equations

231
and
232
.
Let
t_{0}\asymp n^{-2(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}
,
t_{1}\asymp n^{-2/(d+2\beta)}
,

T\asymp\log n
, and use
\mathfrak{r}_{n},q_{\star}
from
Equation

3
.
Let
\widehat{s}^{\rm lb}
be the switched clipped ERM obtained by using

Theorem

G.3
on
[t_{0},t_{1}]
and

Proposition

G.2
on the large-noise slabs
I_{k}
,

k=2,\dots,K
. Let
\widehat{P}_{t_{0}}^{\rm lb}
be the generated law driven by

\widehat{s}^{\rm lb}
. Then there is a constant
C<\infty
, independent of
(n,D)
,
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(P_{0},\widehat{P}_{t_{0}}^{\rm lb})

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{Dt_{0}}+C\sqrt{D}\,\exp(-c_{\rm mix}T)

(253)

\displaystyle+C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}\Bigl(\mathfrak{r}_{n}+n^{-1/2}+n^{-1/2}\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{-d/(2q_{\star})}\Bigr)(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

If
T=c_{T}\log n
with
c_{T}
large enough, depending only on the fixed constants of the
setup, the terminal term is absorbed by the third term in
equation
253
. Since
t_{0}\asymp\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{2}
and

\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}\geq 1/2
, the early-stopping term is also absorbed by that term.
If
d>2
and
q_{\star}\geq d(\beta+1)/(d-2)
, then

n^{-1/2}+n^{-1/2}\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{-d/(2q_{\star})}\leq C\mathfrak{r}_{n}
, and hence

{\mathbb{E}}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(P_{0},\widehat{P}_{t_{0}}^{\rm lb})\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}\mathfrak{r}_{n}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

Writing
L,{\bm{W}},S,B
for the depth, width vector, sparsity, and weight bound of the
switched learned score, the assembled class can be chosen so that

L\vee\log B\leq C\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\vee S\leq C\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{d/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

where

\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}:=(1\vee S_{{\mathcal{M}}})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}(1\vee\kappa^{-1})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}\mathcal{K}_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}.

One may take

\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}=C+\max\left\{8(q_{\rm geom}+2),\left\lceil\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1+\frac{\lfloor\beta\rfloor\,d}{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}+\frac{d}{\beta\vee 1}+\frac{d}{2}+6\right\rceil\right\}.

The universal
C
in this exponent is independent of

\beta,d,D,n,p_{\min}
, and the numerical geometry constants; under the above

d>2
finite-order choice,
\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}={\mathcal{O}}_{\beta}(d)
.

Proof.

Step 1: decompose the distribution error.

Use the interpolation laws from
Lemma

I.17
on the partition

[t_{0},T]=[t_{0},t_{1}]\cup\bigcup_{k=2}^{K}I_{k}
. Define

{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{\rm sm,lb}:={\mathsf{W}}_{1}(\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(0)},\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(1)})
for the
single small-noise replacement and

{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{(k),{\rm lg,lb}}:={\mathsf{W}}_{1}(\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(k-1)},\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(k)})
for the large-noise slab

I_{k}
. With this notation,

\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(K)}
is the exact-terminal reverse law driven by the switched
learned score
\widehat{s}^{\rm lb}
.
By the triangle inequality along the reverse-time telescoping construction,

{\mathbb{E}}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(P_{0},\widehat{P}_{t_{0}}^{\rm lb})\leq{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(P_{0},P_{t_{0}})+{\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(\overline{P}_{t_{0}},\widehat{P}_{t_{0}})+{\mathbb{E}}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{\rm sm,lb}+\sum_{k:\,I_{k}\subset[t_{1},T]}{\mathbb{E}}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{(k),{\rm lg,lb}}.

The early-stopping and initialization bounds follow from

Lemmas

I.15
and
I.16
:

{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(P_{0},P_{t_{0}})\leq C\sqrt{Dt_{0}},\qquad{\mathbb{E}}{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(\overline{P}_{t_{0}},\widehat{P}_{t_{0}})\leq C\sqrt{D}\,\exp(-c_{\rm mix}T).

Step 2: bound the small-noise contribution.

By the clipping radii in

Theorems

G.3
and
G.2
,
and by the bounded ReLU time switches used to assemble the branch estimators,

\sup_{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\|\widehat{s}^{\rm lb}({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq C\sigma_{t}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{1/2},\qquad t\in[t_{0},T].

This verifies the clipped-score hypothesis of
Lemma

I.17
with

a_{\rm clip}=1/2
.
For the small-noise slab, apply
Lemma

I.17
.
Together with
Equation

251
,
t_{1}\asymp n^{-2/(d+2\beta)}
, and choosing the negligible exponent
A
large, this gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{\rm sm,lb}

\displaystyle\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{a_{\rm W}}n^{-1/(d+2\beta)}\sqrt{\log n}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|\widehat{s}^{\rm lb}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{*}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}(D\vee\log n)^{a_{\rm W}}n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

Here we used

n^{-1/(d+2\beta)}\,n^{-\beta/(d+2\beta)}=n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}.

Step 3: bound the large-noise contributions.

On the large-noise slabs,
Lemma

I.17
and

Equation

248
, again with the negligible
exponent chosen large, give

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{(k),{\rm lg,lb}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}(D\vee\log n)^{a_{\rm W}}\sqrt{t_{k}\log n}\,\Bigl[n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+n^{-1/2}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d/2}+n^{-1/2}\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{-d/(2q_{\star})}\Bigr](D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}(D\vee\log n)^{a_{\rm W}}n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}

for each large-noise slab
I_{k}
.
The first term sums to
n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}
up to the displayed polynomial ambient factor because

t_{k}\leq T\lesssim\log n
and
K\lesssim\log n
.
For the stochastic slab-size term, the dyadic grid
t_{k}=2t_{k-1}
while
t_{k}\leq 1
,
the relation
\sigma_{t}^{2}\asymp t
near zero, and

\sigma_{t_{1}}\asymp n^{-1/(d+2\beta)}
give

n^{-1/2}\sum_{k:\,t_{k}\leq 1}t_{k}^{1/2}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d/2}\leq Cn^{-1/2}\bigl(1+\sigma_{t_{1}}^{1-d/2}\bigr)(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}\leq C\left(n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}+n^{-1/2}\right)(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

The remaining order-one-time slabs have
\sigma_{t_{k-1}}\asymp 1
, and contribute only

n^{-1/2}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}
.
The accuracy-cap term is independent of the slab index except for logarithmic factors;
since
K\lesssim\log n
and
t_{k}\leq T\lesssim\log n
, its large-noise contribution is
bounded by

Cn^{-1/2}\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{-d/(2q_{\star})}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

Hence

\sum_{k:\,I_{k}\subset[t_{1},T]}{\mathbb{E}}\,{\mathsf{W}}_{1}^{(k),{\rm lg,lb}}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}\left(n^{-(\beta+1)/(d+2\beta)}+n^{-1/2}+n^{-1/2}\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{-d/(2q_{\star})}\right)(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

Combining these terms proves
Equation

253
.

Step 4: record the network sizes.

The small-noise learned class from
Theorem

G.3
has
separate bounds

L_{\rm sm}\vee\log B_{\rm sm}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

and

\|{\bm{W}}_{\rm sm}\|_{\infty}\vee S_{\rm sm}\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{d/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

For each large-noise slab,
Equation

249
gives

\displaystyle L_{k}\vee\log B_{k}

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}_{k}\|_{\infty}\vee S_{k}

\displaystyle\leq C\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}\left(\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}+\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{-d/q_{\star}}\right)(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}}.

There are
K\lesssim\log n
large-noise slabs, and the ReLU time switches add only

{\mathcal{O}}(K)
depth/sparsity and a logarithmic number of weights.
The dyadic grid gives

\sum_{k=2}^{K}\sigma_{t_{k-1}}^{-d}\leq C\sigma_{t_{1}}^{-d}\asymp n^{d/(d+2\beta)}.

The additional accuracy-cap contribution satisfies

K\mathfrak{r}_{n}^{-d/q_{\star}}\leq Cn^{d/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{C},

because
q_{\star}>\beta+1
by
Equation

1
.
The subnetworks are assembled in parallel behind those switches.
Thus the depth satisfies

L\leq\max\{L_{\rm sm},L_{2},\dots,L_{K}\}+CK\leq C(1\vee S_{{\mathcal{M}}})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}(1\vee\kappa^{-1})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}\mathcal{K}_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

which is the stated depth bound.
The width satisfies

\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\leq\|{\bm{W}}_{\rm sm}\|_{\infty}+\sum_{k=2}^{K}\|{\bm{W}}_{k}\|_{\infty}+CK\leq C(1\vee S_{{\mathcal{M}}})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}(1\vee\kappa^{-1})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}\mathcal{K}_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{d/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

after absorbing
K\lesssim\log n
; this proves the stated width bound.
The sparsity satisfies

S\leq S_{\rm sm}+\sum_{k=2}^{K}S_{k}+CK\leq C(1\vee S_{{\mathcal{M}}})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}(1\vee\kappa^{-1})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}\mathcal{K}_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}n^{d/(d+2\beta)}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

which is the stated sparsity bound.
Finally,

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle\leq\max\{\log B_{\rm sm},\log B_{2},\dots,\log B_{K}\}+C\log K

\displaystyle\leq C(1\vee S_{{\mathcal{M}}})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}(1\vee\kappa^{-1})^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}\mathcal{K}_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{\mathfrak{a}_{\rm geom}}B_{0}^{C}p_{\min}^{-C}(D\vee\log n)^{\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}},

which is the stated log-weight bound.
∎

Corollary H.2

(Fixed-geometry sufficient ambient-growth regime)
.

Fix
\beta>0
and
d\geq 1
. Work under the regular VP schedule convention equation
252
. Suppose
1
,
2
and
3
holds and that, for the fixed order
q_{\rm geom}
, the quantities

\underline{m}
,
\eta_{\Pi}
,
\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}
,
B_{0}
, and
p_{\min}
are bounded above or below by positive constants independent of
(D,n)
. Let

a_{\beta,d}:=\min\left\{\frac{2}{d+2\beta},\frac{2\beta}{(d+2\beta)(\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}-1)}\right\}.

Then there exists a constant
c>0
, depending only on the preceding fixed quantities, such that

D\vee\log n\leq c\,n^{a_{\beta,d}}

implies both

Equations

231
and
232
.
Consequently, under this fixed-geometry growth regime, the bound in

Corollary

H.1
applies.

Proof.

Write
H_{n}=D\vee\log n
. By equation
252
and

t_{1}\asymp n^{-2/(d+2\beta)}
, we have
\sigma_{t_{1}}\asymp n^{-1/(d+2\beta)}
.
Hence the small-noise reach condition

Equation

231
follows whenever

H_{n}\leq c_{1}n^{2/(d+2\beta)}

for a constant
c_{1}
depending only on
\underline{m}
,
\eta_{\Pi}
, and the schedule
constants. Likewise, after absorbing the fixed quantities

p_{\min}^{-1}\Gamma_{{\mathcal{M}},q_{\rm geom}}^{C}B_{0}^{C}
into a constant, the denominator
condition
Equation

232
is implied by

H_{n}^{(\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}-1)/2}\leq c_{2}n^{\beta/(d+2\beta)},

equivalently,

H_{n}\leq c_{3}n^{2\beta/((d+2\beta)(\mathfrak{p}_{\beta,d}-1))}.

Taking

c:=\min\{c_{1},c_{3}\}
proves the claim.
∎

This appendix collects reusable tools that are not specific to the manifold geometry.
They can be treated as black boxes when reading the approximation and estimation proofs.
The most frequently used ingredients are:

,

and

,

,
