I.1
Neural network approximation toolkit

I.1.1
Imported neural network approximation lemmas

Theorem I.1

(ReLU approximation of Hölder functions, after
[
40
, Theorem 5]
)
.

For any function
f\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\beta}([0,1]^{D},B)
and any integers
m\geq 1
and
N\geq(\beta+1)^{D}\vee(B+1)e^{D}
, there exists a network

\widetilde{f}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,1)

with

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=8+(m+5)(1+\lceil\log_{2}(D\vee\beta)\rceil),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 6(D\vee\lceil\beta\rceil)N,

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\leq 141(D+\beta+1)^{D+3}N(m+6)

such that

\|\widetilde{f}-f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{D})}\leq(2B+1)(1+D^{2}+\beta^{2})6^{D}N2^{-m}+B3^{\beta}N^{-\frac{\beta}{D}}.

Lemma I.2

(ReLU approximation of VP schedules, after Lemma B.1 of
[
34
]
)
.

Let
m_{t}
and
\sigma_{t}
be the schedules of the VP forward process on
[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
.
Let
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
.
Then, there exists a neural network
\phi_{m}(t)\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
that approximates
m_{t}
uniformly on
[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
, within the additive error of
\varepsilon
, where
L={\mathcal{O}}(\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}),\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}={\mathcal{O}}(\log\varepsilon^{-1}),S={\mathcal{O}}(\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1})
, and
B=\exp({\mathcal{O}}(\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}))
.

Also, there exists a neural network
\phi_{\sigma}(t)\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
that approximates
\sigma_{t}
uniformly on
[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
, within the additive error of
\varepsilon
, where
L\leq{\mathcal{O}}(\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}),\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}={\mathcal{O}}(\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}),S={\mathcal{O}}(\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1})
, and
B=\exp({\mathcal{O}}(\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}))
.

Lemma I.3

(ReLU multiplication network, after Lemma F.6 of
[
34
]
)
.

Let
d\geq 2,C\geq 1
, and
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
.
For any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a ReLU network
\phi_{\rm multi}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
with

\displaystyle L\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log d(\log\varepsilon^{-1}+d\log C),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}={}

\displaystyle 48d,

\displaystyle S\lesssim{}

\displaystyle d\log\varepsilon^{-1}+d\log C,

\displaystyle B={}

\displaystyle C^{d},

such that

\Bigl|\phi_{\rm multi}(x_{1}^{\prime},\dots,x_{d}^{\prime})-\prod_{k=1}^{d}x_{k}\Bigr|\leq\varepsilon+dC^{d-1}\varepsilon^{\prime},\quad\text{for all }{\bm{x}}\in[-C,C]^{d}\text{ and }{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\text{ with }\|{\bm{x}}-{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}.

Moreover,
|\phi_{\rm multi}({\bm{x}})|\leq C^{d}
for all
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, and
\phi_{\rm multi}(x_{1}^{\prime},\dots,x_{d}^{\prime})=0
if at least one of
x_{k}^{\prime}=0
.
We note that some of
x_{i},x_{j}
(
i\neq j
) can be shared.
For the product
\prod_{k=1}^{I}x_{k}^{\alpha_{k}}
with
{\bm{\alpha}}=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{I})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{I}
and
\sum_{k=1}^{I}\alpha_{k}=d
, there exists a neural network satisfying the same bounds as above, denoted by
\phi_{\rm multi}({\bm{x}};{\bm{\alpha}})
.

Lemma I.4

(ReLU reciprocal network, after Lemma F.7 of
[
34
]
)
.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists
\phi_{\rm rec}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
with

L\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1},\quad\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1},\quad S\lesssim\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1},\quad\log B\lesssim\log\varepsilon^{-1},

such that

\Bigl|\phi_{\rm rec}(x^{\prime})-\frac{1}{x}\Bigr|\leq\varepsilon+\frac{|x^{\prime}-x|}{\varepsilon},\quad\text{for all }x\in[\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{-1}]\text{ and }x^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}.

Lemma I.5

(Pointwise ReLU approximation of
e^{-x}
, after Lemma F.12 of
[
34
]
)
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists ReLU network
\phi_{\exp}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
such that

\bigl|e^{-x^{\prime}}-\phi_{\exp}(x)\bigr|\leq\varepsilon+|x-x^{\prime}|\qquad\text{for all }x,x^{\prime}\geq 0

holds, where

\displaystyle L\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1},\quad\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log\varepsilon^{-1},\quad S\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1},\quad\log B\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}.

Moreover, for all
x\geq\log(3/\varepsilon)
it holds that
|\phi_{\exp}(x)|\leq\varepsilon
.

I.1.2
Auxiliary schedule and arithmetic approximation lemmas

All schedule-dependent lemmas in this subsection are consequences of
Lemma

I.2
and the algebraic network lemmas.

Lemma I.6

(ReLU approximation of the inverse VP mean
1/m_{t}
)
.

Let
\underline{m}:=\inf_{t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]}m_{t}>0
.
Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2]
, there exist ReLU networks
\widetilde{\phi}_{1/m}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
with

L,\,\log B\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{2}\underline{m}^{-1},\quad\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{3}\underline{m}^{-1},\quad S\lesssim\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{4}\underline{m}^{-1}

such that

\|\widetilde{\phi}_{1/m}(t)-m_{t}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}([{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}])}\leq\varepsilon.

Proof.

Choose an auxiliary accuracy

\varepsilon_{m}:=\frac{\underline{m}\varepsilon^{2}}{4}.

By
Lemma

I.2
for
m_{t}
, there exists a ReLU network
\widetilde{\phi}_{m}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L_{1},{\bm{W}}_{1},S_{1},B_{1})
with

L_{1}={\mathcal{O}}(\log^{2}\varepsilon_{m}^{-1}),\quad\|{\bm{W}}_{1}\|_{\infty}={\mathcal{O}}(\log\varepsilon_{m}^{-1}),\quad S_{1}={\mathcal{O}}(\log^{2}\varepsilon_{m}^{-1}),\quad\log B_{1}={\mathcal{O}}(\log^{2}\varepsilon_{m}^{-1})

such that

\|\widetilde{\phi}_{m}-m_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}([{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}])}\leq\varepsilon_{m}.

Now apply the reciprocal lemma (
Lemma

I.4
) with parameter

\varepsilon_{\rm rec}:=\frac{\underline{m}\varepsilon}{2}.

Since
m_{t}\in[\underline{m},1]\subset[\varepsilon_{\rm rec},\varepsilon_{\rm rec}^{-1}]
, the reciprocal network
\phi_{\rm rec}
satisfies

\Bigl|\phi_{\rm rec}(\widetilde{\phi}_{m}(t))-\frac{1}{m_{t}}\Bigr|\leq\varepsilon_{\rm rec}+\frac{|\widetilde{\phi}_{m}(t)-m_{t}|}{\varepsilon_{\rm rec}}\leq\frac{\underline{m}\varepsilon}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{m}}{\underline{m}\varepsilon/2}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}=\varepsilon.

Hence we may define

\widetilde{\phi}_{1/m}(t):=\phi_{\rm rec}(\widetilde{\phi}_{m}(t)).

and we have
\widetilde{\phi}_{1/m}(t)\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
with

L,\,\log B\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{2}\underline{m}^{-1},\quad\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{3}\underline{m}^{-1},\quad S\lesssim\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{4}\underline{m}^{-1}.

∎

Lemma I.7

(ReLU approximation of the rescaled input
{\bm{x}}/m_{t}
)
.

Let
M_{x}\geq 1
and assume
\|{\bm{x}}\|_{\infty}\leq M_{x}
.
For every
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2]
there exists a vector-valued ReLU network

\widetilde{\phi}({\bm{x}},t)=\bigl(\widetilde{\phi}_{1}({\bm{x}},t),\dots,\widetilde{\phi}_{D}({\bm{x}},t)\bigr)\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)

with

\displaystyle L,\,\log B\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{2}(M_{x}\vee\underline{m}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle D\bigl(\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{3}(M_{x}\vee\underline{m}^{-1})\bigr),

\displaystyle S\lesssim{}

\displaystyle D\bigl(\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{4}(M_{x}\vee\underline{m}^{-1})\bigr),

such that

\Bigl\|\widetilde{\phi}({\bm{x}},t)-\frac{{\bm{x}}}{m_{t}}\Bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\{\|{\bm{x}}\|_{\infty}\leq M_{x}\}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}])}\leq\varepsilon.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{\phi}_{1/m}
be the network from
Lemma

I.6
.
Choose an auxiliary accuracy

\varepsilon_{0}:=\frac{\varepsilon}{4(1\vee M_{x}\vee\underline{m}^{-1})}.

Refine
\widetilde{\phi}_{1/m}
so that

\|\widetilde{\phi}_{1/m}-m_{t}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}([{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}])}\leq\varepsilon_{0}.

For each coordinate
j=1,\dots,D
, apply the multiplication lemma (
Lemma

I.3
) on
[-M_{x},M_{x}]\times[-\underline{m}^{-1},\underline{m}^{-1}]
to obtain a ReLU network
\phi_{{\rm mult},j}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L_{1},{\bm{W}}_{1},S_{1},B_{1})
satisfying

\displaystyle L_{1}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log\varepsilon_{0}^{-1}+\log(M_{x}\vee\underline{m}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}_{1}\|_{\infty}={}

\displaystyle 96,

\displaystyle S_{1}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log\varepsilon_{0}^{-1}+\log(M_{x}\vee\underline{m}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\log B_{1}={}

\displaystyle 2\log(M_{x}\vee\underline{m}^{-1}),

such that

\Bigl|\phi_{{\rm mult},j}\bigl(x_{j},\widetilde{\phi}_{1/m}(t)\bigr)-x_{j}m_{t}^{-1}\Bigr|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}+2(1\vee M_{x}\vee\underline{m}^{-1})\,\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon.

Define

\widetilde{\phi}_{j}({\bm{x}},t):=\phi_{{\rm mult},j}\bigl(x_{j},\widetilde{\phi}_{1/m}(t)\bigr),\qquad j=1,\dots,D.

Parallel stacking of the
D
coordinate subnetworks gives the stated vector-valued approximant.
∎

Lemma I.8

(ReLU approximation of positive powers of
\sigma_{t}
)
.

For any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
and any
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2]
such that
\varepsilon\leq{\underline{t}}<{\overline{t}}<\infty
, there exists a ReLU network
\phi_{\sigma^{k}}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
with

\displaystyle L\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{2}k,\quad\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}+k,

\displaystyle S\lesssim{}\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{4}k+k\log\varepsilon^{-1},\quad\log B\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{2}k,

such that

\displaystyle\bigl\|\phi_{\sigma^{k}}(t)-\sigma_{t}^{k}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}([{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}])}\leq{}

\displaystyle 3\varepsilon.

Proof.

By
Lemma

I.2
, applied with accuracy parameter
\varepsilon/k
, there exists a neural network
\phi_{\sigma}(t)\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L_{\sigma},{\bm{W}}_{\sigma},S_{\sigma},B_{\sigma})
with

\displaystyle L\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{2}k,\quad\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{3}k,

(254)

\displaystyle S\lesssim\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{4}k,\quad\log B\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{2}k,

such that

|\phi_{\sigma}(t)-\sigma_{t}|\leq\varepsilon/k,\quad\text{for all }t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}].

Notice that
\sigma_{t}\in[0,1]
for all
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
.
If
k=1
, take
\phi_{\sigma^{k}}:=\phi_{\sigma}
, and the claim follows.
Assume now that
k\geq 2
.
Apply
Lemma

I.3
with
d=k
,
C=1
, and with all
k
true inputs equal to
\sigma_{t}
and all
k
perturbed inputs equal to
\phi_{\sigma}(t)
.
The shared-input form is allowed by the final sentence of
Lemma

I.3
.
Thus there exists a ReLU network
\phi_{\rm multi}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L_{1},{\bm{W}}_{1},S_{1},B_{1})
with

L_{1}\lesssim\log k\,\log\varepsilon^{-1},\quad\|{\bm{W}}_{1}\|_{\infty}=48k,\quad S_{1}\lesssim k\log\varepsilon^{-1},\quad B_{1}=1

(255)

such that

\Bigl|\phi_{\rm multi}\bigl(\underbrace{\phi_{\sigma}(t),\dots,\phi_{\sigma}(t)}_{k\ {\rm copies}}\bigr)-\sigma_{t}^{k}\Bigr|\leq\varepsilon+k\frac{\varepsilon}{k}\leq 2\varepsilon.

(256)

For all
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
, we define

\phi_{\sigma^{k}}(t)\coloneqq\phi_{\rm multi}\bigl(\underbrace{\phi_{\sigma}(t),\dots,\phi_{\sigma}(t)}_{k\ {\rm copies}}\bigr).

Then by the configurations given in
Equations

254
and
255
and the bound in
Equation

256
, we obtain that there exists a ReLU network
\phi_{\sigma^{k}}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
with

\displaystyle L\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{2}k,\quad\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}+k,

\displaystyle S\lesssim{}\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1}+\log^{4}k+k\log\varepsilon^{-1},\quad\log B\lesssim\log\varepsilon^{-1}+\log k,

such that

\displaystyle\bigl\|\phi_{\sigma^{k}}(t)-\sigma_{t}^{k}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}([{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}])}\leq{}

\displaystyle 3\varepsilon.

∎

Lemma I.9

(ReLU approximation of inverse powers of
\sigma_{t}
)
.

For any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
and any
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, there exists a ReLU network
\phi_{\sigma^{-k}}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
with

\displaystyle L\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+k^{2}\log^{2}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2},

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}+k^{3}\log^{3}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2},

\displaystyle S\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1}+k^{4}\log^{4}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2}+k\log(e/\varepsilon)+k^{2}\log(e\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2}),

\displaystyle\log B\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+k\log^{2}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2},

such that

\displaystyle\bigl\|\phi_{\sigma^{-k}}(t)-\sigma_{t}^{-k}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}([{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}])}\leq{}

\displaystyle 4\varepsilon.

Proof.

Set

\varepsilon_{\sigma,k}:=\min\Bigl\{\frac{\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{k}\varepsilon^{2}}{12},\frac{{\underline{t}}}{2}\Bigr\}.

Apply
Lemma

I.8
with accuracy parameter
\varepsilon_{\sigma,k}
.
By construction
\varepsilon_{\sigma,k}\leq{\underline{t}}
, so this parameter lies in the admissible range of
Lemma

I.8
.
Moreover,

\log(\varepsilon_{\sigma,k}^{-1})\leq C\bigl(\log\varepsilon^{-1}+k\log\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-1}+1\bigr),

where the fixed compact time interval contributes only to the constant through
\log{\underline{t}}^{-1}
.
Then, there exists a ReLU network
\phi_{\sigma^{k}}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L_{1},{\bm{W}}_{1},S_{1},B_{1})
with

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+k^{2}\log^{2}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2}+\log^{2}k,

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}_{1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}+k^{3}\log^{3}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2}+k,

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1}+k^{4}\log^{4}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2}+\log^{4}k+k\log(e/\varepsilon)+k^{2}\log(e\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2}),

\displaystyle\log B_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+k^{2}\log^{2}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2}+\log^{2}k,

such that

\displaystyle\bigl\|\phi_{\sigma^{k}}(t)-\sigma_{t}^{k}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}([{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}])}\leq{}

\displaystyle 3\varepsilon_{\sigma,k}\leq\frac{\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{k}\varepsilon^{2}}{4}.

Note that for any
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
, we have
\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{k}\lesssim\sigma_{t}^{k}\leq 1
.

Let
\varepsilon_{\rm rec}:=\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{k}\varepsilon/2
and let
\phi_{\rm rec}
be the network in
Lemma

I.4
with parameter
\varepsilon_{\rm rec}
.
Since
\varepsilon_{\rm rec}\leq\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{k}\leq\sigma_{t}^{k}
and
\sigma_{t}^{k}\leq 1\leq\varepsilon_{\rm rec}^{-1}
, the domain condition in
Lemma

I.4
holds for every
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
.
Then, we have
\phi_{\rm rec}\in{\mathcal{NN}}(L_{\rm rec},{\bm{W}}_{\rm rec},S_{\rm rec},B_{\rm rec})
with

\displaystyle L_{\rm rec}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log^{2}\varepsilon^{-1}+k^{2}\log^{2}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2},

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}}_{\rm rec}\|_{\infty}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log^{3}\varepsilon^{-1}+k^{3}\log^{3}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2},

\displaystyle S_{\rm rec}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log^{4}\varepsilon^{-1}+k^{4}\log^{4}\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2},

\displaystyle\log B_{\rm rec}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\log\varepsilon^{-1}+k\log\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{-2},

such that for all
t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]
, it holds that

\Bigl|\phi_{\rm rec}(\phi_{\sigma^{k}}(t))-\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{k}}\Bigr|\leq\varepsilon_{\rm rec}+\frac{|\phi_{\sigma^{k}}(t)-\sigma_{t}^{k}|}{\varepsilon_{\rm rec}}\leq\frac{\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{k}\varepsilon}{2}+\frac{\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{k}\varepsilon^{2}/4}{\sigma_{{\underline{t}}}^{k}\varepsilon/2}\leq\varepsilon.

Let
\phi_{\sigma^{-k}}(t)\coloneqq\phi_{\rm rec}(\phi_{\sigma^{k}}(t))
.
Adding the displayed size bounds for
\phi_{\sigma^{k}}
and
\phi_{\rm rec}
, and absorbing the lower-order
\log k
terms into the stated
k
-dependent bounds, gives the claimed network size.
∎

I.2
Support lemmas for generalization analysis

Lemma I.10

(Unconditional Fisher bound for the smoothed data law)
.

For the VP process
Equation

4
, for each
t>0
,

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\bigl[\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bm{X}}_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]\leq D\sigma_{t}^{-2}.

Proof.

Write
{\bm{X}}_{t}=m_{t}{\bm{X}}_{0}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{Z}}
, where

{\bm{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{I}}_{D})
is independent of
{\bm{X}}_{0}
. Tweedie’s formula gives

\nabla\log p_{t}({\bm{y}})={\mathbb{E}}\left[-\frac{{\bm{Z}}}{\sigma_{t}}\,\middle|\,{\bm{X}}_{t}={\bm{y}}\right].

Therefore Jensen’s inequality and the tower property imply

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}}\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bm{X}}_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\frac{{\bm{Z}}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=D\sigma_{t}^{-2}.

This is an unconditional Fisher-information bound under the marginal law of

{\bm{X}}_{t}
; no conditional bound given
{\bm{X}}_{0}
is used.
∎

Lemma I.11

(A variant lemma of
[
15
, Lemma 25]
)
.

Fix any
0<{\underline{t}}<{\overline{t}}<\infty
.
Let
s:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
and
s^{\prime}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
be any Borel functions. Then, for all
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\bigl(\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s,{\bm{x}})-\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s^{\prime},{\bm{x}})\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\Bigl(\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\bigl[\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}+\|s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}+4D\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\Bigr)\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s,{\bm{x}})-\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s^{\prime},{\bm{x}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\Bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+\frac{{\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}-\Bigl\|s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+\frac{{\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}+2\Bigl\langle s({\bm{X}}_{t},t),\frac{{\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\rangle-\|s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}-2\Bigl\langle s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t),\frac{{\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\rangle\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\bigl(s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\bigr)^{\top}\bigl(s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\bigr)+\bigl(s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\bigr)^{\top}\frac{2({\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\bigl(s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\bigr)^{\top}\Bigl(s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+\frac{2({\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}.

Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Young’s inequality, we obtain that

\displaystyle|\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s,{\bm{x}})-\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s^{\prime},{\bm{x}})|

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\bigr\|\cdot\Bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+\frac{2({\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\|\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}

(by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}}\cdot\sqrt{\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\Bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+\frac{2({\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}}.

(by Young’s inequality)

For the VP process,
{\bm{X}}_{t}=m_{t}{\bm{x}}+\sigma_{t}{\bm{Z}}
under

{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}
, with
{\bm{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,{\bm{I}}_{D})
. Hence

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\Bigl\|\frac{{\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]=\frac{D}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\Bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+\frac{2({\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sqrt{3\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\bigl[\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}+\|s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]+4{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\Bigl\|\frac{{\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]\Bigr){\mathrm{d}t}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sqrt{3\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\bigl[\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}+\|s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]+4D\sigma_{t}^{-2}\Bigr){\mathrm{d}t}}.

(257)

Hence we obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\Bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+\frac{2({\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sqrt{3\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\bigl[\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}+\|s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}+12D\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}}.

(258)

Thus, again by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\bigl(\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s,{\bm{x}})-\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s^{\prime},{\bm{x}})\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}\cdot\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\Bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)+\frac{2({\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}})}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle 3\Bigl(\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\bigl[\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}+\|s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}+4D\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}\Bigr)\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\Bigl[\bigl\|s({\bm{X}}_{t},t)-s^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{t},t)\bigr\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}.

∎

Lemma I.12

(Uniform boundedness of clipped denoising losses)
.

Fix
0<{\underline{t}}<{\overline{t}}<\infty
. Let
s:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}]\to{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
be Borel and assume that

\sup_{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\|s({\bm{x}},t)\|_{\infty}\leq C_{\rm clip}\sigma_{t}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{1/2},\qquad t\in[{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}].

Then

\sup_{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}|\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s,{\bm{x}})|\leq C(1+C_{\rm clip}^{2})(D\vee\log n)^{2}\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}.

Consequently, if
s
and
s^{\prime}
both satisfy the same clipped envelope, then

\sup_{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}|\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s,{\bm{x}})-\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s^{\prime},{\bm{x}})|\leq C(1+C_{\rm clip}^{2})(D\vee\log n)^{2}\int_{{\underline{t}}}^{{\overline{t}}}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathrm{d}t}.

Proof.

For the VP process,

{\bm{X}}_{t}\mid{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(m_{t}{\bm{x}},\sigma_{t}^{2}{\bm{I}}_{D})
, and

\nabla\log p_{t}({\bm{X}}_{t}\mid{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}})=-\frac{{\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

The clipped envelope gives

\|s({\bm{y}},t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq DC_{\rm clip}^{2}\sigma_{t}^{-2}(D\vee\log n)\leq CC_{\rm clip}^{2}(D\vee\log n)^{2}\sigma_{t}^{-2}.

Also

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{X}}_{t}|{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{x}}}\left\|\frac{{\bm{X}}_{t}-m_{t}{\bm{x}}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=D\sigma_{t}^{-2}\leq(D\vee\log n)^{2}\sigma_{t}^{-2}.

Using
\|a+b\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|a\|_{2}^{2}+2\|b\|_{2}^{2}
inside the definition of

\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s,{\bm{x}})
and integrating over
t
proves the first display. The second follows
from the triangle inequality applied to
\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s,{\bm{x}})-\ell_{{\underline{t}},{\overline{t}}}(s^{\prime},{\bm{x}})
.
∎

I.2.1
Bernstein’s inequality

Theorem I.13

(Bernstein’s inequality for bounded distributions
[
48
]
)
.

Let
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
be independent random variables such that
|X_{i}|\leq K
for all
i\in[n]
.
Then, for every
t\geq 0
, we have

\Pr\Bigl[\Bigl|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}[X_{i}])\Bigr|\geq t\Bigr]\leq 2\exp\Bigl(-\frac{t^{2}/2}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}[X_{i}^{2}]+Kt/3}\Bigr).

In other words, with probability at least
1-\delta
, it holds that

\Bigl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}[X_{i}])\Bigr|\leq\frac{t}{n}\leq\frac{2K\log(2/\delta)}{3n}+\sqrt{\frac{\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}[X_{i}^{2}]\log(2/\delta)}{n}}.

I.2.2
Covering number bounds

Lemma I.14

(
[
43
, Lemma 3]
)
.

For any input dimension
D_{\rm in}
and any
\tau>0
, the covering number of
{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B)
on
[0,1]^{D_{\rm in}}
can be bounded by

\log{\mathcal{N}}(\tau,{\mathcal{NN}}(L,{\bm{W}},S,B),\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{D_{\rm in}})})\lesssim SL\log\bigl(\tau^{-1}L(\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\infty}+1)(B\vee 1)\bigr).

In the score-estimation oracle
D_{\rm in}=D+1
, and the difference between

D_{\rm in}
and
D
is absorbed into the displayed polynomial ambient factors.

I.3
Auxiliary lemmas

We use the following distribution-estimation lemmas from
[
34
]
as black boxes.
For the slabwise lemma we use the standard clipped-score form with coordinatewise clipping
at a fixed polynomial scale;
Appendix

G
supplies the integrated score-error,
clipping, and network-size inputs for the learned scores.
The final
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
corollary is therefore unconditional as a perturbation theorem
given those score inputs.

Lemma I.15

(Early stopping for the VP forward law)
.

For the forward law
P_{t}
and
P_{0}
supported on
[0,1]^{D}
,

{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(P_{0},P_{t_{0}})\leq\sqrt{D}\,\{1-m_{t_{0}}+\sigma_{t_{0}}\}.

(259)

Consequently, under the regular VP schedule convention

Equation

252
,
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(P_{0},P_{t_{0}})\leq C\sqrt{Dt_{0}}
for

t_{0}\leq 1
.

Proof.

Couple
{\bm{X}}_{0}\sim P_{0}
with
{\bm{X}}_{t_{0}}=m_{t_{0}}{\bm{X}}_{0}+\sigma_{t_{0}}{\bm{Z}}
using an
independent
{\bm{Z}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{D})
. Since
{\mathcal{M}}\subset[0,1]^{D}
,

\|{\bm{X}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{D}
almost surely and
{\mathbb{E}}\|{\bm{Z}}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{D}
. Therefore

{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(P_{0},P_{t_{0}})\leq{\mathbb{E}}\|{\bm{X}}_{t_{0}}-{\bm{X}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq(1-m_{t_{0}})\sqrt{D}+\sigma_{t_{0}}\sqrt{D}.

The final display follows from

Equation

252
.
∎

Lemma I.16

(Terminal Gaussian initialization with ambient factor)
.

Let
\overline{P}_{t_{0}}
be the reverse-process output law initialized from the exact forward terminal law
P_{T}
, and let
\widehat{P}_{t_{0}}
be the corresponding output law initialized from the Gaussian proxy
\widehat{P}_{T}
.
Under the regular VP schedule convention
Equation

252
,

{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(\overline{P}_{t_{0}},\widehat{P}_{t_{0}})\bigr]\leq C{\mathsf{TV}}(P_{T},\widehat{P}_{T})\leq C\sqrt{D}\,\exp(-c_{\rm mix}T).

(260)

Proof.

The first inequality is the terminal perturbation bound from

[
34
, Lemma D.6]
. For the second, condition on

{\bm{X}}_{0}={\bm{y}}
. The terminal conditional law is

{\mathcal{N}}(m_{T}{\bm{y}},\sigma_{T}^{2}I_{D})
with
\sigma_{T}^{2}=1-m_{T}^{2}
, while

\widehat{P}_{T}={\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{D})
. When
m_{T}\leq 1/2
, the Gaussian KL divergence is
bounded by
CDm_{T}^{2}
because
\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{D}
and

\sigma_{T}^{2}=1-m_{T}^{2}
. Pinsker’s inequality gives a

C\sqrt{D}\,m_{T}
bound for each conditional law. When
m_{T}>1/2
, the same bound
follows after increasing
C
from the trivial estimate
{\mathsf{TV}}\leq 1
. Convexity over
the mixture gives
{\mathsf{TV}}(P_{T},\widehat{P}_{T})\leq C\sqrt{D}\,m_{T}
. The regular VP terminal decay

m_{T}\leq C_{\rm mix}e^{-c_{\rm mix}T}
proves the claim.
∎

Lemma I.17

(Slabwise
{\mathsf{W}}_{1}
perturbation, after
[
34
, Lemma D.7]
)
.

Fix a partition
[t_{0},T]=\bigcup_{k=1}^{K_{*}}[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
. Let

\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(k)}
denote the reverse-process output law initialized from the
exact terminal law
P_{T}
, using the learned score on the first
k
reverse-time
slabs and the true score on the remaining slabs. Thus

\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(0)}=\overline{P}_{t_{0}}
is the exact-score reverse law and

\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(K_{*})}
is the learned-score reverse law before replacing

P_{T}
by the Gaussian proxy. The increment

{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(k-1)},\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(k)})
is therefore precisely
the perturbation caused by using the learned score on the single slab

[t_{k-1},t_{k}]
.
Suppose that, for a fixed exponent
a_{\rm clip}
, the score used by the reverse process satisfies

\|\widehat{s}(\cdot,t)\|_{\infty}\leq C_{\rm clip}\sigma_{t}^{-1}(D\vee\log n)^{a_{\rm clip}}.

Then, the following holds for all
k=1,2,\dots,K_{*}
:

{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(k-1)},\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(k)})\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{a_{\rm W}}\Biggl\{\sqrt{t_{k}\log n}\sqrt{\int_{t=t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{x}}_{t}}\|\widehat{s}({\bm{x}}_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t})\|_{2}^{2}{\mathrm{d}t}}+n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{d+2\beta}}\Biggr\}.

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\{{\bm{x}}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}}{\mathsf{W}}_{1}(\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(k-1)},\overline{P}_{t_{0}}^{(k)})

\displaystyle\leq C(D\vee\log n)^{a_{\rm W}}\Biggl\{\sqrt{t_{k}\log n}\Bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{\{{\bm{x}}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}}\int_{t=t_{k-1}}^{t_{k}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{x}}_{t}}\bigl[\|\widehat{s}({\bm{x}}_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}({\bm{x}}_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]{\mathrm{d}t}\Bigr)^{1/2}+n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{d+2\beta}}\Biggr\}.

The constants
C,a_{\rm W}
may depend on the fixed clipping exponent

a_{\rm clip}
, but not on
n,D,t_{k}
, or
p_{\min}
.
